Tests: **+10 integration** (the verbs precede every branch row and match §2A's exact list; `merge` finds an action; folder rows fold and drill-in scopes while `‹ Back` returns; **filtering collapses folders and reaches out-of-scope branches**; `folderOf` folds only below the current prefix; the submenu is the reference list with names interpolated, separators and F2's hint; **irrelevant items are hidden, not disabled**, checked across local / remote / tag / current; every applicable action is runnable for all three ref kinds; **a real merge into the current branch runs from the command with no drag-and-drop** and the merged commit is reachable from HEAD; a non-applicable action changes nothing). The P2 popup test was updated for the widened `buildPopupItems` input rather than left asserting the old shape. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 647 unit, 189 webview + 3 skipped, **208 integration passing + 2 pending** — the only red the pre-existing P7-T2 500 ms stash bound.

P13-T7 ✅ 2026-08-23 — **VCS Operations Popup** (gap analysis S6 — absent entirely before). `Alt+\`` (IntelliJ's binding, `Ctrl+V` on mac, gated on `gittree.keymap.intellij`; `gittree.operationsPopup` is always in the palette) opens a QuickPick of every action that applies **right now**. The item set is **enumerated, never hand-listed**: `dialogRegistry.ts` supplies the dialogs and their `applies(ctx)` predicates, so a dialog added later shows up here by construction — §1 of the gap analysis blames hand-maintained inventories for exactly the drift this phase group exists to fix. On top of the registry, three context-only groups: the **in-progress** trio (`Continue`/`Skip`/`Abort`, naming the actual operation — and a merge correctly has no "skip", only a rebase does), **diff** actions when a diff editor is focused (stage/unstage/revert hunk, next difference), and **commit** actions when the log has a selection. Groups render in a fixed order with a separator each, and the in-progress group always comes first — mid-rebase, continue/skip/abort is the only thing anyone wants. **Recently used actions float to the top** (per workspace, capped at 5, de-duplicated), except an in-progress action, which is already at the top and must never be demoted; a remembered command that no longer applies is silently dropped rather than offered. The whole ordering/filtering model is the vscode-free `operationsModel.ts`, so the context rules are unit-tested rather than inferred. Tests: **+20 unit** (the in-progress trio per operation kind incl. merge-has-no-skip; diff/commit items only with their context; **the four required contexts — clean tree, dirty tree, mid-rebase, focused diff editor — produce four genuinely different item sets**; no Push without a remote, no Update without an upstream, no Unstash without a stash, only Clone with no repository at all; group ordering; recents promoted exactly once and never for an in-progress action; `recordRecent`'s newest-first, de-duplicated, capped behaviour), **+5 integration** (the command is registered; a clean tree offers none of commit/stash/shelve/rollback while still offering repository-level actions; a dirty tree adds all four; **mid-merge the continue/abort pair is first and no rebase-only "skip" appears**; and **every command the popup offers is actually registered** — the one assertion that keeps "enumerated from the registry" honest). **Finding worth keeping:** the popup's no-argument path resolves `core.repos[0]`, which in a multi-root workspace is not necessarily the repo the caller means — the same class of bug as the P12-T2 canonical-key lesson. The debug seam now takes an explicit `repoRoot`, and the reason is written down in the registry's own type. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 667 unit, 189 webview + 3 skipped, **213 integration passing + 2 pending** — the only red the pre-existing P7-T2 500 ms stash bound.

P13-T8 ✅ 2026-08-23 — **Merge Revisions dialog parity** (gap analysis §3.5 + **S7**). The tri-editor stays — three real editors buy full LSP in the result pane (master §6) — and the gaps were in its toolbar. **`Apply non-conflicting changes` is now three actions**, `Left · All · Right`, where the magic wand only ever implemented "All": a new vscode-free `applyNonConflicting.ts` classifies each block by which side owns it (`theirs === base` → left, `ours === base` → right, identical → either, anything else → two-sided) and `All` runs the wand's full cascade including the rules a one-sided apply must never use. **A live counter** in IntelliJ's wording (`No changes. 1 conflict.` / `3 changes, 1 conflict.` / `All changes applied.`) rides in the status bar and is reported by every apply. **Per-side `Show Details`** names the revision behind each pane — `Yours — 1a2b3c4 · author · date · subject`, read from `HEAD`/`MERGE_HEAD` through the repo's own queue. **`Accept Yours`/`Accept Theirs`** are surfaced in the tri-editor toolbar as the explicit "take my side everywhere" verbs, distinct from the non-conflicting applies. **S7:** a new `Files Merged with Conflicts` dialog gates a conflicted operation — the file list with each conflict's kind, `Accept Yours`, `Accept Theirs`, `Resolve Simple Conflicts` and `Merge…` (which opens the first *mergeable* file, never a delete/mode conflict that has no line merge). It fires **once per session** off a new `onDidStartSession` lifecycle event, routes every action through the same commands the tree view uses, and the **`gittree.conflicts` tree view stays** as the persistent surface exactly as the plan specifies; `gittree.merge.conflictsDialog` turns the gate off, because an editor-area panel opening by itself is more intrusive than IntelliJ's modal. **The finding that shaped this task:** IntelliJ models *every* base↔side difference as a "change", but GitTree's blocks come from `git merge-file -p --diff3` over the index stages — and **git applies every non-overlapping change itself before the tri-editor opens**. I verified that empirically (a change with no counterpart on the other side never lands inside a conflict block), so `Left`/`Right` act on what remains *inside* the blocks and otherwise report "No non-conflicting changes to apply" with the live counter rather than quietly taking a side. Recorded as deviation **D17**. Tests: **+19 unit** (side classification incl. identical-on-both; one-sided resolution and its refusals; Left leaves the right side pending; All also takes the two-sided rules; already-resolved blocks are skipped; the counter's split and the status wording, singular and plural), **+8 Playwright** on the S7 dialog (the list with kinds and the "no line merge" marker, the four reference actions, Accept Yours carrying only ticked files, Merge… disabled with nothing mergeable, Resolve Simple, everything-unticked disabling the accepts, Close cancelling without acting), **+7 integration** driving a **real conflicted merge through the real tri-editor**: a fixture built to yield exactly two blocks — one interleave-resolvable, one genuine conflict — where **Left and Right both correctly decline the two-sided block** while `All` takes it, the counter reads `1 change, 1 conflict.` then `No changes. 1 conflict.`, `Show Details` names both revisions, the S7 params list the session's files, its `Accept Theirs` leaves their content on disk and the file no longer unmerged (matching the tree view), and a path the session does not own is refused. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 686 unit, 197 webview + 3 skipped, **220 integration passing + 2 pending** — the only red the pre-existing P7-T2 500 ms stash bound.

P13-T9 ✅ 2026-08-23 — **Parity re-audit, deviations register, docs — PHASE 13 CLOSED, and with it the whole Tier-1 rebuild.** **`FEATURES.md` rewritten**: the obsolete-contract banner is gone and every section now carries a *shape* criterion — which container, which toolbar, which menu — beside its behaviour, which is the fix for the root cause the gap analysis §1 identified (a spec of capabilities without containers, against which an audit could pass while the product looked nothing like IntelliJ). §1/§2/§5/§6/§7/§8 rewritten to match the style P11-T9 and P12-T9 established for §3/§4, plus two new sections: **§9 Dialogs, Popups and the Action Hub** and **§10 Worktrees**. §7 now states plainly that provider integrations are an owner decision, not a gap. **`docs/parity-audit.md` replaced**: scored against the rewritten contract with the scoring rule stated up front — *shape **and** behaviour, so a feature that works in the wrong kind of surface scores 🟡, not ✅* — which is precisely what the old audit could not express. Ten sections, every row carrying its evidence, and a closing table of what is still open **with the reason**. **Gap-analysis re-scored against the shipped build**: the §2 structural table (S1–S14) now records an outcome per row rather than a standing indictment (11 CLOSED, 1 PARTIAL, 2 accepted deviations), and 46 inventory rows across §3.2–3.10 were re-scored with their P11/P12/P13 evidence. Counting the same way as the original measurement: **❌ 122 → 79 · 🟡 36 → 33 · ✅ 75 → 118** (+43 at parity). §0 rewritten to carry both the original finding and the outcome, and to name what remains — dominated by the Differences Viewer (Tier 2, D1) and provider integrations (D11). **`docs/deviations.md` finalized** at D1–D17, with D15 (`Push tags: Current Branch` → `--follow-tags`), D16 (worktree lock/prunable derived on git < 2.36) and D17 (`Apply non-conflicting changes` acts on conflict blocks, because git pre-applies the rest) added during this phase. **`docs/keyboard.md`** carries `Alt+\``, `Ctrl+Alt+N` and `F2`, and records that `Ctrl+T`/`Ctrl+Shift+K` now open dialogs. **`readme.md`** parity matrix regenerated — ten sections, honest ✅/🟡 marks with the deviation named on every 🟡, and the measured counts in the lede. **`CHANGELOG.md`** gained the Phase 12 and Phase 13 entries (it had only Phase 11). **`docs/perf.md`** gained the Tier-1-close results. **Gates at close:** typecheck + lint clean, **686 unit**, **197 webview** (+3 skipped), **220 integration passing + 2 pending**, **`test:perf` 8/8 with every §13 budget met** — activation 3.1 ms (≤100), status p95 348 ms (≤400), **log first page 537.8 ms (≤600 — the 611.5 ms reading at P12-T9 was overnight load, exactly as suspected there, and the budget was rightly left alone)**, blame 102.1/0 ms (≤300/30), memory +89.7 MB (≤150) — and the bundle at **1.19 MB effective vs 2.50 MB**. The only red is the pre-existing P7-T2 500 ms stash-view bound, flagged for the owner. **One real bug found by the full run:** the new S7 conflicts gate auto-opens a webview panel whenever a conflict session starts, and that stole editor focus from P6-T4's chevron test. The auto-open is now suppressed under the integration harness (`GITTREE_TEST_CONFLICTS_DIALOG=1` re-arms it); the dialog's own halves stay directly covered by the P13 suite.

P14-T1 ✅ 2026-08-23 — GitTree activity-bar container. New `contributes.viewsContainers.activitybar` entry `gittree` (`media/gittree-activitybar.svg`, a stroke+fill commit-graph mark drawn in `currentColor` so VS Code's activity-bar mask themes it in light/dark/HC), and all ten views move from `contributes.views.scm` to `contributes.views.gittree`. **View IDs are unchanged**, which is the whole reason this is an S: every `when` clause, `view/title` and `view/item/context` contribution, `TreeView`/`WebviewViewProvider` registration and test lookup keeps working untouched. `gittree.log` stays in the `gittree-panel` panel container — the Git tool window at the bottom is the §2A shape and was already right. Two **functional** call sites found and repointed: `src/scm/hunkActions.ts` (`gittree.hunk.openCommitView`) and `src/merge/lifecycle.ts` (post-merge "open the commit UI") both ran `workbench.view.scm`, which after this change reveals VS Code's Source Control view rather than ours — now `workbench.view.extension.gittree`. Revealing the *container* rather than `gittree.commit.focus` is deliberate: while `gittree.commit.window: native` still exists (T2 retires it) the visible commit surface may be `gittree.changelists`, and the container reveal is correct in both modes. Walkthrough copy in `package.nls.json` re-pointed (three steps: commitWindow, firstCommit, resolveConflict) with `[Open GitTree](command:workbench.view.extension.gittree)` replacing `command:workbench.view.scm`. New nls key `gittree.container.title`. New integration suite `test/integration/suite/phase14.test.ts` asserts the phase's *structural* commitments against the live extension manifest — an activitybar container exists with an SVG icon, `views.scm` is `undefined`, `views.gittree` holds exactly the ten expected ids in order, `gittree-panel` still holds only the log — plus a live check that the commit view still resolves from the new container (a manifest assertion cannot see that a container move actually renders). **Fixed in passing:** `docs/plan/PROGRESS.md` contained two literal NUL bytes inside the P11-T6 entry where the text `root\0id\0rel` was meant as an escape; ripgrep classified the whole file as binary and refused to search it. Replaced with the two-character escape. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 686 unit, 223 integration passing + 2 pending (+3 new), webview untouched so `test:webview` not re-run; the only red in the full run was the documented P7-T2 stash-500ms load flake (520 ms vs the 500 ms bound — the pre-existing owner-decision item, unrelated to this task).

P14-T2a ✅ 2026-08-23 — Commit window native context menus. **Split from the plan's T2, and deliberately ordered before it.** Auditing what T2 deletes turned up something the plan missed: the `gittree.changelists` tree view is the *only* menu home for `gittree.changelist.{new,rename,delete,setActive,moveTo,commitList}`. Deleting it as specified would have dropped changelist management to palette-only — every one of those commands is palette-visible, so nothing became unreachable, but right-clicking a changelist row in the commit window would have offered nothing. Doing the additive half first means no commit in this phase ever ships that regression. `commitTree.ts` gains `groups: Map<string, CommitGroupRef>` alongside the existing `files` map — the group node path already encodes identity as `@g:<repoRoot>:<groupId>`, but `groupKey` joins with `:` and a Windows repo root contains one, so parsing it back would be guesswork; the map is the honest answer. `App.tsx` supplies `FileTree`'s existing `rowContext` (the kit already had it, from P11-T6's shelf work): group rows carry `{webviewSection:'commitGroup', repoRoot, listId, listIsDefault, listCheckable}`, file rows `{webviewSection:'commitFile', repoRoot, relPath}`, folder rows carry nothing — a folder stands for no changelist and no file. Ten `webview/context` entries dispatch the existing commands, `when`-gated on `gittree.paradigm == changelists` for the changelist-specific ones (offering "Move to Changelist" in the staging paradigm would be a lie), on `!listIsDefault` for delete and on `listCheckable` for shelve. Host-side the commands learn one new arg shape each rather than growing a second code path: `listArg` also accepts `{repoRoot, listId}`, a new `fileCtxArg` resolves `{repoRoot, relPath}`, `changelist.moveTo` falls back to it, and `fileOps.normalize` gains the same shape so `openDiff`/`openFile`/`rollbackFile` work from a commit-window row. Unresolvable repo roots return undefined and the commands no-op — a context object can outlive the repo it names, and acting on the wrong repo would be worse than not acting (the P12-T2 rule). Testing: +3 unit (groups map covers exactly the group rows and never a file row; identity stays distinct when two repos share a list id; unversioned reports `checkable:false` so shelve is not offered), +1 Playwright (group/file/folder rows carry exactly the right context and a file row carries no `listId`), +1 integration (`setActive` dispatched with the webview shape flips the active list; an unknown repoRoot is a no-op, not a throw — the suite flips to the changelists paradigm and restores it, per the shared-window rule). Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 689 unit (+3), 198 webview (+1), 225 integration passing + 2 pending (+2); zero failures this run — even the P7-T2 stash flake passed.

P14-T2b ✅ 2026-08-24 — Retire the native commit surface. `gittree.commit.window` (setting, `commit.window.toggle` command, `gittree.commitWindow` context key, `commitWindowMode()`) is gone, and with it the four `SourceControlResourceGroup`s, **31** `scm/*` menu entries (`scm/title` 17, `scm/resourceState/context` 9, `scm/resourceGroup/context` 5) and **25** `view/title`+`view/item/context` entries belonging to the two retired views. **Sequencing deviation from the plan:** `scm/change/title` (3 entries) is deliberately KEPT until T4. The plan folds all 34 into T2, but those three are the quick-diff peek toolbar and the quick-diff provider does not go until T4 — deleting them here would leave a live peek widget with a dead toolbar for two commits. Every task in this phase should be shippable on its own. Views `gittree.changelists` and `gittree.shelf` deleted along with their `createTreeView` calls — which is not optional: `createTreeView` throws on an id the manifest does not contribute, so a view removed from `package.json` and left registered in code is an activation-time crash, not dead weight. `ChangelistsViewProvider` and `src/scm/resourceDecorations.ts` deleted outright (the latter existed only to decorate SCM resource states, and took ~18 `gitDecoration.*` `ThemeColor` references with it — a down payment on T5); `ShelfViewProvider` deleted from `shelfView.ts` while `ShelfNode`/`shelfDiffSides`/`isShowUnshelvedEnabled` stay, because the diff-pair helper still takes that shape. `sourceControl.ts` is now a shell: no groups, `inputBox.visible = false`, `sc.count` recomputed from the same classification the groups used to render, and `GitTreeResourceState` demoted from `vscode.SourceControlResourceState` to a plain `{repoRoot, entry, group}` value object that the diff/file-op commands still pass around. `messageBoxFor` is the single commit-message path and is exported on the phase03 registry, which is how the shelve flow's default-name draft stopped reaching into the phase01 registry for the input box. **Correction, recorded at P14-T4:** this entry originally also claimed `rewrite/undoDrop.ts` had been repointed here. It had not — the change was described but not made, and it stayed invisible because `undoDrop` reaches the registry through a `debugRegistry.get(...) as Shape` cast, which typecheck cannot see through. T4 found it (along with two more of the same kind) and fixed it there. Removing `pushShelves` and the `showUnshelved` config listener is safe because `ShelfBridge` already carries its own per-store `onDidChange` subscriptions and its own listener for that setting — checked before deleting, not assumed. Tests: `useCommitWindow` deleted from `setup.ts`; phase01 T1 now asserts the status-entry classification and the count badge (what the four groups always rendered) instead of group membership, T4 drives `gittree.add`/`unstage`/`rollbackFile` through the `{repoRoot, relPath}` context — the T2a path, so the deletion buys extra coverage of the new one — and T7 reads the commit window's message box; phase03 drops the tree-node shape assertion (the DTO is the contract now, asserted in phase11) and passes `{repoRoot, listId}` to `commitList`; phase07's shelf-UI test keeps its real substance by calling `shelfDiffSides` directly and verifying both reconstructed sides, losing only the tree plumbing; phase11's two surface-switching tests go with the surface. **Two latent test defects surfaced, neither introduced here.** (1) phase01 T7's commit was hanging on the check-pipeline gate — the suite never declared `GITTREE_TEST_CHECKS_CHOICE` the way phase03's T5 does and had been passing on luck; it now sets *and restores* it (deleting would disarm the runner's process-wide value, the trap `setup.ts` documents). (2) `commitAmend` decides what it is amending from the repo model while `gittree.commit` resolves with its refresh still in flight; the test now waits for `model.snapshot.headSha` to equal the new commit rather than relying on elapsed time. The tell was that it passed whenever diagnostics were added — timing, not logic. **Environmental find, reported not fixed:** `%TEMP%` held **662** leaked `gittree-*` fixture repos dating to 2026-07-11, because `runTests.ts` and `FixtureRepo.dispose()` both swallow Windows cleanup failures by design. With them present the suite ran 10–11 min with 5–6 cascading `unable to write symref for HEAD: Permission denied` failures; cleared, it ran 7 min with one failure. A startup sweep of stale fixture dirs is worth its own task and may also be worth re-measuring the P7-T2 stash budget against. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 689 unit, 223 integration passing + 2 pending, `docs/settings.md` regenerated; the only red is the documented P7-T2 stash-500ms flake (516 ms).

P14-T3 ✅ 2026-08-24 — GitTree's own change bars and change popup. New `src/scm/gutter/`: `changeModel.ts` (pure, vscode-free) converts the `LineChange[]` that `core/patch/lineDiff` already produces into editor-space rows and answers `changeAt`/`nextChange`/`prevChange`; `changeBars.ts` paints them; `changePopup.ts` replaces the peek widget; `baseText.ts` owns "what the original is". **The diff was never the work** — `computeLineChanges` already emitted VS Code-shaped changes, so T3 is a conversion plus the two rendering halves. The conversion is where the care went: `LineChange` is 1-based with a 0 sentinel per side, editor APIs are 0-based, and a deletion has no line of its own — it anchors to the line that now follows the removed text, which is what IntelliJ shows and where the user looks; a deletion at the top of the file anchors to line 0. All 19 unit tests passed on the first run, including the three cases that motivated writing them (top-of-file deletion, whole-file deletion, EOL-only change). **Border technique, per the spike:** `isWholeLine` + `borderWidth: '0 0 0 3px'` + matching `overviewRulerColor` on the Left lane. The measured alternative — `gutterIconPath` — renders in the glyph margin, the lane breakpoints use, 54px left of VS Code's own bar at zoom 2 and only 2px wide; the border lands 31px right at the exact 4px width (D18). **Colours are `editorGutter.{added,modified,deleted}Background`, which VS Code *core* registers for its own change bars — not `gitDecoration.*`, which the built-in git extension contributes and which therefore goes blank when that extension is disabled.** That is the whole point of T5's colour task and it cost nothing here, so T5 shrinks. Controller behaviour: base text cached per `(repoRoot, relPath, base)` and invalidated wholesale when a repo's `status`/`head` moves (the base *is* the index — a stale cache would compute against the wrong original); recompute debounced 150 ms on document change; untracked and ignored files get no bars, as in IntelliJ; files over `gittree.changeBars.maxFileLines` (default 20 000) paint nothing and say so in the trace rather than stalling; stale ranges are clamped to `lineCount` because an edit can land while the base read is in flight. **The popup is hover- and keyboard-invoked (D19):** VS Code exposes no click handler for a decoration and no API to open a diff peek at a location, so the bar cannot be the trigger; a `HoverProvider` scoped to changed lines only renders the previous text plus actions, and `gittree.change.showActions` (`ctrl+alt+g h`, joining the established `ctrl+alt+g` prefix) does the same at the cursor. A `CodeLens` variant was rejected — it costs a text line per change, which IntelliJ's marker does not. Two details worth keeping: a `vscode.Uri` cannot survive a `command:` URI, so the hover's links go through thin `gittree.change.*` resolver commands that re-dispatch to the existing hunk actions with a real Uri (no duplicated logic, and T4 can collapse the indirection); and the fenced block computes a fence longer than any backtick run in the body, because file content containing ``` would otherwise break out of the code block into the trusted markdown where the command links live. `baseText.ts` also de-duplicates the base read that `hunkActions` had its own copy of — a rollback computed against a different base than the bar the user clicked reverts the wrong text. **Setting rename pulled forward from T4:** `gittree.changeBars.base` is added now with a read-through fallback to `gittree.quickDiff.base` (marked deprecated), because T3 needs the setting and splitting the rename across two tasks would leave the two keys disagreeing in between. Testing: +19 unit (conversion for every kind and boundary, non-wrapping navigation in both directions, original-text extraction incl. the whole-file-deletion phantom-blank-line case), +4 integration (bars cover exactly the changed lines for a modification + insertion + deletion in one file and clear on revert; untracked files get none; the hover fires on a changed line and is silent on an unchanged one, asserted both directions against the real `vscode.executeHoverProvider`; the cutoff suppresses painting), +1 perf budget — change-bar recompute p95 **0.6 ms** at 5 000 lines with 40 scattered edits plus an insert and a delete block, against a 50 ms budget. **Two test races found and fixed at the cause, both the same shape as phase01's amend:** `refresher.refreshNow()` returning does not mean the model has observed a CLI-side change, so a file the model still calls untracked gets no bars and looks broken; and an already-open `TextDocument` does not pick up a disk write synchronously, so bars would be computed against the previous text. The helper now waits on both preconditions. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 708 unit (+19), 227 integration passing + 1 pending (+4), `test:perf` 9/9 with every budget met, `docs/settings.md` regenerated. Two reds in the full run, neither this task's: P5 reorder hit `unable to write symref for HEAD: Permission denied` and the documented P7-T2 stash flake read 552 ms.

P14-T4 ✅ 2026-08-24 — Delete the SourceControl. **`vscode.scm` now appears nowhere in `src/`** — `sourceControl.ts` and `quickDiff.ts` are deleted, the last 3 `scm/*` menu entries (`scm/change/title`, held back from T2b on purpose because the peek widget they belonged to was still alive) went with the provider, and `contributes.menus` has zero `scm/*` keys. What the SourceControl carried has its own home: the status bar owns `StatusBarItem`s (`renderStatusBar` is untouched and still carries its unit tests — only the sink moved), the count badge is `WebviewView.badge` on `gittree.commit`, and `Ctrl+Enter` is a `focusedView == gittree.commit` keybinding rather than `acceptInputCommand`. One behaviour had to be re-created rather than moved: VS Code scoped `statusBarCommands` to the *selected* provider, so with plain items the scoping is ours — each controller hides its items unless its repo is the active one, re-evaluated on `onDidChangeActiveTextEditor`, or N repos would each add three entries. `GitTreeResourceState` survives as a plain `{repoRoot, entry, group}` value object in `fileOps.ts`, its only remaining consumer; `statesFrom` (which unwrapped `scm/resourceGroup/context` arguments) is deleted as unreachable. **The find of this task: three source files were still reading a SourceControl that no longer existed, and typecheck could not see it.** All three reach the registry through `core.debugRegistry.get('phaseNN') as Shape` — a cast, so deleting the real field compiles clean and the code becomes a silent no-op. (1) `rewrite/undoDrop.ts` restored an undone commit's message into `sc.inputBox`; T2b's PROGRESS entry claimed this had been fixed and it had not, which is corrected in that entry above. (2) `merge/lifecycle.ts` prefilled `MERGE_MSG` the same way, so "Commit Merge…" would have opened with no prefill. (3) `ui/progress.ts` used `ProgressLocation.SourceControl`, which renders *inside* VS Code's Source Control view — with no provider registered the operation spinner had nowhere to appear; it is `ProgressLocation.Window` now, beside GitTree's own status items. The same cast pattern hid the breakage in four test files, which declare their own `Phase01RegistryShape` locally: phase01's status-bar assertions now read a new `StatusBarController.titles` seam instead of `SourceControl.statusBarCommands[i].title`, its count assertion moved to the classification total (phase14 asserts the badge itself), and phase05's undo-prefill reads the message box. **A real product bug fell out of chasing a flaky test:** `ChangeBarsController.apply()` awaits the base-text read, so two runs for one document interleave, and the older one — holding a status snapshot from before a `git add` — would `clear()` the result the newer one had just computed. Visible as bars vanishing for a moment after a terminal-side stage. Fixed with a per-document generation guard; the test then needed a convergence wait too, because a transient clear is legitimate product behaviour that a single read can catch. Testing: +2 integration (zero `scm/*` contributions and no `sourceControls` on the registry; the commit view badge carries the pending-change count, via a new `badgeValue` seam). Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 708 unit, 226 integration passing + 2 pending (+2); the P14 suite ran 4× consecutively clean before the full run. Three reds in the full run, none this task's: P5-T3 hit `unable to write symref for HEAD: Permission denied` (verified passing in isolation afterwards), P13-T3 cascaded from it, and the documented P7-T2 stash flake read 532 ms.

P14-T5 ✅ 2026-08-24 — Own theme colours; drop the last built-in-git couplings. **7 `gittree.color.*` colours contributed** (added, modified, deleted, renamed, untracked, ignored, conflicted), each with a default for all four theme variants — light, dark, highContrast, highContrastLight — and the values deliberately match what the built-in git extension ships, so the switch is invisible to anyone who liked the old colours. The point is owning the IDs, not restyling. 14 call sites repointed: `fileDecorations.ts` (7), `conflictsView.ts` (1) and the webview's `status.ts` (6, as `--vscode-gittree-color-*` CSS variables). The failure this prevents is concrete and was already live: `gitDecoration.*` is contributed by the built-in git extension, so disabling that extension dropped every status colour in GitTree's own views to plain foreground. T3 had already removed the largest tranche for free by using `editorGutter.*` — a VS Code *core* colour — for the change bars, and T2b took ~18 more out with `resourceDecorations.ts`. **`nativeBridge.ts` deleted** with the `gittree.merge.ui` setting: `_open.mergeEditor` was the single least stable API in the codebase (internal, underscore-prefixed, explicitly flagged in the master risk register), and with the bridge gone the setting had one option left, so it went too — along with the phase06 test that pinned it. **`git.path` read removed**, including `GitLocatorSettings.builtinGitPath` and its candidate-list entry: it only read a *setting*, so it was harmless, but "GitTree reads no `git.*` configuration" is a rule that can be checked and "reads exactly one key" is not. `gittree.gitPath`, PATH and the well-known install paths remain. Testing: +1 integration (all 7 colours contributed with a default per theme variant — a missing variant is exactly how a colour goes blank, which is the bug being fixed; and `gittree.merge.ui` is gone), 5 unit and 3 Playwright assertions repointed from `gitDecoration-*` to `gittree-color-*`, 1 unit updated for the dropped `builtinGitPath` candidate. **Two self-inflicted problems worth recording.** (1) A PowerShell replacement loop was handed a single-element array-of-arrays, which PowerShell flattens — so `$pair[0]`/`$pair[1]` became the *characters* `g` and `i` and every `g` in `src/merge/conflictsView.ts` became `i` (`string`→`strini`, `getChildren`→`ietChildren`). Typecheck caught it immediately; the file was restored from git and the edit redone through the Edit tool. Nothing corrupt was committed, and the two other files in the same loop had multiple pairs so were never flattened. (2) The `settle` helper added to phase14 in T4 fired `void bars.refreshNow(doc)` from inside a 25 ms poll without awaiting it, queueing a `git show` per tick; under full-suite load that thrashed the repo queue badly enough that it never converged — the helper written to absorb flakiness was manufacturing it. Each attempt is awaited now, polling at 200 ms, so at most one read is in flight. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 708 unit, 198 webview, 227 integration passing + 2 pending, `docs/settings.md` regenerated; the P14 suite ran clean twice before the full run. The two reds are environmental: P5-T5 hit `unable to write symref for HEAD: Permission denied` and the documented P7-T2 stash flake read 511 ms.

P14-T6 ✅ 2026-08-24 — Retire ModeManager; migrate takeover workspaces. **The migration is the important half and was written first.** New `src/core/migrations/unsetGitEnabled.ts`: on activation, a workspace whose `workspaceState['gittree.mode']` says `takeover` has the `git.enabled` override this extension wrote removed, the mode key cleared, a completion key recorded and a **non-modal** notification shown explaining that GitTree no longer needs the built-in extension disabled. Deleting `ModeManager` without this would have stranded every consenting workspace with the built-in Git permanently off and nothing left in the codebase that knew why. Three properties are deliberate and unit-tested: the injected `clearBuiltinGitEnabled()` **takes no arguments**, so no code path can set `git.enabled` to a *value* — only remove GitTree's own override, leaving a user's own setting at another scope alone; the clear happens **before** the done-flag is written, so a failed settings write leaves the workspace un-migrated and the next launch retries rather than marking success over a stranded user; and activation never fails on it (a throw is traced and retried). Workspaces that never consented, or chose companion, are written to not at all. **Everything else went:** `ModeManager`, `GitTreeMode`, `ConsentChoice`, the consent modal, the `gittree.mode` setting, the `pickMode` walkthrough step and its orphaned `media/walkthrough/mode.svg`, the `onDidChangeConfiguration('gittree.mode')` listener, and `mode` from both the debug state dump and the sanitized issue report (`IssueInfo.mode`). `Core` no longer takes or exposes a mode. The one behaviour the mode still gated — skipping explorer decorations so as not to double up with the built-in extension — becomes a plain boolean `gittree.decorations.fileExplorer` (default `true`). **Deviation from the plan, deliberate:** T6.3 said to detect `vscode.git` being active and default that setting to `false` once via the migration. It does not. Doing so writes a setting on the user's behalf unprompted *and* makes GitTree's behaviour depend on what another extension is doing — the exact coupling this phase exists to remove, and a direct contradiction of the phase's own exit criterion that enabling or disabling the built-in Git extension changes nothing about how GitTree behaves. The setting is documented as "turn this off if you would rather see only the built-in extension's decorations". Fixed in passing: `scripts/genSettingsDoc.mjs` still wrote "coexist with the built-in git extension until you opt into takeover mode" into the generated `docs/settings.md` — live product documentation for a mode that no longer exists. Testing: +5 unit (restore-once-and-explain; idempotent on a second activation; nothing written for a never-consented or companion workspace; **not** marked done when the settings write fails, so it retries; only ever clears), +1 integration (`gittree.mode` gone and `gittree.decorations.fileExplorer` present, no surviving `pickMode` step, `git.enabled` unwritten at workspace *and* global scope, and `Core` exposes no mode); the P0-T7 discovery test's mode assertions become an unconditional "activation writes nothing" guarantee, which is stronger than what it asserted before. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 704 unit (−9 ModeManager, +5 migration), **230 integration passing + 2 pending with ZERO failures** — the first completely green full run of this phase, the documented P7-T2 stash flake included.

P14-T7 ✅ 2026-08-24 — Diff controls and cross-file change navigation (D1 Route A). **The behaviour this phase was asked for by name.** VS Code's diff editor wraps to the top of the *same* file when you run off the end, because a diff editor knows two documents and has no concept of a file sequence; GitTree opens the diffs, so it holds the sequence. New `src/diff/`: `navigationStep.ts` is the pure state machine (vscode-free, 9 unit tests) implementing IntelliJ's two-stage gesture — at the last change of a file the first press does **not** jump, it arms and says which file is next; the second press crosses; at the end of the last file it says so and never wraps. `diffSession.ts` registers the sequence and recovers it from whatever diff is on screen rather than tracking a "current" one: `TabInputTextDiff` exposes both URIs, so the open tab identifies itself and nothing has to stay in sync with the user's clicking. `register.ts` wires the commands, the transient in-editor hint (an `after` decoration, the way IntelliJ's popup reads, not a notification) and Route A's view toggles (side-by-side, ignore-whitespace, collapse-unchanged) written at workspace scope so they behave as view options rather than buried preferences. **The sequence is the Changed Files pane's own contents** — whatever it is showing when you click a file. Added and deleted files are excluded: they open as plain documents with nothing to diff, and leaving them in would make them dead stops mid-walk. **Backwards lands on the previous file's LAST change**, not its first, or "previous" would skip everything in the file it just entered; that has its own unit test. Keys are IntelliJ's `F7`/`Shift+F7`, `when`-scoped to `gittree.diff.active` so core's `F7` (next symbol highlight) is untouched everywhere else, and the toolbar controls carry the same scope so they never appear on a diff GitTree knows nothing about. Ours sort ahead of VS Code's own arrows, which remain — an extension cannot unregister a core menu item (D20). Testing: +9 unit (walk within a file; **arms** at the last change instead of jumping — the case that is the whole point; crosses only on the second press; never wraps at either end armed or not; the full gesture mirrored backwards; a file with no changes still arms rather than ending; single-file sequences end immediately; landing lines), +2 integration (a real 3-file commit diff walked end to end, asserting the exact visit order and that the last change of each file is visited **twice** — once reached, once arming — then that the walk stops with a "Last change" hint instead of wrapping; and that all 5 toolbar contributions carry both `isInDiffEditor` and `gittree.diff.active`). **Diagnostic worth keeping:** the walk test first failed because **VS Code parks the cursor on the first difference when a diff opens**, so the test's "land on the first change" step was really jumping to the second. Not a defect — it means the first `F7` in a freshly-opened diff moves to change two, exactly as VS Code's own arrows behave — but a test that assumes a cold cursor at line 0 will be wrong. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, 713 unit (+9), 231 integration passing + 2 pending (+2); the P14 suite ran clean twice before the full run. One red, not this task's: the documented P7-T2 stash flake read 504 ms against its 500 ms bound.

P14-T8 ✅ 2026-08-24 — **Parity re-audit, deviations, migration docs — PHASE 14 CLOSED.** The documentation was rewritten where it had gone stale rather than appended to, because most of it described machinery that no longer exists. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` **§3 is superseded**: a notice states what holds now (no SourceControl, no `scm/*` menus, own container, own change bars, no `git.*` reads, and that enabling or disabling the built-in extension changes nothing), while the Takeover/Companion table stays as struck-through history — PROGRESS.md and six phase documents reference it, and deleting it silently would orphan them. `docs/faq.md`, `docs/migration-from-builtin.md` and `readme.md` lose the "pick a mode" onboarding entirely and gain the honest answer — there is nothing to choose, plus what happens to a workspace that *had* chosen takeover. `FEATURES.md`: the gutter section described `SourceControl.quickDiffProvider`, which no longer exists, so it is rewritten around GitTree's own bars and popup; a new section covers the diff controls and cross-file navigation with their shape criteria. `docs/deviations.md`: **D1 is narrowed, not closed** — an explicit status block records that Route A shipped and enumerates what the renderer still costs (three view modes, five-way whitespace, highlight granularity, per-change apply arrows, align changes) and why Route B stays gated rather than rejected; **D18–D22 added**. D22 is a fifth deviation beyond the four the plan anticipated, registering the T6.3 decision I declined — leaving the plan saying one thing and the code doing another would have been worse than recording the divergence. `docs/parity-audit.md` re-scored: the gutter rows moved off VS Code's quick diff, two rows added (cross-file navigation ✅, Differences Viewer toolbar 🟡), the verification base updated, and the **8.3-short-path finding** recorded as an area where owning the implementation is strictly *better* than the built-in extension's. `docs/keyboard.md` gains `Ctrl+Enter`, `Ctrl+Alt+G H` and `F7`/`Shift+F7`, with an explicit note on the three-way `F7` scoping (diff / merge / VS Code's symbol highlight — each `when`-scoped so only one is ever live) and *why* GitTree's arrows differ from VS Code's. `docs/perf.md` gains the Phase 14 run. `CHANGELOG.md` gains a "GitTree now owns its own surfaces" section written for users rather than for the plan. The phase doc gains an Outcome section with the commit table, the four plan deviations, the three real defects found while building (all pre-existing, all hidden behind `debugRegistry` casts), and the two harness weaknesses left for the owner. Gates at close: typecheck+lint clean, **713 unit · 198 webview (+3 skipped) · 231 integration (+2 pending) · `test:perf` 9/9** with every §13 budget met including the new change-bar recompute bound (0.6 ms p95 at 5 000 lines against 50 ms). The single red is the documented P7-T2 stash flake at **501 ms** against its 500 ms bound — one millisecond, and still the owner's budget decision rather than a product defect.

POST-P14 ✅ 2026-08-24 — **Multi-root commit: `Commit` commits what you ticked.** Raised from a screenshot: a workspace of ~20 repositories rendered ~20 identical `Changes  0 files` rows. The rows were the symptom; the defect underneath was worse. `commitWindowView.buildState()` emits a section per repository, every repository's default changelist is hardcoded `name: 'Changes'`, and `commitTree` kept empty groups when `isDefault` — so every repository contributed one indistinguishable row, with the repo name appearing only as a *folder row inside* the group, which an empty group does not have. **The real defect:** the tree spans every repository and the `N of M` counter counts every repository, but `App.tsx` sent `repoRoot: activeRepo` and the flow committed that one repo alone — with the winner decided by *which file tab was last focused*, and nothing on screen naming it. Ticking files in two repositories and pressing Commit silently committed one and left the other ticked. Fixed as IntelliJ behaves: `runCommitAcross` computes the participating repositories through `hasCommittableChanges` — the same predicate `buildState` publishes as `targets`, so the button label and the flow cannot disagree — and commits each in turn with one message, stopping at the first refusal and naming what already landed rather than implying it away. **≤1 participant delegates to `runCommitFlow` unchanged**, which is why the staging paradigm's commit-all offer, every existing command and all 8 `gittree.commit <root>` test call sites keep byte-identical behaviour; an explicit repo argument stays scoped, because a context menu naming a repository must not be widened. **Two holes found reviewing my own code, both silent:** the message lives in a *per-repo* box, so committing a repo other than the focused one read that repo's empty box and rejected a commit whose message was plainly on screen (now handed down explicitly, and the box that held it is cleared); and amend is per-repo with `gittree.commit.toggleAmend <root>` able to arm it anywhere, so the spanning loop could reach a repo with amend armed and **rewrite its history as part of a plain commit** — the guard now checks every participant, not just the focused one, and asks before scoping. Tree: group rows read `repoA: Changes` in multi-root and the redundant repo folder row is gone (the group label was only ever standing in for it); empty default lists are dropped once several roots contribute one each, so `No changes.` covers the all-clean case. **A crash the type system could not catch:** `CommitStateDto.targets` is required, but the Playwright mock host is a template string — untyped — so it shipped without the field and blanked the entire panel; the webview now reads defensively across `postMessage`, where the compiler enforces nothing, and the mock was made faithful. Harness: **repoD and repoE added** (`runTests.ts`, workspace now 5 repos, `discovery.test.ts` count updated) because this suite needs two repos dirty at once and commits in both — borrowing A/B/C would leave debris in repos three other suites assert against; the spanning test asserts up front that only D and E are targeted, so a stray tick from another suite fails loudly here instead of being swallowed into this commit and surfacing elsewhere later. Deviation **D23** records what did *not* change: changelists stay per-repository (IntelliJ's are project-global), because `changelistsFor(repo)` is consumed by 38 call sites across 9 modules and going global changes membership to `(repoRoot, path)`, makes `reconcile()` watch N models, and needs a state migration — a phase of work whose whole payoff is a nicer tree shape, worth it only for genuinely *related* repositories. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, **714 unit (+1) · 198 webview (+3 skipped) · 235 integration (+2 pending)** with the new 5-test Multi-root suite green twice, once inside the full run and once targeted. The single red is the same documented P7-T2 stash flake, this run at **518 ms** against its 500 ms bound.

POST-P14b ✅ 2026-08-25 — **One `Changes`, one `Unversioned Files`, repositories beneath — IntelliJ's shape.** Raised against the previous entry's own output: GitTree rendered one group row *per repository*, IntelliJ renders one row *per changelist* with a repository row under it. This supersedes the tree half of POST-P14 — the `repoA: Changes` labels and the dropped folder row are gone again, deliberately. **The insight that made it affordable:** the tree shape and the storage model are separable, so `assembleCommitTree` merges while `ChangelistService` stays per repository — D23 is rewritten from "changelists are per-repository" to "stored per repository, presented per project". **The merge key is exact where it can be and approximate only where it must be:** `DEFAULT_LIST_ID` and `UNVERSIONED_ID` are module constants shared by every repository, so those two merge by **id** and nothing is guessed — a repository always has precisely those two. A user list gets a random per-repo id (`cl-<time>-<rand>`), so it can only merge by **name**; the default is keyed off `isDefault` rather than its name so a rename in one repository cannot split the row every repository's new changes land in. **Repository rows are built as real `buildTree` roots, never as a `${label}/` path prefix** — the prefix is what the code did before, and `buildTree` collapses single-child chains, so a repository whose only change is `docs/x.md` rendered as one row reading `demo/docs` with the repository row silently gone, exactly when there is least else on screen to infer it from; that has its own test. Node keys moved from `@g:<repoRoot>:<listId>` to `@g:<mergeKey>[@r:<repoRoot>]`. **Actions fan out, and the fan-out is made honest rather than implied:** a merged row carries `targets` (every repository behind it) and `rename`/`delete`/`setActive`/`commitList` apply to all of them; `commitList` reuses `runCommitAcross` with a new per-repo `lists` map so one changelist commits across repositories with one message, through the machinery the Commit button already uses. `changelist.new` now creates the list in **every** repository — without that the merged row would be a facade over lists that exist in only some, and `Move to…` would offer a name in one repository and not another. Rows that mean exactly one repository — every row in a single-root workspace, and every `repo` row in a multi-root one — still carry `{repoRoot, listId}`, so scoped behaviour and all four existing call sites in phase03/phase14 are untouched; `shelf.shelve` is gated `!listSpansRepos` because a shelf belongs to a repository, and lives on the repository row instead. `delete` across repositories skips the per-repo "move its files to…" pick rather than raising one prompt per repository for one gesture. **A cross-suite bug of my own making, found by the full run and not by the targeted one:** the new suite left editors open and — because `changelist.new` now creates project-wide — left a changelist in all five repositories while its teardown cleaned two. P4 (repoA) and P12 (repoC) resolve *which repository to act on* from the active editor, so five of their tests failed against repoD/repoE with `Not a valid branch point` and log-paging timeouts. `suiteTeardown` closes all editors and the teardown now cleans every repository; the same five pass. Worth keeping: the targeted run (`GITTREE_TEST_ONLY`) and even `Multi-root|P12` were both green — only the full ordering exposed it, which is the argument for never treating a targeted run as the gate. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, **723 unit (+9) · 198 webview (+3 skipped) · 241 integration (+1 pending), zero failing** — the P7-T2 stash flake passed this run too. One webview flake seen twice, both times in a run started immediately after the 7-minute integration suite, green on all four clean runs including immediately after each failure with no code change.

POST-P14c ✅ 2026-08-25 — **The log's `Branch ▾` filter names HEAD.** Raised from a screenshot: the dropdown offered `All branches` and every ref, but nothing called HEAD, so filtering to the current branch looked unsupported. It was not — `DEFAULT_FILTER` is `refs: 'HEAD'` and four gestures already produced it (the Branches pane's `HEAD (Current)` row, the chip's `×`, typing `HEAD` into the ref combo, unticking everything) — but the model treats HEAD as the *absence* of a branch filter (`buildLogArgs` pushes no ref argv for it, which is what plain `git log` already means), and that absence was carried straight into the UI as an unnamed state. **A filter list that names one preset and leaves its opposite implicit reads as a missing feature**, which is exactly how it was reported. `HEAD (Current)` is now the first row, ticked whenever no branch filter is set, and spelled to match the Branches pane rather than inventing a third name for one concept. **Idempotent, not a toggle:** there is no state to un-check HEAD *into*, so an already-ticked click is a no-op rather than a redundant `onChange` — `applyFilter` restarts the whole log session on every change, so a symmetric toggle here would re-run `git log` to arrive back where it started. **A reachable display bug fixed alongside, because the new row forced the predicate to be written correctly:** `range` outranks `refs` entirely in `buildLogArgs` (`--left-right a...b`), and both range setters are live — `Compare with current` (`commitActions.ts:248`) and `Show in log` after an update (`updateDialog.ts:266`), each sending a range with no refs. But `All branches` was checked off raw `filter.refs === 'ALL'`, and its toggle was **the only ref mutation in the file that did not `delete next.range`**. Ticking it under a range therefore left the range winning: the checkbox claimed one thing and the log showed another. Both presets now derive from the same precedence `refsLabel` already used for the chip, both clear the range, and the `All branches` toggle keys off its *displayed* state so clicking an unticked box can never produce HEAD. **Deleted:** `LogView.pickBranches` and `LOG_METHODS.pickBranches` — the host QuickPick that backed the branch chip before P12-T5 replaced it with the in-webview dropdown, with zero callers in `webview-ui` or the tests ever since. It was also the *only* surface that still named HEAD (`$(target) HEAD only`), so two surfaces of one filter disagreed about whether HEAD is nameable; now there is one surface and it names it. **Same-class dead code left in place and reported rather than removed:** `pickAuthors` and `pickDates` are equally uncalled, orphaned by the same refactor — only `pickPaths` survives, wired to `Browse…` — but removing them was outside what was asked, and a deletion nobody sanctioned is not a free win. Testing: +2 Playwright — HEAD present, first and ticked by default with the presets mutually exclusive in both directions; and a synthesised range-carrying `log/reset` proving neither preset reads as active under a range and that picking one drops the range. **The existing `Branch ▾` test indexed checkboxes positionally (`.nth(1)`)**, so inserting a row above it was load-bearing; every spec touching `filter-branch` was checked rather than only the one already known about. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, **723 unit (unchanged) · 200 webview (+2, +3 skipped) · 240 integration passing + 2 pending, zero failing**. The integration total is 242 either way — the previous run's 241+1 and this run's 240+2 are the same tests; the mover is the `copy revision number writes the clipboard` probe (`phase04.test.ts:119`), one of exactly two capability gates in the suite and the only nondeterministic one, which self-documents the Windows clipboard as a shared lockable OS resource. The P7-T2 stash-latency flake passed again.

POST-P14d ✅ 2026-08-26 — **The Branches context menu on the checked-out branch.** Raised from a side-by-side screenshot: right-clicking the current branch offered four rows where IntelliJ offers seven. **Nothing was missing — the rules were.** `branchesView` marks the checked-out branch with its own `contextValue` (`branch:current`), but nine of the sixteen menu entries were written as `viewItem =~ /^branch:(local|remote)$/`, an anchored two-value regex that silently excludes it; `git blame` shows both idioms authored in the same P2 commit (`3fc57602` spelled `(local|current)` for rename/push/favourite and `(local|remote)` for the rest), and P13-T5 then copied the wrong one for all five entries it added. **The branches popup was worse:** `refTypeOf` narrows the current branch to type `head`, and `push`/`rename`/`toggleFavorite`/`newWorktree` all tested `type === 'local'`, so the popup's `›` submenu offered HEAD exactly one row — `New Branch from 'X'…` — while the tree offered four and the log's Branches pane offered nine unfiltered. Three surfaces of one registry, three different answers, under a file header that says they "must never drift". **The collision is now named once:** `isLocalBranch(ctx)` states that `local` and `head` are not alternatives but the same ref seen from two surfaces, and the five predicates that used to spell one of them read it instead. **Restored to the current branch:** `Show Diff with Working Tree` (on HEAD that is the uncommitted work, which is exactly why IntelliJ offers it there — `compareWithCurrent` remains correctly hidden, having no two sides) and `New Worktree from 'X'…`. **New — `Update`:** on the checked-out branch it delegates to Update Project rather than growing a second implementation of the same fetch-and-integrate; on any other branch git cannot integrate without a checkout, so it fast-forwards the ref in place with `git fetch . <upstream>:<branch>` — a refspec **without** a leading `+`, so the refusal on a non-fast-forward is git's own rather than a check of ours that could drift from the ref it guards. A diverged branch is reported with its counts, never rewritten: the user cannot see what they would lose from a branch that is not in front of them. **New — `Tracked Branch`:** the upstream's own action set re-aimed at `origin/<branch>`, a real VS Code submenu in the tree and a drill-in row in the popup, both running the same registry entries the remote row runs — no second implementation, so the two can never come to disagree. `Pull into Current Using Rebase` was added alongside the merge variant to complete it (a documented 🟡 closed). **Gating is spelled twice, deliberately:** `when` clauses cannot read a node's fields, so tracking is projected into the contextValue as a `:tracked` suffix, with `branchActions.upstreamOf` as the definition; a comment on each points at the other. The suffix forced **every** clause off `$` and `==` onto `(:|$)` — the same anchoring mistake that caused the original bug would otherwise have re-introduced it, this time on tracked branches. **Menu order realigned** to the group order `branchActions.ts` has always documented (checkout → compare → integrate → worktree → push → tracked → manage), which is the reference's; the tree had `New Worktree` sorting after `Delete`. The current tracked branch now renders IntelliJ's seven rows in IntelliJ's order, plus our `Toggle Favorite`. **Two live defects fixed while surfacing the rows that exposed them:** `New Worktree from 'X'` sent only a branch name to `gittree.worktree.add`, whose `repoOf` fell back to `repos[0]` — in a multi-root workspace it built the worktree from another repository's branch of the same name; and a start point that is not a local branch (`origin/x` from the new submenu, or any remote row, which has always been offered) was absent from the only list the dialog shows, so the seed silently became whichever local branch sorted first. **Deviation recorded:** VS Code submenu titles are static, so the tree's submenu reads `Tracked Branch` without the ref name; the popup, whose labels are built in code, names it. Testing: **+10 unit** (`branchMenu.test.ts`) evaluating the real `when` clauses against all six contextValues and asserting each menu **whole** — the class of bug this task fixes is invisible to every other kind of test, because a row that vanishes fails nothing; **+8 unit** on the fast-forward argv, including that no refspec is ever forced; **+7 integration** (`phase14branchmenu.test.ts`) over a local bare-repo remote — the tracked bit appearing and disappearing with the upstream, the current-branch action set, `Update` fast-forwarding a branch nobody has checked out, `Update` leaving a diverged branch exactly where it was, and a tracked row landing on `origin/<branch>` rather than the row; **+1 Playwright** on the worktree dialog opening in new-branch mode for a HEAD seed. The existing `phase02` contextValue assertion moved from equality to prefix, since whether the shared fixture has an upstream at that point in the run is not that test's subject. **A harness fact worth recording, found by a failure:** `StatusRefresher.refreshNow()` **coalesces** — if a refresh is already running it records "go round again" and resolves immediately, so awaiting it does *not* mean the snapshot has caught up. Every row added here is gated on tracking read from that snapshot, and the untracked-branch test, which awaited only `refreshNow()`, acted on a branch the model still believed was tracked and fast-forwarded it. The suite now pairs every mutation with `refreshUntil(predicate)`; the same omission in the fast-forward, diverged and tracked-row tests would have made each pass for the wrong reason, so all four were fixed rather than only the one that went red. The product behaviour under the same staleness is deliberate and unchanged — `branchesPaneBridge` already documents it: dispatch runs the action and lets git give its own clear error rather than second-guessing a snapshot that may be older than the menu built from it. **Left undone and reported:** the log window's Branches pane still contributes its nine rows unconditionally — `Rename`/`Delete`/`Push` appear on tags there — which is the same defect class in the opposite direction and wants `isCurrent`/`upstream` on its `data-vscode-context`; and `Checkout and Update` remains ❌.

POST-P14e ✅ 2026-08-26 — **`Paths ▾ · Roots`, and which repository the log is showing.** Raised from a side-by-side screenshot of the reference's `Paths` popup (`Select…` · `Select in Tree…` · a `Roots` section of five ticked repositories) against ours (a `path or glob` box and `Browse…`), with the reporter's own diagnosis attached: *"I think it shows the commit graph of just 1 random branch."* Close — one random **repository**. `LogViewProvider.activeRepo()` read `activeTextEditor` → `repoForUri` → `session.repo` → `repos[0]`, and `startSession` runs on *every* `init`, `setFilter`, `refresh` and rehydrate, so the repository was re-derived on every one of them. Changing a filter while a file from another repository happened to be focused moved the log there **and re-ran the filter against it** — `paths` are repo-relative and a `refs` list names one repository's branches, so the move either emptied the log or failed the page read on `unknown revision`. Nothing on screen named the repository and nothing could choose one. **The guess had already leaked into callers.** `revealCommit(sha, repoRoot)` compared its `repoRoot` argument against the session, and on a mismatch called `applyFilter(session.filter)` — which dropped the argument on the floor and re-guessed, so "show me this commit" landed wherever the editor pointed and then paged 25 times for a sha that was never going to be there. `showFileHistory` computed `repoRelative(repo.root, uri)` and then never said *which* repo, so an Explorer right-click on a file in another repository filtered the repository on show by a path it had never had; `compareInLog` resolved its repository, read `headRef` from it, and handed `--left-right a...b` to whichever repository the log was on. The blame hover's `Show in Log` and the rebase editor's row action passed no root at all. **The test suite was already compensating for it, in writing:** `phase12` T2 wrote an anchor file and opened it — *"The log session follows the active editor's repo; anchor it to repoC the way a user working in this repo would be"* — and `multiRoot`'s teardown closes every editor because *"several later suites resolve 'the repository to act on' from the active editor (logView.activeRepo, pickRepo)"*. Two workarounds for a guess, neither of which a user can perform. **The repository is now an input, not a derivation.** `startSession(filter, repoRoot?)` and `setFilter(filter, repoRoot?)` take it; the focused editor seeds it once and it is then held in `workspaceState` (`gittree.log.root`), so it survives a restart and only an explicit act moves it — a `Roots` row, or a caller that names its repository. All five callers above now name theirs, and both suites dropped their workaround for a one-line `showRoot()` claim. Moving root runs `rootAgnostic()`, which drops exactly what cannot travel — `paths`, `follow`, `range`, and an explicit `refs` array — and keeps what can: authors, dates, the text query, the sort. A **cross-repository `revealCommit` keeps only the sort** and opens on `refs: 'ALL'`, on a different rule: reveal inside one repository respects that repository's filter, but there is no filter for a repository the log has not been showing, and carrying another one's author or text query over would routinely hide the very commit that was asked for. **`Paths ▾` rebuilt to the reference's shape.** `Select…` is `Browse…` renamed and fixed twice over: it *adds* to the selection like the panel's own text box always did rather than replacing it, and a pick outside the repository on show is now reported and pointed at `Roots` instead of being dropped in silence — where dropping *every* pick left `paths: []`, which `buildLogArgs` reads as no path filter at all, so the filter silently widened to unfiltered. `Select in Tree…` is new: `buildPathTree` (pure, `src/log/pathTree.ts`) turns `git ls-files -z` into tree *order plus depth*, since a QuickPick cannot expand a node, and the label indents by it. Its overflow rule is deliberate — over the cap it drops **files wholesale** rather than truncating mid-walk, because a mid-walk cap leaves the alphabetical tail unreachable *by search*, which reads as "this repository has no such file"; dropping a whole kind is at least a rule the title can state. `mergeTreeSelection` folds the pick back in on the rule that the tree owns only what the tree offered, so a glob — which has no row — and a file the folders-only mode withheld both survive a pick untouched rather than reading as un-ticked. `Roots` lists every workspace repository with its graph-palette swatch, disambiguating a colliding basename with its parent directory, and the panel title carries the name of the repository on show. The list is kept current through `onDidAddRepo`/`onDidRemoveRepo` on a **payload-only event** (`evtRoots`) that merges into `init` rather than through `evtReset`, which would restart paging and drop the selection over a list of repository names. **Deviation recorded — D24.** The reference merges every VCS root into one graph, so its `Roots` rows are checkboxes and un-ticking one removes that root's commits; ours renders one repository, so the rows are **radios**. Checkboxes that silently enforce one tick would look like the reference and behave like nothing at all. D24 states what lifting it costs, and none of it is a VS Code limit: `getPage` pages one repository with `--skip`/`--max-count`, and N disjoint histories make that a k-way date merge with a cursor per root; every row would need a `repoRoot` and with it `getDetails`, `changedFiles`, `openFileDiff`, `containedBranches`, `goTo`, `showFileHistory` and all of `commitActions`, which resolve their repository from the *session* today; `sort: 'topo'` has no meaning across disjoint histories. Testing: **+15 unit** (`pathTree.test.ts`) on tree order, the dirs-before-files rule at every level, both overflow modes, that every emitted path is a real pathspec, and the five selection-merge rules including the glob; **+7 integration** (`logRoots.test.ts`) — the `Roots` list *is* the workspace with distinct labels, the log holding its root across a filter change **while another repository's file is focused** (the reported sequence, verbatim), a root switch dropping the repo-relative filter and keeping the portable one field by field, a root outside the workspace refused rather than guessed at, and `Show File History` / `Compare with Current` / `revealCommit` each landing in the repository they name; **+1 Playwright** on the `Roots` rows, including that re-picking the root already on show posts nothing — `setRoot` re-runs the whole log, and rows and selection go with it. The suite is read-only by construction: it asserts against history and files the fixtures already have, and puts the pinned root back in teardown. **Left undone and reported:** the multi-repo graph itself (D24), which is a phase, not a task; `Show Root Names` / per-row root colour stripes, which only mean something once one graph carries more than one root; and — raised by the same report, on a second axis — `DEFAULT_FILTER` is `refs: 'HEAD'`, so the log *does* open on one branch's history where the reference opens on the whole graph. `All branches` is one click away in `Branch ▾` and both presets are named (POST-P14c), but the **default** differs. Not changed here: it is the `Branch ▾` filter rather than `Paths ▾`, and `--all` on a large repository is the exact traffic the §13 budgets in `docs/perf.md` were validated against under the HEAD default. Reported for an owner decision. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, **756 unit (+15) · 202 webview (+1, 3 skipped) · 256 integration passing + 1 pending, zero failing**. The integration total is 257 across every run of this change — 250 before, +7 here — so nothing was dropped in the runs that were not green. Three of those runs were dirty and none of them the same way: one lost six tests across P9/P10/P11/P13 to `spawnSync git UNKNOWN` and `git exited with code 3221225794` (`STATUS_DLL_INIT_FAILED`) — Windows refusing to *start* a process, not git failing — under three back-to-back Electron suites; the next lost the two P3 `T4` hunk-staging tests and one P4, all three of which passed on a re-run of identical code. The P4 mover is the `copy revision number` clipboard probe, the suite's one documented nondeterministic capability gate, which is also why pending reads 1 here and 2 in earlier runs: it ran instead of skipping.

POST-P14f ✅ 2026-08-26 — **A folder full of repositories now shows its repositories.** Raised from the field: opening one directory that *contains* several checkouts left the extension completely empty — no repositories, no branches, no log — where listing the same checkouts in a `.code-workspace` file worked. **Root cause, and it is a design gap from P0 rather than a regression:** `PHASE-00-foundation.md` step 1 specifies "workspace folders + parent-walk (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`)", and that is exactly and only what shipped. `rev-parse` walks *upward*, so an open folder resolves to the repository it sits *inside*; a folder that merely holds repositories resolves to none of them, `scan()` returns `[]`, and every panel is blank. Nothing in `src/` ever looked down — grep for `readdir` across the tree finds the git-dir watcher, the shelf store, local history and the playground, and no discovery caller. Verified rather than assumed: a fixture of three sibling checkouts under a plain parent answers `fatal: not a git repository` to the one probe `scan()` makes. **The fix is a downward breadth-first walk under each open folder**, mirroring the algorithm VS Code's built-in git actually runs — read from `resources/app/extensions/git/dist/main.js` in the 1.134.0 test download rather than from memory, along with its defaults (`repositoryScanMaxDepth` 1, `repositoryScanIgnoredFolders` `["node_modules"]`, `.git` never descended into, unreadable directories warned past). `gittree.repositoryScan.maxDepth` (default 1, `0` restores upward-only, `-1` unlimited) and `gittree.repositoryScan.ignoredFolders` carry the same defaults; both re-scan on change rather than on the next reload. One deliberate divergence from the reference implementation: candidates are pre-filtered on a `.git` entry (one `stat`) instead of spawning git for every subdirectory, so the probe count equals the real repository count — observably identical, because a subdirectory without `.git` resolves by parent-walk to a root the folder itself already contributed. Probes run 8-way parallel. **The nested-repo rule was generalized, not bypassed:** "drop a nested root unless it is an open workspace folder" became "unless it was found *directly*" — the probed directory is itself the root. On the no-scan path the two are provably the same predicate (probing folder F returns F iff F is a root iff F is in the old `folderKeys`), and all seven original unit tests pass untouched; with the scan on it is what makes opening the parent folder and listing every checkout in a `.code-workspace` file produce the *identical* list, which is the property the report actually asked for and which is asserted directly in both suites. Three second-order fixes the change forced: rediscovery is now **serialized** (three triggers instead of one, and two interleaved runs would both see a key as missing, both `addRepo`, and leak the first handle's watchers), a failed `addRepo` no longer aborts the remaining repositories, and a `**/.git` create/delete watcher runs a debounced rediscovery so a clone or `git init` joins without a reload. Repositories also open **4 at a time instead of strictly one after another**: measured on the reporter's own `code` directory, the scan finds **37 repositories at depth 1 in 16.8 ms** across 62 directories — the discovery is cheap, but opening 37 repositories at several git invocations each, sequentially, is seconds of blocked activation. Default depth 1 on an ordinary single repository costs 2.8 ms over 15 directories and finds nothing extra; `-1` on this repository is 326 ms over 410 directories, which is why the unlimited walk is opt-in and bounded at 2 000 directories with a trace line naming what it did not search — a cap that reports itself rather than returning a short list that reads as complete. **Testing: +16 unit** (the walk at each depth, the ignore list, the parity assertion that scanning a parent equals opening each child, the still-dropped indirect nested case, unreadable directories, the runaway cap, and the glob matcher including anchoring and regex metacharacters treated literally) **and +5 integration** against a real temp fixture of three sibling checkouts plus one a level deeper, driven through the real git executor so Windows path spellings get a say, including that the same fixture yields nothing without the scan — the bug, asserted — and that both settings are actually contributed under the ids Core reads. The fixture is built outside the shared workspace and removed in teardown; adding a workspace folder would perturb every other suite in the window. **Not changed:** the two settings are window-scoped where VS Code's are per-folder (existing house pattern, recorded in the gap analysis, not worth the API churn for the rare workspace that wants different depths per folder), and `git.scanRepositories`' explicit extra-paths list has no equivalent. **The `.git` watcher was folded into the existing one, and the suite is what caught it.** The first cut registered a second `FileSystemWatcher` on `**/.git`. P7-T2 (`view reflects external CLI stashes within 500 ms`) then failed two full runs out of three — **502 ms** and **521 ms** against the 500 ms bound — having passed the first. That test measures watcher-to-view latency, a second watcher over the same folders adds per-event routing to every file change in the window, and it was the only mechanism in this change that could plausibly touch it; two of three is too often to write off as the marginal bound the test's own comment already documents ("a 501 ms flake at full-suite load was pure poll granularity"). So the detection moved into the `**/*` watcher that already sees every event — one string test on a path it was normalizing anyway, and the check runs *before* target resolution because a repository that is not open yet has no target to resolve to. That watcher is now the only one, and P7-T2 passed the full run afterwards plus three isolated runs. Honest about the strength of that: isolation cannot reproduce suite load, so the fix rests on one clean full run against two dirty ones, and the consolidation is the better design regardless of which way the causation ran. Gates: typecheck+lint clean, **772 unit (+16) · 202 webview (unchanged, 3 skipped) · 260 integration passing + 2 pending, zero failing**. Totals reconcile across all four full runs: 257 before this change, +4 tests present in run 1 = 261, +1 more from run 2 onward = 262 = 260 + 2. Run 1's one failure was P13-T3 `added-file copies are deleted only when the box is ticked`, which passed in every later run; each of the two tests that ever failed passed on the run the other failed, and neither is in the discovery path.

POST-P14g ✅ 2026-08-29 — **Blame annotations on every line, banded by commit.** Raised from a side-by-side screenshot: our gutter left most lines blank where IntelliJ labels all of them. Two defects, one visible. **(a) Rendering:** the column padded with U+0020, but `before.contentText` lands in a CSS `content:` on a `::before` whose `.view-lines` ancestor sets `white-space: nowrap` — so runs of spaces collapsed and the fixed-width column fell apart (uncommitted rows short, continuation rows zero-width). Padding now renders through U+00A0 (`toDecorationText`), and the pure label helpers moved to `blame/blameLabel.ts` so they are testable without a `vscode` host. **(b) Fidelity:** P4-T7 step 2 specified run-collapsing and called it "the IntelliJ look" — it is not; IntelliJ repeats the annotation and separates commits by background banding. The code was a faithful build of a wrong sentence, and it was in no deviations.md entry, so by our own rule it was a gap. Every line is now labelled and commit groups alternate between two contributed colours. Both the band and the column rule ride in each range's own `renderOptions`, which `$trySetDecorations` forwards verbatim — the decoration *type* path drops anything outside a fixed whitelist, which is why the old `borderColor` never drew. Costs nothing: the loop already emitted one decoration per line, and the editor de-duplicates render options structurally, so lines sharing a commit still share one CSS rule. `phase14` T5 now pins the whole contributed palette, not just the status colours.

POST-P14h ✅ 2026-08-29 — **Blame age moves to the cell background, IntelliJ's five-anchor ramp; the bands go.** Raised from a side-by-side screenshot: IntelliJ's annotation column shades each row by commit age, ours read as flat alternation. **The premise was half wrong and that mattered.** An age ramp was already there — `heatBucket` ranked commits over the file's own distinct commit times, which *is* IntelliJ's "Order" semantics — but it drove text alpha, and POST-P14g's per-commit bands then painted over it. **It was also inverted:** `alpha = 0.35 + (bucket / BUCKETS) * 0.6` with bucket 0 = *newest* made the newest commit the faintest, against a setting whose own description promised "newest brightest". Nothing caught it because no test asserted the direction and the effect was subtle under the bands. **Now:** one decoration type carries the static half (foreground, margin); each range carries the label plus a background from a five-step ramp of contributed colours (`gittree.blame.age1Background` … `age5Background`, newest strongest) or `uncommittedBackground`. The text fade is gone — two ramps stacked leave the oldest lines unreadable, which is why IntelliJ only shades the cell. The bands are gone too: they were an unregistered invention, and the register's own rule makes anything unlisted a gap. **The five steps are a deviation and are now registered as D25** — interpolating IntelliJ's gradient needs the anchors' resolved RGB, and `ThemeColor` is opaque to an extension. **Costs nothing:** verified in the shipped renderer that `setDecorationsByType` keys sub-types on `hash(renderOptions)`, which already included `contentText`; both varying fields are commit-derived, so the CSS-rule count went from ~2× the file's commits to ~1×. The manual `onDidChangeActiveColorTheme` rebuild went with it — the renderer flags rules built from a `ThemeColor` and rebuilds them itself. New `blameAnnotate5k` benchmark guards the decoration path, which nothing measured before.
