Testing: **+6 unit** on `fileRowContext` (clicked row alone, click inside the selection, click outside it, the all-or-nothing `fileUnversioned` gate, the repository boundary, and a directory row carrying nothing) and **+1 webview** driving the real round trip — Ctrl-click two rows, read the attribute, then right-click a third and watch both the payload and `aria-selected` move.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean, **30 unit** in `commitTree`, **1 webview** (`POST-P14v`), **6 integration passing / 0 failing** in `P1 core SCM` — the suite that drives `gittree.rollbackFile` through the very `{repoRoot, relPath}` context shape this change widened.

POST-P14w ✅ 2026-09-21 — **`Add to .gitignore` in the commit window's context menu.** Owner request; IntelliJ's own action on the Unversioned Files rows, and the reason half of them are there.

**The rule is "nearest, anchored, relative to itself"**, and each third of that is load-bearing. *Nearest*: the pattern goes into the closest existing `.gitignore` at or above the file, so a monorepo that keeps one per package gets the rule in that package rather than in a root file the whole tree reads; with none anywhere, the repository root's is created. *Anchored*: a bare `build.log` also hides `vendor/build.log`, which is a rule nobody asked for and nobody notices until something goes missing — every pattern starts `/`. *Relative to itself*: a package's `.gitignore` saying `/packages/api/gen.ts` matches nothing at all, so the path is re-expressed against the directory the file it lands in. A multi-select spanning two such subtrees writes both files, which is what makes the nearest-file rule usable on a selection rather than only on one row.

Glob metacharacters in a name are escaped (`notes[1].md` → `/notes[1].md`): unescaped, it is a character class that matches `notes1.md` and misses the file it was written for. A trailing space is escaped for the same reason in reverse — git strips it. `#` and `!` need nothing, because the `/` has already displaced them out of column 1. Duplicate suppression is **literal only**: whether some other rule already covers a path is `git check-ignore`'s question, and a path that reaches this command came out of Unversioned Files, so by construction nothing covers it.

Writing is a plain file edit, not a git call — `.gitignore` is ordinary content — followed by an explicit `refreshNow()`. The workspace watcher would get there on its own, debounced; the rows vanishing as the menu closes is what makes the action legible. The menu entry rides the `fileUnversioned` gate POST-P14v added, and the command re-filters on `kind === 'untracked'` because ignoring a tracked file changes nothing (the index wins) and would leave a rule that quietly does nothing forever.

Testing: **+14 unit** over the pure rules (anchoring, glob and trailing-space escaping, `#`/`!` left alone, CRLF preserved, a missing trailing newline added — without it the new rule joins the previous line and *both* are lost — literal-duplicate suppression, nearest-file choice including the trap where a directory shares the file's name, and a two-subtree split). **+1 integration** on the real repository: a package that already has an ignore file plus a root that does not, one command over both, then the rows asserted gone from the status model.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean, **14 unit** on `gitignoreRules`, **7 integration passing / 0 failing** in `P1 core SCM`. **One real defect caught by running the whole suite rather than the new test alone:** the first draft's cleanup deleted the ignore files and the package directory but not the untracked file at the root, and T7 — four tests later — timed out committing. Self-contained-per-test is not a style rule here; it is the thing `discovery.test.ts` records as the cause of cascading false failures.

POST-P14x ✅ 2026-09-21 — **Hovering a commit row shows its whole message.** Owner request; IntelliJ's own hover card, and the only way to read a body without spending the selection — the Details pane follows the selection, so reading one commit's message means losing the one you were comparing it against.

**The body is fetched lazily, on a new `log/commitMessage` RPC.** Putting it on `LogRowDto` would carry every body of a 100k-commit history that will never be shown, and `log/details` — which does return the body — pays for a `--name-status -M` rename detection on the way. `git log -1 --format=%B` is the whole cost. Answers are cached per sha for the life of the view, because the same rows are hovered over and over as the pointer travels down the list; the second hover of a row runs no git at all, which the test asserts by counting host calls rather than by timing.

**A card of our own, not a `title` attribute.** A `title` written *after* the browser has already started its own hover timer is not read again, which is exactly the shape of a lazily-fetched tooltip; and `title` collapses the message's line breaks in some renderers, which turns a bulleted body into a paragraph. The card is `pointer-events: none` — it follows the pointer around and must never become the thing under it — and flips above the row rather than being clamped to the viewport's bottom edge, which would put it over the row it describes.

**The handler had to move onto the row, and that is a `VirtualList` change.** It was first hung on the `.gt-log-row` span, and nothing fired: that span is `inline-flex` with `max-width: 100%`, so on a short subject it occupies the left third of the row and the pointer spends most of its time to the right of it — including the exact centre, which is where a test's `hover()` lands. `onRowMouseEnter` on the row element is the fix, and the same reasoning applies to any consumer: a row's content is usually narrower than its row. Leaving is handled once at the pane rather than per row, because the windowing recycles rows and a scroll that swaps the row under a stationary pointer fires no `mouseleave` on the row that left; `mousedown` cancels too, so a click never leaves a card sitting over the rows it was aimed at.

Testing: **+1 unit** on `getCommitMessage` (whole body returned, `%B`'s trailing padding trimmed — unhandled it puts a blank line under every message — and an unknown rev answering `undefined`). **+1 webview** over the real round trip: nothing on arrival, the body on dwell, a second row fetched, the first row re-hovered *without* a second host call, and the card gone on click.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean, **6 unit** in `logService`, **54 + 60 webview passing / 0 failing** (`log.spec`, `commit.spec`, `demo.spec` — the last two because `VirtualList` is shared). **A flake worth recording, and it is not the product:** run in parallel on a loaded machine, `log.spec` failed 1–4 *different* tests per run with `net::ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES` on `page.goto`, and a three-file run took V8 down with an OOM stack. 44 stranded Chrome processes (~5 GB) from the crashed run were the cause. `--workers=1` is green and deterministic; prefer it here over chasing a phantom regression.

POST-P14y ✅ 2026-09-21 — **The log opens in date order.** Owner request; IntelliJ's default, and GitTree had shipped topological order since P4-T1 without anyone choosing it — `DEFAULT_FILTER` was written next to the graph layout, where topo is the natural thing to think about.

Topological order groups a branch's commits together wherever in time they were made. That reads well for archaeology and badly for *what happened this week*, which is the question the log is opened for. All three sort modes stay in the dropdown, and nothing else changed: `sort` reorders the stream, it never shortens it, so `isUnfiltered` and every cache key are untouched.

Two places had to agree, not one. The webview carries its own fallback filter for the frame before the host's `log/init` answers, and a fallback that disagreed with `DEFAULT_FILTER` would label the view with an order it is not in — the bar renders `filter.sort` directly.

Testing: **1 unit changed** — the default-filter argv assertion now expects `--date-order`, which is the change's own proof: it read `--topo-order` until this commit. **+1 unit** pinning `DEFAULT_FILTER.sort` itself, because the dropdown and the argv are two readers of one value. **1 integration changed**, `P4`'s boot assertion on `session.filter.sort`.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (all three tsconfigs) + lint clean, **19 unit** in `logFilter`, **18 integration passing / 0 failing** across `P1 core SCM` and `P4 log & blame host`. **Narrow the grep too far and `P4`'s `commit actions` test fails on its own** with `unknown revision HEAD~1`: it reads `HEAD~1` in repoA, which only has a second commit because `P1` ran first. Reproduced identically on the unmodified tree. The two suites have to be grepped together — worth knowing before it is mistaken for a regression.

POST-P14z ✅ 2026-09-21 — **The commit row shows the exact date and time, not an age.** Owner request; IntelliJ's Date column.

`10d` is compact and answers a question nobody asked. Two rows both reading `2mo` can be six weeks apart, and *was this before or after the release* — the reason the column is read at all — cannot be answered from an age at any precision. The old ramp also degraded to a bare `toLocaleDateString()` past 60 days, so the row lost its clock time exactly where a date was least ambiguous and gained nothing.

Date and time are two `Intl.DateTimeFormat`s joined with a space rather than one combined format, whose locale separator (`, ` in en-US) buys nothing in a dense row. Both are built **once per formatter**, not per row: this runs for every visible row on every repaint, and constructing an `Intl` formatter is not free. Locale and zone are the host's — a date order is a regional convention, and 09/21 against 21/09 is not a detail. The screen-reader label moved with the visible text; it was reading the age too.

Testing: **+5 unit** over the formatter with locale and zone pinned (en-GB/en-US/de-DE field order, a zone shift, a `+14` zone that puts the same instant on the *next day* — the thing an age hides completely, no locale comma, and stability). **+1 webview** on the real row, locale-tolerant by construction: it requires a calendar date **and** a clock time in both the text and the `aria-label`, and rejects the `N(m|h|d) ·` shape it replaced.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean, **5 unit** on `dateFormat`, **55 webview passing / 0 failing** in `log.spec` at `--workers=1` (see POST-P14x for why that flag, and why a parallel run here proves nothing about the product).

POST-P14v (addendum) ✅ 2026-09-21 — **`gittree.addToVcs` gets the end-to-end test it shipped without.** The original commit tested the *payload* the menu hands over and left the command itself covered only by inspection.

**+1 integration** in `P3 commit lifecycle`, over a two-file selection including a nested path, with an **active list that is not the default** — without that distinction the test cannot tell reconcile's adoption rule (active) from IntelliJ's (default), which is the one thing the command adds over doing nothing. It was **watched to fail**: replacing `service.moveFiles(targets, DEFAULT_LIST_ID)` with a no-op turns it red on `v-one.txt not in the default list`, then green again restored.

**Two things the assertions had to be taught about `add -N`.** `git diff --cached` reports *nothing* for an intent-to-add entry — git hides them there unless asked with `--ita-visible-in-index` — so the first draft failed against a working command; `git ls-files` asks the index directly and is the honest question. And "no content was staged" is checked as `git cat-file -p :v-one.txt` being empty rather than by a blob hash, which would have pinned the test to SHA-1 repositories. The unversioned assertion names its own two files rather than claiming the bucket is empty: earlier tests in the suite leave unversioned files in repoC that this command never touched.

Gates (scoped): typecheck + lint clean, **11 integration passing / 0 failing** in `P3 commit lifecycle`.

POST-P14aa ✅ 2026-09-23 — **The change popup no longer opens on hover unless asked to.** Owner request: hovering a changed line in the editor popped GitTree's previous-text-and-actions widget, and they wanted it gone. Of the three options put to them — delete it, keep it behind a setting defaulting off, or shrink it to a label — the owner chose the setting: **`gittree.changeBars.hoverActions`, default `false`**.

**Why a setting and not a deletion.** Removing the hover is *more* faithful to IntelliJ, where hovering a gutter marker shows nothing and a click opens the popup. But GitTree cannot offer the click — a `TextEditorDecorationType` has no click handler and no API opens a diff peek at a location (**D19**) — so the hover was the only **mouse** route to per-hunk Rollback and Stage. Off by default gives the owner IntelliJ's quiet editor; the setting keeps the route one click away for anyone who wants it. `Ctrl+Alt+G H` (`gittree.change.showActions`) is untouched and always on, and the setting's description names it, so turning the hover off never strands the actions.

**Off means structurally off.** The provider is registered and disposed by a configuration listener rather than consulted inside `provideHover`, so with the setting off VS Code never calls GitTree on a hover at all — not a call per hover that answers nothing. The same listener makes the toggle live, no reload. The `gittree.change.*` commands the hover's links dispatch to stay registered either way; they cost nothing idle.

**The regression test was the old test, inverted.** `P14`'s *the hover fires on a changed line* asserted exactly the behaviour being reversed. It now asserts silence by default on the very line that used to answer, then that turning the setting on brings the popup back on that line and **not** on an unchanged one, then that turning it off again takes effect live. Watched both ways: against the previous `changePopup.ts` it fails on `the hover must be off by default`; against this one it passes. It polls `vscode.executeHoverProvider` — the observable contract — rather than the setting's value, because the listener registers the provider some time *after* the new value is readable, and a poll on the value would pass or fail by luck. The setting is reset in `suiteSetup` as well as teardown: the test VS Code keeps one user-data-dir across runs, and a `true` leaked by an interrupted run would fail the default assertion on a product behaving correctly — the same trap `resetCutoff` already guards for `maxFileLines`.

**Docs moved with it.** D19 is renamed *keyboard-invoked; hover is opt-in* and now records why the hover was kept rather than deleted (its anchor updated in the index table — the only reference). `FEATURES.md`, `parity-audit.md` and `docs/settings.md` (regenerated) follow. The `[Unreleased]` changelog bullet was corrected in place rather than given a "Changed" entry of its own: the on-by-default hover only ever existed in unreleased builds, so no release notes should announce taking it away. The description is plain `description`, not `markdownDescription`: no setting in the manifest uses the latter and `genSettingsDoc` does not read it — the first draft rendered as `undefined` in the generated doc, which `settingsDoc.test` caught.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean, **5 unit** docs gates, **17 integration passing / 1 failing** in `P14 owning the SCM surface`. **The failure is not this change:** `T1: every view lives in the GitTree container` reports an unexpected `gittree.branchesTree` in the container's view list, and fails identically with this change stashed. It has been red since `87798ad` (*branches sidebar: a webview, for the two search fields a tree cannot hold*) added that view without updating the assertion's expected list — left for the owner, since whether `branchesTree` belongs in the list or the list belongs to the view is their call.

POST-P14ab ✅ 2026-09-23 — **Update Project asks in a native modal, not an editor tab.** The owner hit Update on a branch row and got "a weird page": the P13-T2 dialog, a webview panel in the editor area, for what is one two-way choice. Asked whether GitTree could follow IntelliJ strictly, the answer was *the behaviour yes, the container no* — VS Code gives an extension no modal surface that can hold a radio button (D2) — and of the three containers offered (native modal, keep the tab, a top-of-window popup) the owner chose the **native modal**.

**Shape.** A modal `showInformationMessage` titled *Update Project*: the detail line names what is updated — `develop ← origin/develop · 3 behind · 0 ahead`, which the old dialog's title never said — then, with `gittree.dialogs.showCommandLine` on, each button's exact commands from the same `buildUpdatePlan` the host runs (off, IntelliJ's own wording for the two options). Buttons `Merge` / `Rebase` / `Reset to the Remote Branch` plus VS Code's `Cancel`; pressing one *is* the OK. The question is pure data first — `buildUpdateQuestion` in the vscode-free `updateArgs.ts` — so its order is unit-tested rather than eyeballed.

**Two IntelliJ settings that GitTree had folded into one are now two.** IntelliJ saves the Merge/Rebase radio on *every* OK and keeps `Don't show again` separately; GitTree only remembered a strategy when "Do not show again" was ticked, so an update that asks nothing could not know what the user last chose. The answer is now kept in workspace *state* (`gittree.update.lastStrategy`), and the remembered strategy is the **first** button, so `Enter` repeats it as IntelliJ's pre-selected radio does. State, not a setting, on purpose: it changes on every answer, and writing a setting that often would keep rewriting a `.vscode/settings.json` many repositories track — IntelliJ keeps it in `workspace.xml` for the same reason. A `Reset` answer is never remembered, and `Reset` is always the last button, where `Enter` cannot reach it.

**`Don't show again` moved onto the result.** A message box cannot hold a checkbox, so the opt-out is a `Don't Ask Again` button on the update's result notification — the one place the user is looking right after answering — which writes `gittree.update.strategy` exactly as the checkbox did, then says how to undo it (`GitTree: Update Project Strategy…`, unchanged). IntelliJ shows `Reset to the Remote Branch` only in some states — present in one reference screenshot, absent from the owner's latest — and its rule is not known here, so GitTree keeps offering it always, behind its own confirmation and a Local History snapshot as before. Recorded as **D30**.

**Deleted, not left behind:** the `UpdateProject` webview component and its router case, `UpdateDialogParamsDto`/`UpdateDialogResultDto`, the `.gt-update-*` CSS (`.gt-radio` stays — Reset and New Worktree use it), `updateProject.spec.ts` and its mock host. `dialogHost`'s header no longer lists Update Project among its dialogs. The command id `gittree.update.dialog` is kept, so `Ctrl+T`, the popup verb, the Operations Popup and every menu still reach it. Docs: D30 added to the register **and D29's index row, which had been missing since POST-P14u**; the `[Unreleased]` changelog, `readme.md` and `FEATURES.md` corrected in place from "twelve dialogs" to eleven plus this question — the webview version never shipped, so no release notes should announce replacing it; `keyboard.md` and the setting's description follow; `docs/settings.md` regenerated.

Testing: **+3 unit** on `buildUpdateQuestion` (remembered strategy first, `Reset` never first and always last, each button's commands identical to `buildUpdatePlan`'s). **P13 T2 reworked, +2 integration**, all driven through `GITTREE_TEST_UPDATE_CHOICE` — the native modal cannot be clicked, so the seam answers it, and the prompt is recorded either way so a test reads exactly what a user would have been asked: the question names branch and upstream and offers Merge first; an answer runs, is remembered while the question is still asked, and becomes the first button (then Merge is restored by answering, so later suites start from IntelliJ's default); `Cancel` runs nothing, not even the fetch, and remembers nothing; the setting bypasses the question entirely — asserted with `cancel` as the answer, so a regression fails fast instead of hanging on a modal nobody can answer; and the `Reset` button lands on the destructive confirmation and is never remembered.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (all three tsconfigs) + lint clean, **13 unit** in `updateArgs` plus the docs, `t4Args` and `operationsModel` gates that enumerate `gittree.update.dialog` (**85** total), **43 integration passing / 0 failing** in `P13 dialogs`, **27 webview** (`dialogs.spec`, `opDialogs.spec`, single worker) over the router the case was removed from. **Grep narrowly and T2 fails in `before all`**: it clones a branch that P13's own push tests create first — run `P13 dialogs` whole.

POST-P14ac ✅ 2026-09-23 — **Update Project sets local changes aside instead of refusing.** The precondition the owner's "skip the dialog on a branch row's Update" rests on: IntelliJ can update without asking because its update shelves (or stashes) uncommitted work, integrates, and puts it back. GitTree's ran a bare `git merge` / `git rebase`, and a scratch repository measured what that costs: **`git rebase` refuses on any uncommitted change at all**, and `git merge` refuses whenever an incoming commit touches a file you edited. Nothing is ever lost — git refuses before touching anything — but a one-click update would have failed most of the time a developer is mid-task.

**The fix is git's own `--autostash`, and three measurements shaped where it may be used.** (1) **It breaks on intent-to-add entries.** `git stash` cannot save a `git add -N` entry ("Cannot save the current worktree state"), and `Add to VCS` (POST-P14v) and the changelist commit path both create them — worse, a *plain* `git merge` handles them fine, so a naive `--autostash` would have been a regression. They are taken out of the index around the step (`rm --cached`, which leaves the file on disk) and put back with `add -N`; a same-path collision with an incoming commit is refused by git with the file intact. (2) **It flattens the index**: `MM` comes back as ` M` — content kept, staging undone. In the changelists paradigm the index holds nothing else, so nothing is lost; with real staged content (the staging paradigm, or a terminal `git add`) `--autostash` is **not** passed, and git proceeds with the index intact or refuses in its own words. (3) **A conflicting restore exits 0.** Both `merge --autostash` and `rebase --autostash` report success with `UU` files and the stash kept, so a result that trusted the exit code announced "Merged". The run now counts unmerged files after the step, returns them as `restoreConflicts`, and the notification becomes a **warning** saying a copy is in the stash.

**Two things kept honest along the way.** GitTree's conflict lifecycle already opens a session for op-less conflicts, but would have filed this one as `checkout-merge`. It is told `stash-pop` — and told **only after** the conflict is seen, inside the queued operation, the way the stash service does it: a hint registered in advance would outlive a clean update and mislabel the repository's next, unrelated conflict (the first draft did exactly that). And when a rebase stops on its own conflict, the detached intent-to-add files are **not** put back — the autostash still has to be re-applied when the user finishes, and git stash cannot stand them — so they stay untracked in their changelist and the failure message says so on the one line the notification shows. `git merge --autostash` needs 2.27, now `caps.mergeAutostash`; the rebase half is far older. The question's command lines show `--autostash` exactly when the run would pass it. Recorded as **D31** — IntelliJ's default saver is the shelf; git's stash was chosen because it is the one that survives the integrate step stopping.

Testing: **+10 unit** on the pure rules (`planSetAside`'s matrix — edited, intent-to-add, staged, half-staged, the 2.27 gate — the plan wrapping only the integrate step, `reset` never setting anything aside, and the question per strategy) and **+1** on the capability. **+5 integration** on the real repository and remote, each **watched to fail** against a plausible wrong implementation: against the old behaviour (nothing set aside) the edit-over-rebase, conflicting-restore and stopped-rebase tests fail; against a naive always-`--autostash` the added-file test fails with git's own "Entry … not uptodate. Cannot merge", the staged test fails on "the staged change must still be staged", and the stopped-rebase test fails again. Both patches were restored byte-for-byte.

**A pre-existing race the new tests exposed, not fixed here.** `StatusRefresher.refreshNow()`, called while a refresh is already running, flags one more pass and **returns immediately** — so `await refreshNow()` can resolve before any read that post-dates the call, and a refresh that began mid-rebase landed *after* the test's cleanup and repainted a detached HEAD; the next suite then read `currentBranch === 'HEAD'`. Measured by logging the model across the cleanup. The test now waits for three settled reads in a row; the refresher's contract is worth its own task, since other call sites `await refreshNow()` and then read the model.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (all three tsconfigs) + lint clean, **23 unit** in `updateArgs` + **9** capabilities + the docs gates, **119 unit** across `vitest related` for the three changed sources, **48 integration passing / 0 failing** in `P13 dialogs`.

POST-P14ad ✅ 2026-09-23 — **`Update` on the checked-out branch updates without asking.** The owner's report: IntelliJ's two Updates behave differently — `Update Project…` asks Merge or Rebase, a branch row's `Update` just runs — and GitTree's branch `Update` opened the question. It now delegates to the half of Update Project that **does not ask**, `runRememberedUpdate`, behind an internal `gittree.update.run` command (registered, not contributed: the palette verb stays `Update Project…`). One correction to the report, from the tooltip in the owner's own screenshot — *"update selected branch using merge or fast-forward"*: IntelliJ's branch Update is not always a merge. On the checked-out branch it uses the saved update method, and on any other branch it fast-forwards only, which GitTree already did.

**Which strategy is "the saved one" is why POST-P14ab came first.** It is `gittree.update.strategy` when that names one (Don't Ask Again), else the last answer to the question (workspace state), else Merge. The branch row asks nothing, so it has to know an answer given elsewhere — which GitTree could not before, having kept the answer only when "Do not show again" was ticked. And it is safe to run without a question only because POST-P14ac sets uncommitted work aside: without that, a one-click rebase failed whenever you had uncommitted changes.

**The all-repositories fan-out now stops where Update fails.** `runCommonAction` stops at the first failure — but only thrown ones, and `Update` (like other branch ops) reports its own failure and returns, so `Update in all N repositories` ran on past a conflicted root; before this commit it also opened the question once per repository, and Cancel in one did not stop the rest. `BranchAction.run` may now resolve `false` — "did not complete, already reported" — which the fan-out treats as a stop ("Stopped at X. Already done in: …"), while the four single-row callers (register, popup submenu, sidebar webview, log pane bridge) ignore it unchanged. A new exception type would have needed all four to catch it, or produced a second report from each. `updateBranch` resolves `true` only when the branch ends level with its upstream (updated or already up to date).

Testing: **+3 integration**, each **watched to fail** against the old behaviour (the row routed back to the question, and the fan-out ignoring `false`): the checked-out branch's `Update` moves it to its upstream and **records no question** — run with `GITTREE_TEST_UPDATE_CHOICE=cancel` as a hang guard, so a regression fails instead of waiting on a modal; it uses the **last answer** while the setting is still `ask` (answered Rebase with nothing incoming, then the upstream moves on under local work, and the history must be rebased — single parent — not merged; Merge is answered again afterwards so later suites start clean); and a fan-out stops at the first root whose action resolves `false` (a fake action over repoD/repoE — the seam `runCommonAction` already exposed). **The first draft of the second test proved nothing**: it set the *setting* to `rebase`, which the old path honoured too, and it passed against the old code. Rewritten to exercise the answer the setting does not hold.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean, docs gates, **28 integration passing / 0 failing** across `P14 branch menu parity` and `POST-P14p project-level branches popup`, **22** across `P2 branches` and `P13 branches hub` (the other dispatchers of the widened `run`). **Pre-existing and not this change:** `branchMenu.test.ts` → *both branch trees share every row* fails on a `gittree.branchesTree` clause, identically with this change stashed — the same `87798ad` gap as `P14` T1's view-set assertion (POST-P14aa).

POST-P14ae ✅ 2026-09-23 — **Update Project updates the project.** Found while answering the owner's Update question, and agreed with the rest of that plan: IntelliJ's Update Project updates every repository in the project; GitTree's `gittree.update.dialog` with nothing named resolved "the active editor's repository, else the only one" — in a multi-root workspace it updated one repository, or, with several repositories and no editor open, **nothing at all** beyond "Open a repository to update." The branches popup even carried a comment promising the command "asks which one it means"; it never asked.

**Shape.** With nothing named — `Ctrl+T`, the popup's project-level verb, the Operations Popup — the scope is every repository (`updateScope`). One question covers them all: a line per repository naming its branch, upstream and counts, then any being skipped and why. Across several, the buttons are `Merge` / `Rebase` only — one button hard-resetting every repository, each behind a confirmation the user would learn to click through, is not a question IntelliJ's single-repository dialog ever asked — and one sentence says what each repository runs, instead of two command lines per repository. They then update **in order and the run stops at the first failure**, the idiom `runCommitAcross` and `runCommonAction` share, with a single notification naming where it stopped, what was already updated and what was never attempted. A repository it cannot update — detached, no upstream, a merge or rebase in progress — is **skipped, not attempted** (`runCommonAction`'s rule), so one checkout mid-rebase cannot stop the other six. One repository in scope takes exactly the single-repository flow (exact commands, `Reset`, `Show in Log`), so a single-root workspace sees no change. A **named** repository — a Repositories row, which passes its root — still updates just that one; the command now distinguishes "named" (`namedRepo`) from "guessed", and no longer guesses.

**Two smaller defects on the same path.** The Operations Popup passes its own repository to every command it dispatches, which would have scoped Update Project back down to one repository; the dialog registry's entry now carries `args: []`, the idiom Fetch already used there (POST-P14o), and `buildOperationsItems` passes registry `args` through. And `Show in Log` after an update filtered **whichever repository the log happened to show**, where in a multi-root workspace neither end of the fetched range need exist: `gittree.log.setFilter` takes an optional root now (`logView.setFilter` always did), every update result passes its repository, and a project run offers `Show in Log` only when exactly one repository received commits — the log shows one repository at a time (D24). D27 amended: Update Project leaves its "still per repository" list, Push stays on it.

Testing: **+2 unit** (`updateChoices` never offers `Reset` across several repositories; the Operations Popup dispatches Update Project with an explicit empty argument list — **watched to fail** without the registry's `args: []`). **+3 integration**, all three **watched to fail** against the old behaviour (the command's old no-argument handling, a run that carries on past a failure, and no-upstream repositories attempted rather than skipped), patches restored byte-for-byte: `gittree.update.dialog` with nothing named asks **once**, and every workspace repository appears in that question — answered Cancel, so it reads the question and mutates nothing, with every HEAD asserted unmoved; one answer updates repoC and repoD (given its own bare remote and an incoming commit) and skips repoE by name, with no `Reset` offered; and a run whose first repository cannot fetch stops there, leaving repoD, one commit behind, exactly where it was. Two defects in the first draft of the fixture, both caught by running it: deleting the throwaway clone failed EPERM on Windows' read-only pack files (a known trap — the suite teardown already removes its temp dir best-effort), and because that threw, the rest of the cleanup never ran and the next test's push became a non-fast-forward. Each call now gets its own remote directory and every restore step runs regardless.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean, **203 unit** across `test/unit/scm`, `operationsModel` and the docs gates, **51 integration passing / 0 failing** in `P13 dialogs`.

POST-P14af ✅ 2026-09-24 — **A rejected push asks Merge or Rebase, then pushes again.** Item 2 of the "what next" list, specified from five screenshots of the owner's IntelliJ: *Settings › Version Control › Git* (*Auto-update if push of the current branch was rejected* off, *Update method* Merge), the *Push Rejected* dialog, the notification after Cancel, the one after an update that hit conflicts, and the one after a push that went through. IntelliJ answers a push the remote refused because it has commits the branch does not with *Push of the current branch "X" was rejected. Remote changes need to be merged before pushing.*, `☐ Remember the update method and update silently in the future` and `[Merge] [Rebase] [Cancel]` — then updates and pushes again. GitTree stopped at an error: `gittree.push` raised P10-T4's "Pull (or fetch and rebase) before pushing", and the Push dialog raised "Push failed: To <url>", the first line of git's stderr, which for a push names the remote and not the problem.

**Shape.** One runner (`pushRun.ts`) behind every push of a tracked branch: the Push dialog's accept half, `gittree.push` (so Commit and Push, and Sync) and a branch row's `Push…`, the last two through a new internal `gittree.push.run`. It runs every push first, then answers the rejected ones with one question — a warning message box, D30's form (D32) — then Update Project's own update (`applyUpdateResult`, autostash and all), then the same push again, stopping at the first repository that does not go through. When to ask is IntelliJ's rule (`canUpdateAndRetry`): the checked-out branch, pushed to the branch it tracks, refused only because the remote moved on — `[rejected] … (fetch first | non-fast-forward)`, read from git's ref status lines, which git does not translate — and neither forced nor over an operation in progress. A stale lease, a hook or protected-branch refusal, a push to another branch or remote, a branch that is not checked out: reported, never updated. The answer is Update Project's remembered strategy, as IntelliJ keeps one update method, so either question's answer is the other's default. `Remember…` is `Don't Ask Again` on the result, writing the new `gittree.push.autoUpdateIfRejected` (default off, as in the screenshot). Over unpushed merge commits of the user's own (`rev-list --merges <tracking>..<branch>`), IntelliJ's warning: Merge first, `Rebase Anyway`, no `Don't Ask Again` — and asked even with auto-update on while Rebase is the remembered answer, since a silent rebase would flatten them. A remote that moves again mid-round gets another round with the same answer, at most three (`MAX_PUSH_ATTEMPTS`).

**Reporting.** One notification per push, in IntelliJ's words: *Pushed N commits to origin/X* (plus *Merged / Rebased onto N incoming commits first*), *Pushed X to new branch origin/X*, *Push to origin/X was rejected* (with the remote's reason when it is not "the remote moved on"), and *Push was canceled, because there were conflicts during the update…* — `UpdateOutcome.conflicts` is new: the integrate step stopped part-way (a `merge-conflict` error, or an operation still on disk) as opposed to one git refused to start. "Show details in console" became `Show Details`, opening the Console tab through a new `LOG_METHODS.evtShowTab`, with `LogInitDto.tab` for a view the focus is only now creating (it would miss the event). "View files updated during the push" is `Show in Log` (D9). A plain push used to report nothing (`gittree.push`) or "Push: main → origin/main: pushed"; a single classified failure (authentication, a lock) still gets the P10-T4 dialog. Found on the way: a branch row's `Push…` always pushed to `origin`, whatever remote its upstream is on; it now uses that remote. And because a tracked branch's failed push is now reported rather than thrown, the row resolves `false` when its push did not go through — `runCommonAction` stops only on a throw or a `false`, so `Push` across all repositories would otherwise have carried on past a rejected one.

**Not done, pending the owner:** IntelliJ's *Update all repositories* (projects with several repositories). The owner could not capture that dialog; only the rejected repositories are updated, and D32 records the open question.

Testing: **+24 unit** (`pushRejection.test.ts`: git 2.28's real `fetch first` output with its 8.3 temp path, `non-fast-forward` with CRLF line ends, the porcelain form, `stale info`, a protected-branch hook, refs rejected for different reasons, a `remote:` line quoting a status, failures that are not rejections; the seven cases the retry rule refuses; button order; `pushFailureLine`). **+2 webview** (`evtShowTab` opens the Console and ignores a tab the strip does not have; `init.tab` opens a starting view on the Console), both **watched to fail** with the handling removed. **+7 integration** in `P13 dialogs`, against the suite's local bare remote with a second clone as the teammate: Merge (the question's sentence, buttons and command line; a two-parent merge that reached the remote), Rebase (mine on top of theirs, pushed; the answer becomes Update Project's default), Cancel (nothing pushed, not even a fetch; last answer first), auto-update through `gittree.push` (no question; the remembered Rebase ran), unpushed merge commits (asked despite auto-update: `Merge` / `Rebase Anyway`), conflicts (nothing pushed, `MERGE_HEAD` left for the user), and a push to another branch (reported, never asked). Six **watched to fail** with the answering switched off; the seventh, which asserts that *no* question is asked, **watched to fail** with `canUpdateAndRetry` blind to "to the branch it tracks". **+1 integration** in `P14 branch menu parity`: a branch row's `Push…` on a branch tracking `p14bmremote` pushes there and carries on, then, rejected, resolves `false` without asking — **watched to fail** twice, against the old `origin` push (`'origin' does not appear to be a git repository`) and against the old action that never said `false`. Every patch restored byte-for-byte and rebuilt.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; `vitest related` **925 passing, 1 failing** — the known stale `both branch trees share every row` (87798ad), unrelated; webview `log.spec.ts` **57/57**; integration `P13 dialogs` + P1 sync **59 passing / 0 failing**, `P14 branch menu parity` **11/11** (in one of the two proof runs its first test, `the tracked bit appears and disappears`, timed out waiting for a refresh before any patched code ran — the `refreshNow()` coalescing flake already on the list). The log suites (`P4 log & blame host`, `P12 git tool window`, `Log roots & path filter`) fail 3 filtered together and 1 with P4 alone — **the same on the untouched baseline** (stashed, rebuilt, run), so filtered-run ordering: `Log roots` pins another repository before P4 runs, and P4 alone lacks a commit that earlier suites make.

POST-P14ag ✅ 2026-09-24 — **Update all repositories.** The question POST-P14af left open, answered by the owner with the recommended option: IntelliJ's *Push Rejected* dialog has, in a project with several repositories, an *Update all repositories* checkbox — ticked, as far as is known here, when IntelliJ first shows it (the owner could not capture that dialog). A message box cannot hold it, so it is a setting: `gittree.push.updateAllRepositoriesIfRejected`, **on by default**, the owner's decision.

**Shape.** With it on, a Merge or Rebase answer updates the rejected repositories **and every other repository Update Project could update** — `updateScope`'s targets, so one detached, tracking nothing or mid-operation is left out rather than attempted. The question names them (*Also updates …*), where IntelliJ shows the ticked box, and the merge-commit check covers them too (`@{upstream}..HEAD`). IntelliJ's order: **update everything, then push again**, and nothing is pushed again unless every update went through. `runPushes` now runs the updates in workspace order and stops at the first failure, as Update Project does; a rejected repository whose own update failed is `update-failed`, as before; one whose update went through but the run stopped elsewhere keeps its rejection with a new `stoppedAt` (*Push to origin/X was rejected, and not pushed again: the update stopped at repoB.*); only then are the rejected ones pushed again, each with its own further rounds if the remote moves once more (`pushAgain`, which replaces `updateAndPush`). The run returns what the other repositories' updates did (`PushRun.alsoUpdated`), and the one notification names those that received commits or failed — not those already up to date, which would bury the push. `Show in Log` counts them among the repositories that received commits.

**A seam.** `PushRunOptions.project` narrows what *all* means. Production passes nothing (the workspace); the tests narrow it to repoC, repoD and repoE, because other suites leave the shared fixture repositories tracking remotes they have deleted — which is also why the POST-P14af suite turns the setting off for its single-repository tests and puts the default back after. Registry: `pushTracked(repo, project?)`, `setUpdateAllRepositoriesIfRejected`.

Testing: **+3 integration** in the POST-P14ae suite, which owns the multi-repository fixture (`withProject`: repoD with its own bare remote and an incoming commit, repoE tracking nothing): with the setting on (the default), a rejected push of repoC names repoD and not repoE in the question, brings repoD up to date, then merges and pushes repoC; with it off, repoD is left exactly where it was; and when repoD cannot fetch, repoC — updated first — is **not** pushed again, its result naming repoD as where the run stopped. **Watched to fail** three ways, each patch restored byte-for-byte and rebuilt: with the other repositories never updated (the behaviour before this: tests 1 and 3 fail), pushing again after the update stopped (test 3), and with the setting ignored (test 2). The POST-P14af push-helper functions moved up to the T2 suite so both suites share them.

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; `test/unit/docs` + `test/unit/scm` **202/202**; integration `P13 dialogs` **61/61**, and with `P14 branch menu parity` and P1 sync **72 passing, 1 failing** — P1 T5's own setup, `git remote add` hitting "could not write config file .git/config: Permission denied" (Windows lock contention, before any GitTree code ran); **passing** on its own re-run.

POST-P14ah ✅ 2026-09-24 — **Commit and Push… opens the Push dialog first.** Spotted in the owner's *Settings › Version Control › Git* screenshot (POST-P14af): *Show Push dialog for Commit and Push* is ticked — IntelliJ's default — so IntelliJ follows a commit-and-push with the Push dialog, for the repositories just committed, and nothing leaves the machine until Push is pressed. GitTree's `Commit and Push…` — the ellipsis already promising a dialog — pushed the moment each repository's commit landed (`postCommit` → `gittree.push`).

**Shape.** The push step leaves `postCommit` and runs once the commits are in: `runCommitFlow` hands its repository over, and `runCommitAcross` holds back each repository's push and hands over every repository it committed at once — so a commit spanning several opens **one** dialog, not one each (a run stopped part-way hands over what did land, which is what pushing straight after each commit used to do). A new internal `gittree.push.afterCommit` decides, in the push module: with `gittree.push.showDialogForCommitAndPush` (new, default on) it opens the Push dialog for exactly those repositories; off, it pushes them one at a time through `gittree.push`, as before — Publish for a branch that tracks nothing, the Push Rejected flow for one that does. **The dialog is not awaited.** It is an editor tab the user may leave open, and the commit window's `guard` keeps it busy — refusing the next commit — for as long as the commit call runs; awaiting the dialog there would have locked the commit window until the Push dialog closed.

**One race found in the writing, and closed.** `showPushDialog` is now called without being awaited, so its `GITTREE_TEST_PUSH_DIALOG=cancel` seam (new: records what the dialog was opened for, then closes it unanswered) must be read **before** its first await — a test restores the seam as soon as its command returns, and a seam read after `buildPushParams` would have found it gone and opened a real, never-answered dialog tab in the test window. Registry: `lastPushDialogRequest`, `clearLastPushDialogRequest`, `setShowPushDialogForCommitAndPush`.

**Not done:** IntelliJ's nested *Show Push dialog only when committing to protected branches*. It needs IntelliJ's *Protected branches* list, which GitTree does not have — and which in IntelliJ also governs force push, so it is its own piece of work (gap analysis 3.3, 🟡).

Testing: **+3 integration**. In `Multi-root commit` (repoD and repoE, no remotes): `gittree.commitAndPush` with nothing named — the commit window's button — commits both, opens the Push dialog **once** for exactly those two, and runs no `push` at all. In `P13 dialogs` (repoC, tracking the suite's bare remote), written for either commit paradigm since an earlier suite leaves `staging` on: with the default, the commit lands, the dialog is asked for repoC, and the remote is untouched; with the setting off, no dialog and the commit is on the remote. **Watched to fail** three ways, each patch restored byte-for-byte and rebuilt: a push step per repository as each commit lands (the multi-root test: the dialog named one repository), the old always-push (the default test), and a setting ignored the other way (the setting-off test).

Gates (scoped): typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; `vitest related` **925 passing, 1 failing** — the known stale `both branch trees share every row`; `test/unit/docs` **5/5**; integration `Multi-root commit` + `P13 dialogs` **72/72**; `P1 core SCM` + `P3 commit lifecycle` + `P11 commit tool window` + `P13 dialogs` **106 passing, 1 failing** — P13's `T2: the rebase strategy…` asserting a clean tree over `?? .gittemplate`, which P3's template test writes into repoC and never deletes; **identical on the untouched baseline** (stashed, rebuilt, run: 104 passing, the same failure), so debris from running P3 without the suites that normally sit between it and P13.

POST-P14ai ✅ 2026-10-31: **Presentation Settings · Tag Names, and Branches Pane Settings · Show Tags.** The owner asked from a log screenshot whether tags could be hidden. They could not. Every ref was a named chip, and the parity docs credited P12-T5 with a presentation menu ("compact refs, tag names…") and a highlight menu that it never shipped (its own PROGRESS entry lists only *Highlight my commits*, and no commit in history ever held either menu). IntelliJ, per its Log tab and Log settings docs, never hides tags in the commit table. **Tag Names** (eye menu › Show) chooses between the tag icon with the name and the icon alone, named on hover. **Show Tags** (Branches pane gear; also the Branches popup's gear) removes the Tags node.

**Shape.** The eye, `Presentation Settings`, sits between Refresh and Go to Hash/Branch/Tag, IntelliJ's order. Its menu has a `Show` caption (a new non-interactive `header` kind in the shared `Menu`, skipped by pointer and arrow keys) and one checkbox, **Tag Names**. Off, the default as in IntelliJ, `refLabels` gathers a commit's tags into one icon-only chip where the first tag stood. Hovering over it opens the existing hover card naming them, in place of the commit message, keyed `tags:<sha>` so a message fetch in flight cannot land over it. Leaving the chip restarts the row's own card. On, each tag is a chip with the tag icon and its name. The Branches pane rail gains the gear `Branches Pane Settings`, opening beside the rail, with **Show Tags**. The pane's tree building moved out to `branchesPaneModel.ts` (pure) to make that testable. The rest of both IntelliJ menus is not listed: no checkbox that changes nothing.

**Settings, not view state.** `gittree.log.showTagNames` (default false) and `gittree.branches.showTags` (default true) appear in the Settings editor, where IntelliJ's appear on its Version Control pages. A toggle posts `log/setPresentation`. The host writes the setting where it is already set, else globally for Tag Names (IntelliJ: IDE-wide) and per workspace for Show Tags (IntelliJ: per project), never to workspace settings without a workspace (`writeScope`). It answers with what the settings then say, so a failed write puts the checkbox back. Any change, from a menu or the Settings editor, reaches the view as `log/presentationChanged`, not `evtReset`, so the rows redraw without a `git log` and keep the selection. **Show Tags is one setting for every branch list**, as IntelliJ's is: `applyShowTags` empties the tag lists in the `TreeContext` that the popup and both sidebar trees build from. That drops `Tags`, `Common Tags` and tag search hits, since popupTree already skips empty sections. The two sidebar views refresh on the setting; the popup reads it each time it opens. The log's `Branch ▾` filter and Go to still offer tags.

**Docs corrected:** gap analysis §3.4 Presentation row (🟡 *Tag Names only*) and Highlight row (✅ → 🟡 *My Commits only*), parity audit §4, FEATURES §4, CHANGELOG, settings.md regenerated. The dated tallies (2026-08-23) are left as the snapshot they are; the Highlight correction moves one item from ✅ to 🟡 at the next re-score.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction): **+4 unit files**. `test/unit/log/presentation.test.ts` covers `writeScope` and `presentationPatch`, and checks that package.json declares both settings as booleans with the defaults the views assume. `test/unit/branches/popupTree.test.ts` checks that `applyShowTags` drops Tags, Common Tags, a repository's Tags and search hits, and leaves branches and its input alone. `test/unit/webview/refLabels.test.ts` covers the icon gathering and its order. `test/unit/webview/branchesPaneModel.test.ts` covers the Tags node and search with Show Tags off. **+5 Playwright** in `log.spec.ts` (mock row 7 now carries two tags; the mock answers `log/setPresentation`): the icon and its hover card; the eye menu toggling Tag Names, with the caption, the checkbox state and the posted request; the keyboard landing on Tag Names past the caption; a host `presentationChanged` redrawing without a `log/page` and keeping the selection; and the gear hiding and restoring the Tags node while `Branch ▾` still lists tags.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; webviews and the extension bundle built. No suite run.

POST-P14aj ✅ 2026-11-01: **More than one Log tab (IntelliJ's Open Another Log Tab).** The owner asked whether GitTree could open several log tabs, as IntelliJ's `+` does. It could not: one panel, one session, and File History, Compare with Current and an update's Show in Log all *replaced* the filter you had set. Specified from the owner's IntelliJ screenshots: the strip reads `Git · Log · Console× · Worktrees× · Log× · Log× · + ⌄`; unfiltered tabs are titled `Log`, a branch-filtered one `Log: 2026.1`; a new tab's Branches pane is folded to its strip. Four questions settled the rest. The `⌄` offers nothing useful, so it is omitted. The tab menu is Close Tab / Close Other Tabs / Close All Tabs. Console and Worktrees stay unclosable (**D33**, owner's call). A new tab always starts with Branches folded. The approved plan: `+`, own tabs for those three commands, filter-made titles, lazy restore.

**Model.** `src/log/logTabs.ts` (pure) holds the tab list: the main tab (`log`, the strip's old id) first, extras `log-<n>` appended, ids never reused, plus the active tab, stored in workspace state (`gittree.log.tabs`). `restoreTabsState` checks every field, puts the main tab first, drops malformed entries one at a time and never hands out an id in use. `findTab` / `sameView` reuse a tab showing the same commits in the same repository, ignoring sort, empty lists and an empty search. The main tab keeps following `gittree.log.root`; an extra tab stores its own root.

**Host.** `LogViewProvider` holds one `Session` per shown tab: row store, graph layout, Changed Files sequence, selection, repository watches and `lastHeadSha`. Every tab-scoped request and event carries `logTab` (absent = the main tab, so nothing older breaks). New methods: `log/tabs`, `log/openTab` (`+`, copying the source tab's live filter and repository), `log/closeTabs`, `log/activateTab`, and the event `log/tabsChanged`, sent after every refilter so titles follow. `log/init` no longer carries the strip tab; POST-P14af's early `showTab` rides on `log/tabs` as `show`. Public API: `revealCommit`, `setFilter`, `refresh`, `currentSession`, `selectedCommits` and `findLoadedChild` act on the active tab. New `openTab(filter, root)` reuses or appends, and the webview starts the session when it shows the tab. `showFileHistory`, `branches.compareInLog` and both Show in Log paths (new internal command `gittree.log.openTab`) use it. `gittree.log.setFilter` still filters the tab in front. **Cost:** history moving marks every affected session `dirty`, but only the active tab re-pages; another re-pages on `activateTab`. Closing no longer re-activates the fallback tab, since the strip says what comes forward. A seeded legacy filter re-announces the tabs. New test seam: `logTabs`.

**Webview.** `App.tsx` is now the shell: the strip, `+` (`Open Another Log Tab`, a toolbar button right after the last tab), Console, Worktrees, the tab menu, and global prefs (presentation, files rollup, preview, highlight). `LogTab.tsx` is the old log body, one per tab: every request goes through `send` with its `logTab`, and every event for another tab is ignored. A tab mounts the first time it is shown, plus the active Log tab always, so commands have a session. It then stays mounted, hidden by `visibility` rather than `display: none` (which would reset its scroll). Titles come from `logTabsModel.tabTitle`: `Log`, `Log: <branches>`, then labelled `User:` / `Date:` / `Paths:` / `Text:`, and `History: <file>` for a single followed path. `afterClose` brings the left neighbour forward. `Tabs` (shared) gained optional `closable` (a `×` span, not a second tab stop), middle-click, `onTabContextMenu` (keyboard: context-menu key, anchored under the tab), `title` and a `trailing` slot. `BranchesPane` passes `logTab` with rail actions. Per-tab focus and layout persist under `tabsUi`; the single log's old top-level keys seed the main tab once.

Docs: FEATURES §4, gap analysis §3.4 (new row ✅; History row retexted, still 🟡), parity audit §4, D33, keyboard.md, CHANGELOG.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction): **+2 unit files**. `test/unit/log/logTabs.test.ts` covers open/close/activate/update, `sameView`, `findTab` across repositories, and `restoreTabsState` over garbage, duplicates, a missing main tab and id floors. `test/unit/webview/logTabsModel.test.ts` covers every title form, strip order, `afterClose` and the three close actions. **+9 Playwright** in `log.spec.ts`; the mock now keeps a tab list, answers the four tab methods and tags rows and resets with `logTab`. They cover:
- `+` opening a tab after the others with its own init and pages, and Branches folded;
- only extras having a `×`;
- titles following filters, and `+` copying them;
- `×` behind and in front, with the left neighbour coming forward;
- middle-click, which never closes the main tab;
- the menu's three items and disabled states;
- a tab behind keeping its selection, with `activateTab` heard;
- a command-opened `History: app.ts` tab coming forward;
- restored tabs not starting until shown.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; webviews and the extension bundle built. No suite run. The integration suites read `currentSession` after File History and Compare; it now names the active tab, which those commands make the new one, so they should hold. That is unverified until a run.

POST-P14ak ✅ 2026-11-01: **Graph Options (IntelliJ's sort-and-options menu) in place of the sort dropdown.** The owner showed IntelliJ's button after `Paths ▾` (an arrow beside three rows, a green dot) and its menu: `Sort` · By Commit Date · Topologically, `Options` · First Parent · No Merges. GitTree had a `<select>` of Date / Topo / IntelliSort and neither option. Facts from IntelliJ's source and its `VcsLogBundle`: the group is `Graph Options`; Topologically is IntelliSort (Bek, "incoming commits first, directly below merge commit"); First Parent is a `PermanentGraph.Options` value *in place of* `Base(sortType)`; No Merges is a parent-count *filter* whose `displayText` is `--no-merges`; a tab is titled by `VcsLogFilterCollection.getPresentation` — one filter bare, several prefixed (`on`, `by`, `made since/until/between`, `for`, `containing`) and joined by spaces, a range and `--no-merges` unprefixed, paths by file name, text in single quotes. The owner settled four points: two Sort rows like IntelliJ's; First Parent leaves no Sort row ticked; the dot shows whenever the graph is not By Commit Date with nothing ticked; titles switch to IntelliJ's rule.

**Model.** `LogFilter` gains `firstParent?` and `noMerges?` (protocol DTO too); both count in `filterKey`, `isUnfiltered` and `sameView`, and `restoreTabsState` validates them. `buildLogArgs`: First Parent adds `--first-parent` and draws in date order (`graphSort`), the stored sort waiting underneath. No Merges adds **no argv**. git's `--no-merges` hides a merge before anything sees its parents, and `--parents` does not rewrite across it (checked on git 2.55), so every merge's child would leave a lane open to the bottom of the log. The host drops merges itself instead (`hidesCommit`), passing each through the new `GraphLayout.hide`, which re-points lanes waiting for the merge at its first parent; the merged branch arrives as a tip. Likewise `--first-parent` still lists every parent under `--parents`, so `graphParents` draws only the first. A page that was all hidden merges reads on to the next, since the webview pages on scroll and an empty list has none.

**Webview.** `graphOptions.ts` (pure): the six rows, `applyGraphOption` (Topologically picks IntelliSort unless the tab is already in git's topo order; a sort ends First Parent; First Parent keeps the sort to return to; the ticked row returns `undefined` so nothing re-runs) and `graphOptionsChanged` for the dot. `ToolbarButton` gains `glyph` (an inline SVG of IntelliJ's icon; no codicon resembles it) and `badge` (the dot, `--vscode-charts-green`, `Highlight` under forced colours). Reset all filters keeps the sort and First Parent and drops No Merges, IntelliJ's filter. `tabTitle` follows IntelliJ's rule; GitTree's `HEAD` default stays untitled, ISO dates stay ISO.

Docs: FEATURES §4 (Graph Options bullet; title rule), CHANGELOG (Added + Changed), gap analysis §3.4 (sort row rewritten, First Parent / No Merges ✅, Collapse/Expand Linear Branches ❌), parity audit, keyboard.md, README.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction): **+1 unit file** (`test/unit/webview/graphOptions.test.ts`: rows, ticks, every click, the dot). Additions to `logFilter.test.ts` (argv, key, `isUnfiltered`, the three helpers), `graphLayout.test.ts` (`hide`: no row, the child's line runs into the first parent, the tip, no dangling lane vs. git's own `--no-merges`, several children, a hidden tip, page carry), `logTabs.test.ts` (`sameView`, restore) and `logTabsModel.test.ts` (titles rewritten to IntelliJ's rule). **Playwright** in `log.spec.ts`: the old sort round-trip now goes through the menu, plus six new tests covering:
- the groups, the rows and the default ticks, with no dot;
- Topologically, the dot, and the ticked row re-running nothing;
- No Merges titling the tab `Log: on main --no-merges`;
- First Parent clearing the Sort tick, and a sort ending it;
- the keyboard skipping both captions;
- Reset keeping the sort and First Parent.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; webviews and the extension bundle built. No suite run.

POST-P14al ✅ 2026-11-01: **The commit list in IntelliJ's columns.** The owner asked for the rows to match IntelliJ's log: graph · message · refs · Author · Date. GitTree drew one line per commit, `[chips] subject  author · date · sha`. Everything below was read from IntelliJ's source rather than guessed: `VcsLogDefaultColumn`, `VcsLogColumnManager`, `GraphCommitCellRenderer`, `LabelPainter`, `TagPainter`, `SimpleRefGroup`, `GitRefManager`, `VcsUserUtil`, `VcsLogApplicationSettings` and `VcsLogBundle`, fetched with `gh api` search and raw files. The owner chose four extras: IntelliJ-style labels, Compact References View, References on the Left and the Author `*`. They also chose Hash hidden by default and always-full dates, with no "Today"/"Yesterday". The "You" in the owner's screenshot is the sandbox user's real name; IntelliJ has no such substitution.

**Columns** (`webview-ui/src/log/columns.ts`, pure). Subject takes the remainder, then Author, Date and Hash in IntelliJ's order, with no header row. Author fits `name*` over the first 1000 rows, capped at 300 px. Date is sized by a sample date-time in bold. Hash is eight characters, sized by `e`, `0`, `8` and `d` in bold, because IntelliJ's `e` sample under-measures digits in a proportional font (seen in a screenshot). Dragged widths are kept while they fit the row. Columns give way together before the message drops under graph + 80 px. Each column's left border is a 5 px drag zone and double-click resets it. Widths persist per tab in `LogTabUiState.columnWidths`, saved on mouse-up from the last computed width, since React may not have drawn the final move yet.

**Labels** (`refLabels.ts`, pure; `RefLabelsView.tsx`). Grouping ports `groupForTable`:
- labels sort HEAD, current branch, main/master, origin/main, other locals, other remotes, tags;
- a branch pairs with its upstream (now on `LogBranchesDto.locals[].upstream`), else with `<remote>/<name>`, as `origin & main`;
- the current branch is its own group;
- in compact mode everything is one group;
- HEAD joins the first group, or forms a `Detached HEAD` group.

Fitting ports `calculatePresentation`, `getAvailableWidth` and `shortenRefName`: padding 4/4, gaps 12/6, icon width `(6.25 + 2(n−1))·h/6.25`, groups that do not fit fold into one last icon, and names over 22 characters become `..` plus the end. The icon is IntelliJ's `currentBranch.svg` tag outline, one per colour, each masked under the next, with the top one dotted. Colours are HEAD `--vscode-charts-yellow`, local green, remote purple, tags grey. Hover cards list a label's refs, or show IntelliJ's detached-HEAD warning.

**Host.**
- `buildLogArgs` adds `--decorate=full`, and `parseDecorations` reads full names. This fixes two existing bugs: a local `feature/x` was typed remote, and `refs/stash` became a "remote" chip. Stash, notes and remote `HEAD` now get no label.
- `vcsUser.ts` ports `getUserName`, `getNameInStandardForm` and `isSamePerson`, so the DTO carries `committedByOther` and the email fallback for an empty name. IntelliJ's robot-committer list is empty by default, so no committer is exempt.
- Five settings join `PRESENTATION_SETTINGS`, all global as in IntelliJ: `compactReferences` (true), `referencesOnLeft`, and `columns.author` / `.date` / `.hash`. `presentation()` and the config listener iterate the table.
- The webview merges a partial presentation over the defaults, so a column never disappears for want of a key.
- Dates follow IntelliJ's `formatDateTime`: numeric month and day, four-digit year, unpadded hour.

Checked by hand in screenshots of the mock page, at 1x and 4x zoom: the columns line up, the stacked icons draw, Hash fits, and dragging the Author border went 59 → 181 px without selecting the row.

Docs: FEATURES §4, CHANGELOG (Changed, and the Tag Names entry corrected), gap analysis §3.4 (Presentation row updated, new commit-table row ✅), parity audit, keyboard.md, settings.md regenerated.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **+4 unit files:** `columns.test.ts`, `presentationMenu.test.ts`, `log/vcsUser.test.ts`, and `refLabels.test.ts` (rewritten: grouping, colours, icon width, shortening, budget, folding, Detached HEAD, tooltips, repoRefs).
- **Additions** to `dateFormat.test.ts`, `presentation.test.ts`, `logFormat.test.ts` (full names) and `logFilter.test.ts` (`--decorate=full`).
- **Playwright:** the four ref/tag tests reworked for labels, plus seven new tests covering:
  - the columns lining up, with Hash hidden;
  - the Author `*`;
  - Columns ▸ Hash;
  - a hidden column's room going to the message;
  - labels at the end of the message, or on the left;
  - the HEAD + branch icon;
  - drag, persist and double-click.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; webviews and the extension bundle built. No suite run.

POST-P14am ✅ 2026-11-01: **Fetch one repository, from its row in the Branches section.** The owner asked whether GitTree could fetch only the current repository. Since POST-P14o every Fetch is project-wide, as IntelliJ's is, and the only per-repository Fetch was the Repositories section's right-click menu, collapsed and easy to miss. The owner checked IntelliJ and found no per-repository fetch there either, so this is GitTree's own. They asked for it on a repository row's right-click in the sidebar's Branches section.

That section had no right-click menu at all, only the `›` button on branch rows. Now:
- **A repository row's right-click (and `Shift+F10` / the context-menu key) opens the repository's menu.** It is the Repositories section's menu for the same repository, less `Checkout Branch…`, which is what the Branches section already is. The list lives in `src/branches/repositoryMenu.ts` (pure), and a unit test holds it to that section's `view/item/context` entries in `package.json`, entry for entry: same commands, same order, same rules, same titles. Every entry runs its command with the root, which each command reads as "this repository only".
- **Fetch from that menu** marks the row `busy`, a new optional `TreeRow` field that no builder sets. The row's icon becomes a spinning `sync`, its aria label adds ", fetching", and it clears when the fetch settles. The fetch is not awaited inside the RPC handler, because a fetch can outlast the request's timeout. A second request for a repository already fetching is dropped.
- **A branch row's right-click** opens its `›` menu, and a common branch's opens the host's all-roots menu, so right-click means the same on every row that has a menu. Over action, section and folder rows VS Code's useless Cut/Copy/Paste menu is kept shut. The repository search field keeps it.
- **Keyboard placement:** Chromium marks a keyboard-opened menu with `button: -1`, so that opens under the row.
- `VirtualList.onRowContextMenu` now receives the event.
- `gittree.fetch <root>` on a repository with no remote shows `<name> has no remote to fetch from.` instead of git's error dialog, which waits on a button. `hasRemote` is factored out of the fetch-all target filter.

Checked by hand in screenshots of the new Branches mock page: the menu items, the spin while fetching, the branch menu on right-click, and Shift+F10 placing the menu right under the row.

Docs: FEATURES §2 (the sidebar's Branches section), CHANGELOG, keyboard.md (new Branches section table).

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **+1 unit file:** `test/unit/branches/repositoryMenu.test.ts`, checking the menu against `package.json` and the NLS titles, the rules and the entry lookup.
- **+1 integration test** in `syncFetchAll.test.ts`: an explicit root with no remote returns without fetching.
- **New Branches webview harness:** `webview-ui/tests/mockBranchesHost.ts` with `branches.spec.ts`, 7 Playwright tests covering:
  - the repository menu;
  - Fetch's request and the row spinning and settling;
  - Shift+F10 placement;
  - click still toggling, with no `›`;
  - a branch's right-click matching its `›`;
  - a common branch's right-click asking the host;
  - the default menu kept shut on plain rows and kept in the search field.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs, plus the integration tsconfig) + lint clean; webviews and the extension bundle built. No suite run.

POST-P14an ✅ 2026-11-01: **Selecting several commits in the log, as IntelliJ does.** The owner asked for IntelliJ's Ctrl/Shift multi-selection, its combined changes and its context menu for a selection, with screenshots of all three. GitTree already kept a list of selected commits, but:
- only the focused row was ever highlighted, so it looked as if nothing was selected;
- Shift ranged from the first-clicked commit, and Ctrl+click left the focus behind;
- Changed Files showed `git diff <oldest>^..<newest>`, which pulled in commits nobody selected;
- Commit Details showed only one commit;
- the native menu treated every selection as one commit.

IntelliJ's behaviour was read from its source:
- the menu from `Vcs.Log.ContextMenu` + `Git.Log.ContextMenu` + `Dvcs.Log.ContextMenu`;
- per-action visibility and enablement from `VcsLogSingleCommitAction`, `VcsLogOneCommitPerRepoAction`, `GitSingleCommitEditingAction`, `GitCommitEditingActionBase`, `GitRevertAction`, `CompareRevisionsFromLogAction`, `GoToParentOrChildAction` and `GitCreateNewBranchFromCommitAction`;
- the copy format from `CopyRevisionNumberAction`;
- the changes from `VcsLogUtil.collectChanges` / `CommittedChangesTreeBrowser.zipChanges`;
- the details from `CommitDetailsListPanel` / `MAX_COMMITS_TO_LOAD = 50`;
- squash and drop from `GitSquashLogAction`, `GitRebaseTodoModel.unite`, `GitSquashedCommitsMessage.prettySquash` and `GitDropOperation`.

**Selection** (`webview-ui/src/log/logSelection.ts`, pure): JTable multiple-interval rules, with an anchor, kept in graph order.
- Click selects one; Ctrl/⌘ toggles one and moves the anchor; Shift selects anchor..row; Ctrl+Shift adds that run.
- Shift with an arrow, PageUp/Down or Home/End extends from the anchor; Ctrl+A selects every loaded row.
- Right-click outside the selection selects that row; inside, it keeps the selection.
- `VirtualList` gains `isRowSelected`, which sets `aria-selected` and `gt-selected`, plus `multiselectable` and `focusIndex(i, {silent})`, so Ctrl and Shift gestures move the lead without collapsing the selection.
- The CSS highlights every selected row. A focused row that is not selected keeps only its focus outline. Rows are `user-select: none`, because Shift+click would otherwise select text.

**Changed Files** (`src/log/zipChanges.ts`, pure, IntelliJ's algorithm): the files the selected commits changed, keyed by where each file ends.
- A change continues an earlier one, keeping its start; deletions are placed last; add-then-delete drops out; a copy counts as an add.
- Each row carries `before` (`<sha>^` of the earliest selected commit that touched it) and `after`. `openFileDiff` and the diff sequence use them, and the multi-diff button now works for several commits.
- The files come from one `git log --no-walk=unsorted --stdin -m --first-parent --name-status -z -M` call, which avoids the command-line limit. The cap is 1000 commits (`MAX_SELECTED_COMMITS`).
- `getAggregateFiles` and the DTO's `base` are gone. `LogChangedFilesDto` now carries `shas`.

**Commit Details** stacks up to 50 blocks with rules between them and IntelliJ's `(showing 50 of N selected commits)`. Each commit's details are fetched once and cached per tab, and the cache clears on reset.

**Menu.** `data-vscode-context` gains `gittree.commitCount`, `gittree.multiCommit` and `gittree.mergeSelected` (`commitMenuContext.ts`, pure), and `package.json`'s commit menu is rewritten in IntelliJ's order and groups:
- **Hidden** for several commits (`when`): Checkout Revision, Compare with Local, Revert/Drop Commit (the singular forms), Undo, Edit Message, Fixup, Squash Into, Interactively Rebase, Branches…, Open on Remote.
- **Greyed** (`enablement: !gittree.multiCommit`, which VS Code evaluates against the menu's context keys): Show Repository at Revision, Reset, Push All up to Here, New Branch…, New Tag…, Go to Child/Parent.
- **New commands:** `gittree.commit.revertCommits` and `gittree.commit.dropCommits` (the plural titles), `gittree.log.squashCommits` (all three greyed when a merge is selected) and `gittree.log.compareVersions` (`commitCount == 2`).
- **Renamed** to IntelliJ's titles: Cherry-Pick, New Branch…, New Tag…. The log-only New Branch, New Tag and Reset are hidden from the palette; they did nothing there without a commit, and "New Branch…" would have shown twice.
- Copy Revision Number joins with spaces.

**Drop and squash** (`src/rewrite/multiCommitEdit.ts`, with pure plans in `multiCommitPlans.ts`):
- IntelliJ's checks, each named when it fails: every commit is in the current branch (`merge-base --octopus` gives the oldest, and the range must contain them all), there is no merge in `base..HEAD`, and none is the initial commit.
- The published guard asks for confirmation; this is GitTree's rule, where IntelliJ refuses commits on a protected branch.
- Drop of an unbroken run uses the new `RebaseEngine.dropRange` (`rebase --onto oldest^ newest`), because a todo of nothing but drops is invalid. Any other selection uses a drop plan.
- Squash builds a root `reword` with the other selected commits moved behind it as `fixup`s. Its message is IntelliJ's `prettySquash`, edited in `RewordService.promptMessage`, which is now public and has a per-use Accept title.
- Both offer Undo, which resets to the pre-edit HEAD.

Checked by hand in screenshots of the mock page with VS Code's dark list colours injected:
- Click, Shift+click and Ctrl+click select rows 2–4 and 7, with four stacked detail blocks and the merged files.
- Shift+↓ extends from the anchor, and Ctrl+click removes a row, leaving it outlined.
- Right-click outside the selection selects that row.

Docs: FEATURES §4 (selection bullet, Changed Files and Details, the commit menu rewritten in IntelliJ's order and rules), CHANGELOG (Added + Changed), gap analysis §3.4 (Squash Commits ✅, new multi-select row ✅), keyboard.md.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **+6 unit files:**
  - `log/zipChanges.test.ts`: union, ends, add/delete, rename chains, copies, deletion order, the parser.
  - `rewrite/multiCommitPlans.test.ts`: contiguity, drop plan, squash plan and its serialized todo, `prettySquash`.
  - `webview/logSelection.test.ts`.
  - `webview/commitMenuContext.test.ts`.
  - `log/commitMenu.test.ts`: evaluates the manifest's `when` and `enablement` and reads the menu for one, two, four and merge selections against IntelliJ's screenshot.
- **Integration:** `phase12.test.ts`'s aggregate test is rewritten to check that the selected commits' files exclude an unselected commit's.
- **Playwright:** the multi-commit Changed Files test is rewritten (graph order, multi-diff with `shas`, per-file `before`/`after`), the context test is extended with the new keys, and five tests are new:
  - Ctrl+click toggling and highlighting;
  - Shift and Ctrl+Shift ranges;
  - Shift+arrows and Ctrl+A;
  - right-click selection;
  - stacked details with the 50 cap.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs, plus the integration tsconfig) + lint clean; webviews and the extension bundle built. No suite run.

POST-P14ao ✅ 2026-11-01: **Push, as IntelliJ's.** The owner asked whether Push showed no dialog, and for IntelliJ's: the commits about to go, and the target branch, `New` when the remote does not have it. GitTree had a Push dialog (P13-T1), but:
- only `Push…`, the popup's verb and a Repositories row opened it;
- a branch's `Push…`, `Push All up to Here` and the palette's `Push` pushed at once;
- its layout was GitTree's own: a commit list with hashes and dates, the whole push's diff, and `Run Git hooks` always shown.

IntelliJ's behaviour was read from its source:
- the dialog from `VcsPushDialog`, `PushLog`, `PushLogChangesBrowser`, `PushController`, `RepositoryNode`/`SingleRepositoryNode`/`CommitNode` and `RepositoryWithBranchPanel`;
- targets from `GitPushSupport`, `GitPushTarget`, `GitPushSpecParser`, `GitPushTargetPanel` and `GitPushTargetCompletionProvider`, with recent targets as `GitVcsSettings` keeps them;
- the commits from `GitOutgoingCommitsProvider` and the refspec from `GitPushOperation.createPushSpec`;
- the footer from `GitPushOptionsPanel`;
- Force Push from `ForcePushAction` and `GitSharedSettings`, and the remote form from `GitDefineRemoteDialog`;
- the entry points from `VcsPushAction`, `GitPushBranchAction` and `GitPushUpToCommitAction`;
- strings from `DvcsBundle` and `GitBundle`.

The owner checked one thing the source could not settle: right-clicking a commit in IntelliJ's Push dialog shows no menu, so GitTree's shows none either.

**Rules** (vscode-free, shared by host and webview):
- `src/scm/dialogs/pushModel.ts`: sources (branch, a commit on a branch, detached), the default target, typed targets, the outgoing range, the refspec, when the upstream is set, the `Set upstream` checkbox and the `New`/`Upstream` labels, protected branches, the Force Push warning, recent targets and their suggestion order, the title, and which repositories start checked.
- `pushSpecParser.ts`: `remote.<name>.push`.
- `src/core/git/refName.ts`: IntelliJ's `GitRefNameValidator`.

**Host** (`pushDialog.ts`, rewritten):
- `PushDialogRequest {repos, preselected, current?, source?}`.
- Rows built in parallel, with `git log --max-count=1000` over the outgoing range.
