- Handlers: `retarget`, `changes` (`getCommitsFiles` + `zipChanges`), `details`, `openDiff` (a diff sequence opened at the file), `openFile`, `defineRemote` (name checks, `ls-remote`, `remote add`, optional fetch) and `saveUi`.
- `applyPushResult` takes each row's source and builds `pushRefspec` + `setsUpstream`. It remembers recent targets for pushes that went through, and the targets marked *don't warn*.
- `buildPushArgs` now takes a refspec. `pushRun`'s `PushTarget` gained `source`, so a commit pushed on its own is counted correctly and is never updated and pushed again.

**Entry points:**
- `gittree.push.dialog` lists every repository and checks the one named, or else the current one.
- New internal `gittree.push.branch` (a branch's `Push…`; a common branch's runs once via the new `BranchAction.runAll`) and `gittree.push.upTo` (`Push All up to Here…`, on the branch holding the commit).
- Commit and Push lists every repository, checking the committed ones.
- `gittree.push` and `gittree.pushForceWithLease` are hidden from the palette.
- New setting `gittree.protectedBranches`; `gittree.push.runHooks` removed.

**Webview** (`Push.tsx` rewritten, plus `pushTree.ts`, `PushTargetEditor.tsx`, `PushChanges.tsx` and `PushOverlays.tsx`):
- The tree reuses the log's selection rules.
- The changes pane is grouped by repository.
- The details are the log's `DetailsPanel`, whose copy, parent and contained-branch extras are now optional. Its CSS and the Changed Files CSS moved to `lib/commitDetails.css`, which both apps import.
- Force Push and Define Remote are drawn inside the dialog, so IntelliJ's checkboxes stay.
- `Menu` items gained a `title` tooltip.

Checked by hand in screenshots of the mock page with dark theme colours injected:
- one repository, a selected commit with details, the target editor with suggestions and `Set upstream`, a retarget to a `New` branch;
- the Force Push warning;
- two repositories (checkbox, faded unchecked row, `Edit all targets` mirroring the typed text);
- Define Remote with a refused URL;
- a protected `main` greying Force Push with its tooltip.

That pass found and fixed two problems: a click inside the target editor applied the edit under it, and the Define Remote fields overflowed.

Docs: FEATURES §9 (the Push dialog, the P12 list), CHANGELOG (Changed), gap analysis (Push dialog, Push All up to Here, the protected-branch note), keyboard.md (a Push dialog table; the palette note).

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):

POST-P14ap ✅ 2026-11-01: **Completion notifications.** The owner reported that many operations finish with no word that they did. Their example was the Branches view's Fetch button, which fetches every repository and then said nothing. They asked for every such case to be found and given the correct notification.

**IntelliJ's behaviour, read from its source:** the 36 `notifySuccess` call sites in git4idea, plus `GitFetchSupportImpl`, `ShowNotificationCommitResultHandler` and `GitStashUtils`. The strings came from `GitBundle`, `DvcsBundle` and `VcsBundle`.

**The audit** went through every repository-changing operation (`kind: 'mutate'`) and every palette and webview action.

Silent on success:
- **Fetch:** a deliberate "the counts speak" rule.
- **Pull and Sync.**
- **Commit.**
- **Branches:** checkout (plain, forced and smart), checkout of a remote branch, new branch, rename, delete and remote delete, merge, both rebases, Pull into Current, and the Branches tree's drag-to-rebase.
- **Log:** New Branch, New Tag, Checkout Revision, Reset Here, and the fixup/squash autosquash.
- **Rewrites:** Undo Commit and Edit Commit Message.
- **Rebase and conflicts:** rebase continue, skip and abort (a five-second status-bar line only), the interactive rebase editor (its panel log only), conflict Continue and Abort, and cherry-pick/revert sequence abort.
- **Stash and shelf:** stash, apply, pop and branch from stash; shelve (quick and dialog), shelf export and import.
- **Files:** Roll Back… and Delete Untracked Files.

Already reporting: push, Update Project, branch Update, revert, cherry-pick, drop, squash, the option dialogs and worktrees.

Left quiet, as in IntelliJ: staging, gutter rollback, `.gitignore`, changelists, stash drop and clear, shelf rename and delete, and background fetch.

**Helper:**
- `src/ui/notify.ts` has `notifySuccess`/`notifyWarning` with action buttons, recorded for tests (`notifications` debug registry, `GITTREE_TEST_NOTIFICATION_ACTION` to click an action).
- `collectNotifications`/`showCollected` (`AsyncLocalStorage`, so a concurrent operation is not swept in) let `runCommonAction` say an all-repositories action once.
- Pure wording lives in `src/scm/resultMessages.ts` (`plural`, `pullResultMessage`, `committedMessage`, `joinMessages`) and `fetchAll.ts` (`parsePrunedRefs`, `fetchSuccessMessage`).

**Messages** are IntelliJ's:
- **Fetch:** *Fetch successful* plus the pruned references.
- **Commit:** *N files committed: subject*, one for a multi-root commit. `runCommitFlow` gained `notify`/`onCommitted`.
- **Branches:** *Checked out X*, *Checked out new branch X from Y*, *Branch X was created*, *Branch X was renamed to Y*, *Merged X to Y* or *Already up to date*, *Rebased X on Y*, *Checked out X and rebased it on Y*, *Deleted remote branch X*.
- **Branch delete:** *Deleted branch X* (+ *Unmerged commits were discarded*) with **Restore** (the branch at its old tip, its upstream reset) and **Delete Tracked Branch**. These two buttons replace the old "Also delete the remote branch?" question.
- **Reset and undo:** *Reset successful*, *Undo commit successful*.
- **Edit Commit Message:** *Commit message changed* with **Undo** (`reset --keep` to the pre-reword head, refused when HEAD has moved on).
- **Rebase, merge, cherry-pick, revert:** *Rebase successful*, *Abort rebase succeeded*, *{Merge|Cherry-pick|Revert} abort succeeded*, *Cherry-pick continue successful*.
- **Stash and shelf:** *Files stashed successfully*, *Stash successfully applied*, *Changes shelved successfully*, *Successfully created patch …*.

**Pull** counts against the pulled branch (`countPulled`: `before..@{upstream}`), so a rebasing pull does not count the user's own rewritten commits.

The Merge, Rebase and Reset dialogs took the same wording; the Stash dialog took *Files stashed successfully*. The branches registry exposes `runCommonAction`, so a test sees the extension's own notifications.

Docs: FEATURES §1 (Completion notifications), CHANGELOG (Added).

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `scm/fetchAll.test.ts` (+ prune parsing, the fetch message) and the new `scm/resultMessages.test.ts`.
- **Integration:**
  - `phase02` T3: create, rename, delete, **Restore** through the seam, and force delete.
  - `phase02` T4: smart checkout.
  - `phase13`: Fetch with a pruned branch, and without.
  - `phase03` T5: changelist commit count.
  - `phase05`: Undo commit; reword's notification and a new test clicking **Undo**.
  - `multiRoot`: one notification for a spanning commit.
  - `projectPopup`: an all-repositories checkout says it once.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs, plus the integration tsconfig) + lint clean; extension bundle built. No suite run.

POST-P14aq ✅ 2026-11-01: **Reported-bug sweep.** The owner listed bugs they had hit and asked which were still present, and for those to be fixed with commits.

**Already fixed** before this sweep:
- **Commit graph not updating after a commit:** fixed in 452618b (auto-refresh on model refs/head change, covered by a `phase04` test).

**Fixed now:**
- **Graph flashes after cherry-pick and push** (ad47123): a `preserve` reset no longer empties the rows or the selection. The webview keeps both, restores the top row, prunes the selection against page 0, and re-reads the details in place (`detailsEpoch`). `runAutoRefresh` reschedules while the repository's queue runs a mutation (up to `AUTO_REFRESH_MAX_WAIT_MS` = 5 s), so a multi-step operation costs one re-read.
- **Laggy text filter** (cdc15d5): `FilterBar` applies the text 300 ms after typing pauses (`TEXT_FILTER_DELAY_MS`), and at once on Enter.
- **"master" shown for a repo on "abc"** (777d698): `RepositoryModel.refsGeneration` is bumped on every `refs`/`head` change, and `LogService.cacheKey` includes it. This is the most likely cause found; the report did not include steps to reproduce it.
- **Stale pending-changes badge after Refresh** (1d27e96): `StatusRefresher.refreshNow` resolves only after a pass that started after the call (`nextPassWaiters`).
- **Update progress closes and reopens; false "Already up to date"** (9352154):
  - `beginOperationProgress` (`src/ui/progress.ts`) is one progress for a multi-step operation. Its steps run `quietProgress` with linked tokens (`stepUnder`).
  - The incoming count is `HEAD..upstream`, measured before integrating (`incomingFromUpstream`).
- **No click feedback** (1ff4f6a):
  - `:active` styling on `.gt-toolbar-button` (`toolbar.activeBackground`, icon scaled to 0.85) and `.gt-button`.
  - `ToolbarButton.busy` (`gt-spin`, `aria-busy`, clicks ignored), driven by the new `useBusy` hook (min 450 ms). It is wired to commit Refresh (awaits the host), Branches header Fetch (10-min timeout), log Refresh (until page 0 arrives; the host now answers `{ ok }`), the Worktrees refresh and the Shelve dialog's refresh.
  - `LogViewProvider.refresh()` always preserves.
- **Slow first load** (f514b3e): `LogService.maybeMaintain` queued `commit-graph write --reachable --changed-paths` as a *mutation* at every Log session start, once a day per repository. A mutation cancels in-flight reads and blocks new ones, so the first page, status, branches and details waited for it, under a progress notification. It now runs outside the queue:
  - It waits `MAINTENANCE_DELAY_MS` (60 s), then retries every 15 s while a mutation is active.
  - It is stamped when it starts and skipped once the repository is disposed (`LogService.dispose`).
  - Other startup costs checked: per-repo first reads take about 80 ms on this machine, and nothing else takes the mutation lane unprompted. The UI still registers only after every repository's first read. That was left as is, because early registration would let the Log pin whichever repository finished first.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `repositoryModel.test.ts` (refsGeneration), `statusRefresher.test.ts` (refreshNow during a pass, dispose), `logService.test.ts` (five maintenance cases).
- **Integration:** `phase13` (Update counts commits an earlier fetch brought down).
- **Playwright:**
  - `log.spec.ts`: auto-refresh never blanks, search debounce, Refresh log spins and keeps the selection.
  - `commit.spec.ts`: Refresh spins until answered, and for at least a moment.
  - `branches.spec.ts`: the header Fetch spins.
  - `kit.spec.ts`: press feedback.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs, plus the integration tsconfig) + lint clean; extension and webview bundles built. No suite run.

POST-P14ar ✅ 2026-11-02 (branch `new-way-4`): **The Branches panel, redesigned.** The owner found the sidebar panel hard to use, "worse than VS Code's Source Control panel". A native-tree demo of the same content (kept on `new-way-3-native-tree-demo`, commit 1551424) was judged no better, so the panel was redesigned as a webview of GitTree's own (D34).

**Design:**
- **Status first:**
  - several repositories: a Repositories section, one row each — colour dot, what is checked out, `↓ ↑`, changes, hover Update / Push / Fetch / `⋯`;
  - one repository: a current-branch row with the same buttons and a card;
  - an operation-in-progress banner (Resolve… / Continue / Abort).
- **Relevance:** Recent (starred, then reflog-recent, merged across repositories a rank at a time, at most 8), Local (folders, starred first), Remote (per remote, shut), Tags (newest first, shut).
- **One list for many repositories:** a branch name is one row, with a marker per repository in view (filled = checked out, ringed = has it, dot = absent). A scope (click a repository; Ctrl+click adds one; the filter's pill or *Show all* widens it) narrows both what is shown and what is acted on.
- **Cards:**
  - a click opens the branch's card: last commit, author, age; where it stands against its upstream and against HEAD (`rev-list --left-right --count HEAD...<sha>`, read when the card opens and cached by sha and HEAD);
  - two or three buttons chosen by state: Checkout and Merge; Checkout and Delete when merged or gone; Update, Push and New Branch on HEAD; Checkout and New Branch on a tag. Across repositories they are the `COMMON_ACTIONS`.
- **Menus:** the shared `branchActions` registry with real submenus: Tracked Branch, one submenu per repository, and the all-repositories operations first. Show in Git Log (new `gittree.branches.showInLog`).
- **Filter:** every word must match, matches are marked, sections flatten, Recent steps aside. The no-match hint offers Create branch.
- **New Branch form** (inline): `cleanUpBranchNameOnTyping`, a Check it out box, repository chips. It is validated before Create (`newBranch.ts`), then by the host (`createBranches.ts`): everything is checked first, then repositories are done in turn, stopping at the first failure.
- **Clean Up Branches…** (`gittree.branches.cleanup`):
  - offers branches merged into the main branch (ticked) and branches whose upstream is gone (unticked);
  - leaves branches sitting on main's tip unticked (`atMain`);
  - deletes with `-D` per repository and offers Restore.
  - The main branch is `mainBranchOf`: `origin/HEAD`'s target, `origin/main` or `origin/master`, then `main` or `master`.
- **Drag a branch onto another:** the branch trees' `showDropActions`.

**Code:**
- **Host:**
  - `branchPanel.ts`: provider, state with recent and merged caches, card facts, run dispatch, `fetchAll`, drop, a pending-event queue for a webview not yet listening;
  - `panelActions.ts`: menus, card buttons, the `PANEL_COMMANDS` allowlist;
  - `panelState.ts`: pure — `toRefDto`, `mainBranchOf`, `mergedBranches`, `shownMerged`, `operationOf`, `buildRepoDto`, `cleanupCandidates`, `preTicked`;
  - `createBranches.ts`, `branchCleanup.ts`.
- **Shared:** `rootColorIndices` (log and panel share repository colours). New protocol: `BranchPanel*Dto`, `BRANCHES_METHODS`.
- **Webview:**
  - `model.ts`: pure — entries, sections, search, folders, heights, card sizes, wording;
  - `PanelList.tsx`: a variable-height windowed list, focus kept by row id;
  - `Rows.tsx`, `NewBranchForm.tsx` with `newBranch.ts`, `App.tsx`.
- **Removed:** `branchesWebView.ts`, `buildSidebarRows`.
- **Gated:** the Repositories section moved behind `gittree.sidebar.extraViews`.
- **New setting:** `gittree.branches.groupByFolder`.

Docs: FEATURES §2 (the sidebar's Branches panel), D34 (+ a note on D29), keyboard.md, CHANGELOG, settings.md.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `test/unit/branches/panelState.test.ts`, `test/unit/webview/branchPanelModel.test.ts` (model and New Branch rules).
- **Integration:** `branchPanel.test.ts` — state, create (and its refusals), multi-repository menu and card, Checkout across both repositories and back, card facts, merged and Clean Up by name. It restores repoD's `main` and deletes its branches.
- **Playwright:** `branches.spec.ts`, rewritten against a three-repository mock host (`mockBranchesHost.ts`; `__mockSingleRepo`, `__mockHoldFetch`).

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs, plus the integration tsconfig), lint, extension bundle and webview build clean. Screenshots of the mock in dark theme checked by eye at 380px and 280px wide.

POST-P14as ✅ 2026-11-02 (branch `new-way-4`): **New branches stop tracking on their own; Stop Tracking.** The owner reported two problems: a branch made from an existing branch "automatically set that branch to be remotely tracked", so pushing it "created no branch on GitHub, and an existing branch there received new commits"; and "there is no way to untrack the remote branch". The first reproduced in a scratch repository: GitTree passed the start point straight to git, `branch.autoSetupMerge` (on by default) set the upstream, and the Push dialog targets the upstream (`defaultPushTarget`, as IntelliJ's). IntelliJ behaves the same on both counts (`GitBranchCheckoutOperation` leaves tracking alone; its only untrack is a checkbox in the Rename dialog), so both changes are deliberate deviations (D35).

**The rule (`src/branches/newBranchArgs.ts`, vscode-free):** a new branch tracks nothing, unless it takes the name of the remote branch it starts from. That is git's `branch.autoSetupMerge=simple`, but the flag is always spelled out so the user's config cannot change the answer.
- `buildNewBranchArgs` is the argv for the panel's form (`createBranches.ts`) and the input box (`BranchOps.newBranchFrom`).
- `buildWorktreeAddArgs(o, remotes)` follows it too. The New Worktree dialog gets `remoteNames` in its params, so its preview shows the real command.
- `checkoutRemote` keeps `--track`: same name. `commitActions.ts` starts from a sha, which nothing tracks.

**Stop Tracking:**
- `stopTracking` in the `branchActions` registry, group `tracked`, on any local branch with a configured upstream (`configuredUpstream`, which includes a gone one).
- `BranchOps.stopTracking`: `git branch --unset-upstream`, an immediate refresh, a notification with `Undo` (`--set-upstream-to`) unless the upstream was gone.
- In `COMMON_ACTIONS`, so it works across repositories.
- Trees: `gittree.branch.stopTracking`, group `6_tracked@2`. `branchContextValue` now has `branch:<kind>:gone`, so a gone upstream gets this row and not `Update`.
- Log's Branches pane: `gittree.log.branch.stopTracking`, shown when the row's `data-vscode-context` has `gittree.branchTracked`.

**Panel:**
- `BranchEntry.tracksElsewhere` and `upstreamElsewhere`: a `→ origin/x` chip on rows, the same in place of the current-branch row's upstream, on the repository row's tooltip, and in a multi-repository card's lines.
- A **Stop tracking** link on a card: always for one repository; across several, only on a line that tracks elsewhere.
- `remoteTwin`: *Not tracking origin/x* rather than *Not published* when the remote has a branch of that name.
- `trackingNote` in the New Branch form, using the host's `newBranchTrackFlag`.
- `detailsKey` includes the upstream: a card's cached buttons kept `Update` after Stop Tracking.

**Found while reading, fixed:**
- **Rename could delete the wrong remote branch.** `renameBranch` offered `Push "new" and delete "old" on the remote` and deleted the *upstream's* name. For `mine` tracking `origin/develop` that is `develop`. It is now offered only when the upstream's name is the old name. The question also became actions on the notification (`Stop Tracking` always), so a rename no longer stays pending until a toast is answered.
- **The panel swallowed failures.** A rejection from `runPanel` on one repository stopped at the webview's `.catch`. `BranchPanelProvider.run` now shows the first line of the message and still answers, so a card's button stops turning.
- **Clean Up's Restore dropped the upstream.** It now passes it back (`--set-upstream-to`, non-zero allowed). The refresh moved to a `finally` before the notification: Restore skips a branch the snapshot still lists, and a fast click could beat the post-op refresh.
- **The opt-in tree's menu did nothing.** `branchArg` accepted only the legacy tree's `BranchNode`, while `gittree.branchesTree` hands its menu a `TreeRow`, so every row there returned early, the new one included. It accepts both now.
- **Stale test:** `branchMenu.test.ts` still asserted `view == gittree.branches ` from before the view moved to `gittree.branchesTree`.

Docs: FEATURES §2 and §10, D35, CHANGELOG.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `test/unit/branches/newBranchArgs.test.ts` (new), `worktreeArgs.test.ts`, `branchMenu.test.ts`, `test/unit/webview/branchPanelModel.test.ts`, `branchesPaneModel.test.ts`.
- **Integration:** `branchPanel.test.ts` T7-T17, against a bare repository: no tracking from another name, with `autoSetupMerge=always` too; tracking under the same name; the input box; Stop Tracking and Undo; a gone upstream; several repositories; rename's offers; Restore; a reported failure; the opt-in tree's row. `phase14branchmenu.test.ts`: `branch:local:gone`.
- **Playwright:** `branches.spec.ts` ("what a branch tracks", mock gains `feature/mistracked`), `newWorktree.spec.ts` (preview).

POST-P14at ✅ 2026-11-02 (branch `new-way-4`): **Walking a commit's changes across files, on VS Code's own arrows.** The owner: clicking a commit's file and iterating with "next", VS Code "only loops around the changes within that file only, while IntelliJ shows a tooltip telling me that if I hit next again it will go to the next file". P14-T7 had built this, but nobody could reach it:

**Found:**
- **The arrows never appeared.** They were contributed under `editor/title/navigation`, which is not a menu. `menusExtensionPoint.ts` ends on `if (!menu) continue;`, so VS Code dropped them silently, and T7's manifest test read the same wrong key. F7 needed `gittree.keymap.intellij`, off by default. `test/unit/diff/diffManifest.test.ts` now checks every menu key against VS Code's list at 1.96.0.
- **Added and deleted files were left out of the sequence**, and opened as plain documents with no next button: "next" skipped them.
- **Every crossing left a tab** (`preview: false`), one per file of the commit.
- **The order was git's**, while the pane sorts folders first.
- **GitTree's own diff chose where to stop**, so with `ignoreTrimWhitespace` on it could stop on a whitespace change VS Code does not show, and it counted CRLF against LF as a change on every line.
- **The hint** was italic text in CodeLens colour at the end of the line, gone after 3s, and off screen on a long line.
- **Images opened an error page** with no toolbar, ending the walk: VS Code's image viewer cannot read `gittree-rev:`.

**Design, each part tried in a real window before relying on it (below):**
- **`NativeArrows`** (`src/diff/register.ts`) registers `workbench.action.compareEditor.nextChange` and `previousChange` while a sequence's diff is active, and only then. `CommandsRegistry` puts the latest registration first and `CommandService` waits for extensions "in case an extension wants to overwrite it". To run VS Code's command it releases ours for the call, since from the extension host the id means ours. `args[0]` is the toolbar's resource, and a press from another group's diff passes straight through.
- **VS Code decides where the changes are** (`goToDiff`), and the only way to ask is to move the cursor, which at a boundary is a wrap to the top of the file. Doing that and putting it back showed: on 1.96 a frame of the wrong place, on both versions a view 4 or 5 lines off (`AtTop` pads for sticky scroll). So `predict` answers first with GitTree's own diff, normalised as VS Code compares (line endings, `ignoreTrimWhitespace`). A press predicted to have nothing ahead shows the hint and moves nothing. Before actually leaving a file, `needsAsking` has VS Code confirm, so a wrong prediction can show a hint early but can never skip a change. A diff too far apart for an exact answer (`exceedsLineDiffCap`) is `unknown`: VS Code is asked, and `restore` repeats its own reveal.
- **The hint is a real hover.** `editor.action.showHover` with `noAutoFocus` over a decoration carrying `hoverMessage`, so no `HoverProvider` is registered for anything. Like IntelliJ's it goes at the next key or scroll. It is repeated in the status bar. IntelliJ's strings, with the next file's path, its position and a link to the setting.
- **Landing** goes through VS Code too: from the last line "next" wraps onto the first change, from the first line "previous" onto the last. The file opens with `selection` at the predicted change, so it does not flash its top, and view state cannot interfere. `alreadyLanded` skips this where the cursor could not move, which every added or deleted file would otherwise wait out.
- **`nextStop`** skips files with no text change: R100/C100 by status (`identical`), others by reading them. VS Code greys out ↓ on them.
- **`override: true`** on `vscode.diff` forces the text diff, and `binaryPlaceholder` gives a binary revision a one-line document with its size and object id, so that two versions of one size still differ.
- **The pane:** `openFileDiff` carries `files` (`diffChain`, IntelliJ's `getListSelectionOrAll`: several rows selected, those; otherwise all, in tree order) so the host holds no copy to go stale, and `browsing` keeps focus in the list on preview-on-click. `evtDiffFile` moves the selection and scrolls to it without reopening.
- **Files:** `gittree.diff.prevFile` and `nextFile` on the toolbar at `navigation@12` and `@13`, right after VS Code's ↑ ↓, greyed at the ends. Their keys are not behind the IntelliJ keymap, because VS Code's tooltip shows a key whatever its `when` says.

**Checked in a real window, not by the suites:** a throwaway driver in the scratchpad started real VS Code 1.96.0 and 1.144.0 (the owner's version) with `--remote-debugging-port`, the real extension and a scratch repository, and clicked the real buttons through Playwright over CDP (`_electron.launch` cannot start release builds). The commit held modified, added, deleted, binary and pure-rename files, a change on the last line, and a big file. Walked forwards, backwards and by file arrows on both versions: every stop in pane order, one tab, pane following, the hint's text from the DOM, and the first visible line per animation frame showing no movement at a boundary. Also: CRLF against LF, a whitespace-only change skipped, focus on the left side, moving the cursor disarming, a double press, a kept tab, multi-select (`1/2`), preview-on-click keeping focus, a diff GitTree did not open still wrapping with no file arrows, the setting off, inline view with smooth scrolling, and the Settings link. The big file is the one case that still shows a frame of the wrap: changes more than about 2000 lines apart.

**Not done:** the Commit tool window, stashes, shelves and Compare Branches still open diffs one at a time. Images are a placeholder (D20).

Docs: FEATURES, D1, D20 (rewritten), keyboard.md, settings.md, parity-audit, CHANGELOG.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `test/unit/diff/navigationStep.test.ts` (rewritten), `test/unit/diff/diffManifest.test.ts` (new), `test/unit/core/binaryPlaceholder.test.ts` (new), `test/unit/webview/changedFilesChain.test.ts` (new), `test/unit/core/patch/lineDiff.test.ts`.
- **Integration:** `phase14.test.ts` T7: the walk through `runCommands`, so through VS Code's own registry; going back; disarming; one tab and a kept tab; added, deleted and renamed; a binary file; a plain diff; the setting.
- **Playwright:** `log.spec.ts`: order, a multi-selection, following without reopening, another commit, `browsing`.

POST-P14au ✅ 2026-11-02 (branch `new-way-4`): **The Commit window's changes walk the same way.** The owner, having tried POST-P14at on a commit's files: "start implementing with the Commit tool window very carefully". This list differs from a commit's in ways that mattered more than the wiring:

**IntelliJ, read first:** `ChangesViewDiffPreviewHandler.iterateAllChanges` walks `changesNodes` then `unversionedFiles`, so every changelist, with Unversioned Files last. Several rows selected, those (`getSelectionStrategy`), with the selection left alone (`updateSelection = false`). It reads the live tree on each press. `forceKeepCurrentFileWhileFocused` keeps a file up when its last change is reverted.

**What had to change:**
- **The sides** (`localDiffSides.ts`, vscode-free). `gittree.openDiff` compared the index with the working tree, while the preview pane and `git commit --only` use `HEAD`, so a file staged by hand showed no changes. Changelists now use `HEAD`. With staging the `Stage hunk` lenses need an index-based left side, so a row opens on what is not staged yet (`:0` to the file), or else on what is staged (`HEAD` to `:0`). VS Code hides CodeLens in diffs unless `diffEditor.codeLens` is on, so for most people those lenses never showed either way; with it on and changelists, they now do not.
- **The right side is a real file**, which can be gone or not be text by the time the walk reaches it. `DiffEntry.settle` asks VS Code (`openTextDocument`) each time an entry is about to be used. If VS Code refuses, `fs.stat` says which: missing gets `EMPTY_SIDE_REF`, otherwise `WORKTREE_STUB_REF` and a placeholder. Asking VS Code rather than sniffing matches its own rule for what it will open, and it is lazy, so a list of thousands costs nothing until walked. `DiffEntry.key` keeps an entry's identity when its sides change.
- **The list is live.** `DiffSessionRegistry.update` replaces a named sequence, keeping the file on screen where it was when it has left the list (`forceKeepCurrentFile...`). The webview sends `commit/diffChain` when its tree order changes, only after opening a walk over the whole list, since the order is the webview's to know. A list change clears a warning that may now name the wrong file. `onMove(index)` became `DiffEntry.onShown`, since an index means nothing once the list has moved.
- **The row menu** gets `gittree.commit.openChanges`, which asks the webview (`commit/openDiffAt`), since only it knows the order and the selection.
- **`diffWalk` in `treeModel.ts`** is IntelliJ's `getListSelectionOrAll` for every tree, with a selected folder standing for its files, as `VcsTreeModelData.selected` does. `selectionFollowing` leaves a multi-selection alone. The log's pane collapsed one before.

**Found by driving the real window:**
- **Language hovers buried the hint.** On a `file:` document a language server answers too, and a decoration's hover sorts after every provider, under a doc comment. The hint is now a `HoverProvider` registered only while it shows: the newest of equally matching providers goes first. It answers only at the cursor.
- **The hover covered the button.** VS Code draws a hover above its line if the window has room, counting what is above the editor. On lines near the top, every added file included, that is over the toolbar, and in a group under about 1200px wide over the ↓ just clicked, so the second click hit the hover. On the first four lines in view (`hoverWouldCoverToolbar`) the hint is a decoration at the end of the line, in the hover's colours. This also fixes the log's walk.
- **Crossing could show the old file's top.** The second press asked VS Code, which wraps, and read the next file only afterwards. Now the next stop is read first, and VS Code is not asked at all when the rest of the last change is on screen (`nothingHiddenAhead`), since a split of it could not be walked past unseen. Single-line last changes and any that fit on screen go straight across.
- **Typing did not disarm.** The second press from the same line crossed after an edit. A disarm now needs the cursor back at the very position, and the spot includes the document's version.
- **`modifiedEditorOf` could pick the wrong editor.** A working-tree file can also be open on its own in another group. Only the editors inside a diff have no `viewColumn`. Path casing is folded on Windows and macOS (`uriKey`), where VS Code keeps the casing it saw first.

**Checked in a real window, not by the suites**, with the driver from POST-P14at, on real VS Code 1.96.0 and 1.144.0: a scratch repository with a TypeScript project and a dirty tree holding modified, staged, added, renamed, deleted, binary and untracked files. Walked forwards, backwards and by file arrows, with the first visible line per animation frame. Also: a file appearing and one reverted mid-walk, the file on screen reverted, typing in the diff, the same file open in another group, several rows selected, a folder row, the row menu, the staging paradigm, and two repositories in one workspace. Screenshots checked by eye.

**Not done:** stashes, shelves and Compare Branches do not walk yet (POST-P14av took Compare Branches, which opened multi-diff, not one diff at a time as this said). A tab's `3/7` is set when it opens, so it can be behind a list that has changed until the next move: VS Code keeps a tab's title when the same diff is opened again.

Docs: FEATURES, D20, CHANGELOG.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `test/unit/commit/localDiffSides.test.ts` (new), `test/unit/webview/treeModel.test.ts`, `commitTree.test.ts`, `changedFilesChain.test.ts`, `test/unit/diff/navigationStep.test.ts`, `diffManifest.test.ts`, `test/unit/core/binaryPlaceholder.test.ts`.
- **Integration:** `phase14.test.ts` T7, "from the Commit window": every kind of local change on real files, a staged file against `HEAD`, the list changing under the walk.
- **Playwright:** `commit.spec.ts`: order, a selection, a folder, following without reopening, a selection left alone, the menu's path, and the host told about a changing list only for a walk over all of it.

POST-P14av ✅ 2026-11-02 (branch `new-way-4`): **Comparing branches walks the same way.** The owner: "Please continue with Compare Branches".

**What was there, by driving it:** `Compare with 'X'` opened the `gittree.compare` tree of two commit lists, whose rows ran `git show` into a new unsaved `Untitled` document each click, with no file list and no walk. Its title action and Show Diff with Working Tree opened VS Code's multi-diff. The log's range mode had been built for this at P4-T5, with a comment saying the command had been rerouted, but only a separate `compareInLog` went there, so FEATURES described something the menu never did.

**IntelliJ, read first:** `GitCompareBranchesUi` is two `VcsLogUi`s in an editor tab (`Compare: X and Y`). `GitShowDiffWithBranchPanel` and `GitBrancherImpl.showDiff` (`action.Git.Compare.Selected.Heads.title` = Show Files Diff) are a `SimpleAsyncChangesBrowser` in the `ChangesBrowserToolWindow`, which `showTab` empties on each request, with `DefaultChangesTreeDiffPreviewHandler` walking it by `getListSelectionOrAll`. The list is read once. `GitChangeUtils.getDiff` is tip against tip.

**What changed:**
- **`Compare with 'X'` is a log.** `gittree.branches.compareWithCurrent` runs `compareInLog`. The tab is titled `Compare: a and b`. `CompareStrip.tsx` above its commits is IntelliJ's explanation line for a table holding both sides: what ◀ ▶ mean, how many commits each side has alone (`git rev-list --left-right --count`, read on every reset), and Show Files Diff. The commit-list tree, `parseLeftRight` and `showCommit` are gone.
- **The `gittree.compare` pane lists files** (`compare.ts`, pure parts in `compareModel.ts`), a native tree: `canSelectMany`, `showCollapseAll`, `reveal` to follow the diff. Colours and letters take a `FileDecorationProvider` on a private `gittree-compare:` scheme, since a tree row's label has no colour of its own; that URI also gives the icon theme a file name. Words go in `TreeView.message`, since VS Code capitalizes a view's title, which turned `feature/compare` into `Feature/Compare`.
- **A comparison holds shas**, resolved when it is read. A branch moving under a walk leaves every diff agreeing with the list until Refresh.
- **A row's click**: VS Code runs a tree row's command with no word of how it was clicked, so a second click within 500ms on the same row goes into the diff (`goesIntoDiff`), and a lone click browses, as in its own Source Control view. It also runs it only when one row is selected, so several rows walk from Show Diff in the row's menu.
- **`treeModel.ts` moved to `src/shared/`**, so the host's tree and the webviews' agree on order and on `diffWalk`. `settleWorktreeSide` moved out of the Commit window for the pane's working-tree side.
- **Against the working tree, the right side is the real file**, the branch on the left, opposite to IntelliJ's default: VS Code only edits the right side (D36).
- **Palette:** `compareWithCurrent` and `diffWithWorkingTree` were offered there with no branch, and failed on `undefined...HEAD`. `compareInLog` did nothing. All hidden.

**Found while building:** lint's complaint about `string | 'worktree'` was a real bug, a branch called `worktree` would have been taken for the working tree. The right side of a request is now `undefined` for that. And an update's Show in Log opens a range tab too, of two hashes, which would have read `Compare:` with `0 commits only on …`: `comparedRefs` keeps the title and the line to named sides.

**Checked in a real window, not by the suites**, with a throwaway driver clicking the real UI, on real VS Code 1.96.0 and 1.144.0: a scratch repository with two diverged branches holding modified, added, deleted, renamed, renamed-untouched and binary files and a path with a space and an accent. Compare with from a branch's menu, running it twice, a commit's own walk; Show Files Diff, walked forwards, backwards and by file arrows with the selection following; a click, a double click, `Alt+F5` with focus in the list; several rows, a folder row; the multi-diff; Refresh after the branch moved; Close and reopening; a branch at the same commit; Show Diff with Working Tree, walked, with an edit that kept its tab, a binary file and the file gone from disk; two repositories in one workspace; the strip leaving with the filter.

**Observed limits:** the pane's Close sits left of VS Code's own Collapse All, which it always puts last. With a row selected, the diff's `1/2` stays until the next move when the same file reopens under a different walk: VS Code keeps a tab's title when the same diff is opened again. A diff opened before a new comparison keeps walking the old list until another is opened.

**Not done:** Swap Branches and Get from Branch (D36), stashes and shelves. Compare with Local and Compare Versions on a commit still open multi-diff, though in IntelliJ they are this same window.

Docs: FEATURES, D36, gap analysis, parity audit, CHANGELOG.

- **Integration:** `phase14.test.ts` T7, "from the Compare pane": the walk, a branch moving, several rows, the working tree, no differences. `phase02.test.ts` T5 and `phase14branchmenu.test.ts` rewritten for the log.

POST-P14aw ✅ 2026-11-03 (branch `new-way-4`): **Update Project fetches first, and one repository's trouble no longer stops the rest.** The owner asked whether GitTree can pull every repository's current branch. It can (`Ctrl+T`); driven on eight scratch repositories it showed three problems, and the owner agreed to fix all three "carefully":

1. **It stopped at the first failure.** One conflicting repository left three later ones, with nothing wrong with them, *not attempted*. IntelliJ's `GitUpdateProcess.updateImpl` joins every root's result and goes on past a conflict. Now every repository with something to pull is updated, and one summary (`updateReport.ts`, vscode-free) says what became of each, problems first: conflicts, failures (the fetch's apart from the update's), what came in, what was up to date, what was skipped or cancelled. A fetch that fails leaves only that repository out, where IntelliJ cancels the update.
2. **It said `git exited with code 1`.** A conflicted merge says it all on stdout. `describeGitFailure` (`errors.ts`) picks the line naming the problem: the first `fatal:`/`error:` line that is not git's generic last word, the files a refusal lists after its colon, `CONFLICT` from stdout; `hint:`/`warning:` and progress dropped, a remote's long URL or path dropped where the remote is already named. Conflicts get their own words from the status read after: *The merge stopped on conflicts in a.txt*.
3. **The question's counts were the last fetch's**, so it said `0 behind` for every repository and pulled commits anyway. It now fetches first, every repository at once through `runFetchAll`, then asks, `main ← origin/main · 3 to pull · 1 to push` in the Branches panel's words; with nothing to pull anywhere it does not ask (D30). Each button's command line drops the fetch, which has run (`buildUpdateQuestion(…, fetched)`).

**Also:** `fetchForUpdate` / `integrateFetched` / `resetFetched` split the old single function, so the project fetches once and merges after the question; `applyUpdateResult` keeps fetch-then-integrate for the push flow and a branch row's Update. A branch with nothing to pull is no longer merged. `MergeLifecycle.holdNotices` keeps the conflict lifecycle's own toast back while an update reports the conflict itself, so one conflict is one toast; the Conflicts view and the Files Merged with Conflicts dialog are unchanged. With several repositories open that toast names the repository, where it said only *merge produced 1 conflicted file(s)*. The one-repository result on conflicts is a warning with Show Conflicts; a failure gets Show Details.

**Checked in a real window**, with a throwaway driver on eight scratch repositories in real VS Code 1.144.0: clean, uncommitted changes, a conflict, one after the conflict, up to date, a feature branch, no upstream, a remote that is gone; Merge and Rebase; the second run with nothing to pull; one repository opened on its own, asked and then up to date. Screenshots checked by eye.

**Not changed:** a rejected push's *Update all repositories* still stops at the first failure: nothing is pushed again unless every update went through, so the rest would no longer serve the push.

Docs: FEATURES, D27, D30, CHANGELOG.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `test/unit/scm/updateReport.test.ts` (new), `test/unit/core/git/errors.test.ts` (`describeGitFailure`), `test/unit/scm/updateArgs.test.ts` (the question once fetched).
- **Integration:** `phase13.test.ts`: nothing to pull asks nothing; the question counts what the fetch found; Cancel now leaves the fetch done; Reset needs something to pull; a repository that cannot be fetched does not stop the others; a conflict does not stop the next and is the one toast; nothing to pull anywhere is said without a question.

POST-P14ax ✅ 2026-11-03 (branch `new-way-4`): **One branch in every repository: Checkout across them, and New Branch from a common one.** The owner: "a way to change all the current branches of all repositories to a given branch, or create a new branch from a given (common) branch … with the best UI/UX available".

**What was there, by driving it** on five scratch repositories (`release/2.0` local in one and only on `origin` in three, missing in one; uncommitted changes in two, one of them in the branch's way): a branch's row offered `Checkout in all N repositories` only where a *local* branch had the name, so the local `release/2.0` row checked out one repository; the remote row's common actions had no Checkout at all (`COMMON_ACTIONS` took `local` and `tag`). Smart Checkout asked per repository, and Cancel did not stop the fan-out. Nothing at the project level offered a branch to switch to. The New Branch form started where the gesture that opened it said, with no way to choose.

**What changed:**
- **`checkoutAcrossModel.ts`** (vscode-free): per repository, switch to the local branch, else `checkout -b x --track <remote>/x` from the remote asked for, the tracked one, `origin`, the first; already there, missing, or busy. The picker's choices (most widely held first, a branch checked out everywhere last) and the one summary, problems first.
- **`checkoutAcross.ts`**: each repository in turn, never stopping; one that git refuses for local changes in the way is set aside, and once every repository has had its turn **one question** covers them all (`askAboutLocalChanges`): Smart Checkout, Force Checkout, or leave them, `gittree.smartCheckout.behavior` answering it when set. Smart Checkout's steps are `setAsideAround` now, shared with the one-repository Smart Checkout, whose behaviour is unchanged. Cancel stops the next repository from starting. The conflict lifecycle's toast is held for the summary's Show Conflicts.
- **Wired:** the registry's `checkout` gets `runAll`, so every common-row Checkout (popup, panel, tree) is this run; a remote branch whose local branch is checked out is no longer offered `Checkout`. The panel sends the repositories in view with a menu or card request, and a branch's Checkout reaches all of them that have it, local or remote: **Checkout in 4 of 5 repositories** (`acrossOffer`, at least two changing). `Checkout Branch in All Repositories…` in the panel's `⋯` (several repositories in view), the palette (`gittree.multiRepo`), and the popup's project level.
- **New Branch:** a **From** field with suggestions (`startPoints`: what most repositories share first, a local branch before a remote one before a tag); a start point no repository has as a ref, a hash or `main~2`, is offered everywhere for the host to resolve. `createBranches` goes on past a failure; a checkout refused for local changes leaves the branch made there, and the same one question follows. Nothing went through anywhere: the form keeps the reason.

**Found by driving the real window:** after a Smart Checkout whose changes conflicted coming back, the next checkout across repositories hung: that repository's mutations are held by the conflict lifecycle until resolved, there is no merge in progress to skip it by, and every repository after it waited too. `repoBusyReason` (vscode-free) names both cases, and Update Project's scope, the checkout's plan and New Branch's checks skip such a repository, *it has conflicts to resolve*. Update Project had the same hang, unnoticed.

**Checked in a real window**, with a throwaway driver on real VS Code 1.144.0 and 1.96.0: the local row's Checkout in 4 of 5 with Smart Checkout restoring a change, then conflicting on the way back (kept in the stash, Show Conflicts); back to main from `⋯` and the picker; Cancel leaving one repository; the remote row with Force Checkout; the run skipping a repository with conflicts; New Branch from `main` in five, and from `origin/release/2.0` with one repository greyed out and one asked about. Screenshots checked by eye.

**Not done:** no Rollback after a partial checkout (D27); the popup's common rows still list only refs every root carries.

Docs: FEATURES, D27, gap analysis, CHANGELOG.

- **Integration:** `branchPanel.test.ts` T18–T22: the local row reaching a repository where only the remote has it; Cancel leaving one; Smart Checkout putting a change back; a repository with conflicts skipped, not waited on; New Branch made where checkout was refused. `projectPopup.test.ts`: the common Checkout's new summary.

POST-P14ay ✅ 2026-11-03 (branch `new-way-4`): **A branch's graph in a Log tab of its own, from the Branches panel.** The owner: "When I right click on a branch in the Branch panel, in the context menu, there should be a button that will create a new tab in the Git panel to show the git graph of that branch … with good UI/UX."

**What was there:** `Show in Git Log` (`gittree.branches.showInLog`, `{sort: 'topo', refs: [name]}` in a tab of its own, reused when one already shows exactly that) was the last item of a branch's one-repository menu. A branch several repositories have, the common case in a multi-repository workspace, offered it only inside each repository's submenu under *In one repository*. IntelliJ's branch menu (`Git.Branch`) has no such item: its branches tree sits in the log and filters it (`Update Branch Filter in Log` on a double-click).

**What changed:**
- **`panelActions.ts`:** **Show in New Log Tab** leads the group that looks at a ref, right after the checkout group and beside Compare with… and Show Diff with Working Tree, for a local or remote branch and a tag alike. A branch several repositories have gets a **Show in New Log Tab ›** submenu naming each repository, after the all-repositories operations. The log draws one repository at a time (D24), and moving a tab's root drops its ref list, so it is one tab per repository picked.
- **Tab titles** (`logTabsModel.stripTitles`, `LogTabDto.repo`): with several repositories the host names each tab's repository (`repoLabels`, the names `Roots` uses), and two tabs that would read the same while showing different repositories each add it, in IntelliJ's words for a root filter: `Log: on main in api` beside `Log: on main in web`. Otherwise a title stays as it was. The hover always names the repository.
- `LogViewProvider.showLogTab` / `closeLogTabs` (the reuse path and the strip's close, now callable), so a test can leave the Log as it found it; `logTabs` also reports each tab's repository.

**Found by driving the real window:** a click on any submenu item did nothing. A submenu is drawn beside its menu, not inside it, so the menu's outside-press handler closed everything on the press, and the click landed on nothing. That covered every repository's submenu and Tracked Branch in the Branches panel, Columns in the log's Presentation Settings and Delete preset in its saved filters. The keyboard worked, and the one Playwright test that clicked a submenu item (`Columns ▸ Hash`) was written, not run. `Menu.tsx` now counts a press in the open submenu as a press in the menu.

**Tried and taken out:** the same item on the log's own Branches pane. A right-click there moves the selection, as IntelliJ's does, and the selection filters the tab the pane is in. By the time the menu opened, the tab already showed the branch, so the item could only bring that tab forward.

**Checked in a real window**, with a throwaway driver on real VS Code 1.144.0 and 1.96.0, over five repositories: `feature/login` (two repositories) by its submenu, one tab per repository, both then named and the second request bringing its tab forward; `release/2.0` (one repository) straight from the menu; `origin/release/2.0` (four) by its submenu. In one repository: a branch, the checked-out branch's head row, a tag; the new tab's own Branch filter ticks the branch. `Presentation Settings ▸ Columns ▸ Hash` by mouse. Screenshots checked by eye.

Docs: FEATURES, D24, D34, CHANGELOG.

- **Integration:** `branchPanel.test.ts` T23 (where the item sits for one repository, for a remote branch, and the submenu for two) and T24 (it opens that repository's branch in a tab of its own, brings it forward when asked again, opens another for the other repository, names each tab's repository, and leaves the Log as it was); T3 counts the repositories' own submenus after the *In one repository* header.

POST-P14az ✅ 2026-11-03 (branch `new-way-4`): **The list a diff came from shows which file it is on.** The owner: "Back to the 'changed files iteration' feature that we implemented before, please carefully implement this: The file currently showing should be highlighted in the changed file list."

**What was there:** a walk over a whole list already moved the list's selection to the file it reached (`onShown`, IntelliJ's `selectChange`), and one over several selected rows left them selected (IntelliJ's `ChangesNavigatable` with `updateSelection` false). It did not read that way. `FileTree` lit the keyboard's row (`gt-focused`, VirtualList's roving row) in the active selection colour whether the tree had focus or not, and a walk moved only the selection, which an unfocused tree draws in the inactive colour. So the row double-clicked kept the brighter bar and the file on screen had the fainter one, and in VS Code 1.144's default theme the two are nearly the same grey. With several rows selected, nothing said which one was on screen.

**What changed:**
- **The mark (`DiffEntry.onScreen`, `DiffSessionRegistry.mark`):** the registry tells the list an entry came from when its diff comes on screen (the diff the arrows act on, the active tab of the active group) and when it leaves, the leaving entry first. `nextMark` (`src/diff/onScreen.ts`) keeps a diff of another file leaving from dropping the mark. The Log keeps one per tab (`log/diffOnScreen`, and `LogInitDto.diffOnScreen` for a view started again), the Commit window one (`commit/diffOnScreen`, and `CommitStateDto.diffOnScreen`), and the Compare pane one tied to its comparison: Refresh keeps it, another comparison drops it.
- **How it shows:** `FileTree.current` highlights the name (`aria-current`) by VS Code's own rule for a highlighted name in a tree view, `list.filterMatchBackground` with a dotted `list.filterMatchBorder`. The Compare pane's native tree gets the same from a `TreeItemLabel` highlight over the whole name. VS Code 1.144's default theme draws it teal, 1.96's Dark Modern orange.
- **The keyboard's row** is lit only while the tree has focus (`.gt-tree:focus-within`), as in VS Code's lists, so an unfocused tree shows only its selection. A walk's `reveal` also moves the keyboard's row (`VirtualList.setLead`, which takes no focus) and the Shift anchor.
- **Enter** on a row reached with the arrows selects it as it opens it (`selectionFollowing`, so a row inside a multi-selection keeps it). In `FileTree` the arrows move only the keyboard's row, which the old painting hid by lighting that row as if it were selected.

D20 has a row for the addition.

**Checked in a real window**, with a throwaway driver on real VS Code 1.144.0 and 1.96.0, reading each row's computed colours: the Log's Changed Files over a seven-file commit (double click, ← →, ↓ across a file, a click elsewhere, ↓ and Enter, Close All Editors); a walk over three selected files; the Commit window over eight local changes; the Compare pane over nine files. In each, one row is marked and it is the file in the tab; closing the diff clears it; nothing is lit before a list is used. Screenshots checked by eye.

Docs: FEATURES, D20, CHANGELOG.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `test/unit/diff/onScreen.test.ts`: the mark is the file that came on screen; it goes when that diff leaves; it stays when another file's diff leaves, the same path from another commit included; a list read again ends where it was.
- **Integration:** `phase14.test.ts` T7: the registry tells a list on and off (the leaving entry first), with another editor in front and back, and on close; a Log tab marks its file and no other tab's, follows a walk, clears; the Commit window marks, carries the mark in every state, follows a walk, clears; the Compare pane lights one row, keeps it through Refresh, drops it for another comparison, clears on close.
- **Playwright:** `log.spec.ts`: the pane marks the file on screen and only it, ignores another commit's, clears; a walk takes the keyboard's row and leaves no bar behind; an unused tree lights no row; Enter on a row reached with the arrows selects it; Enter inside a multi-selection keeps it. `commit.spec.ts`: a walk takes the keyboard's row; the tree marks the file on screen and not another repository's, and clears; a state naming it marks it; Enter on a row reached with the arrows selects it.

POST-P14ba ✅ 2026-11-03 (branch `new-way-4`): **File names in GitTree's webviews have their status colors again.** Found while checking POST-P14az in a real window: every name in the Log's Changed Files was the plain foreground.

**Why:** VS Code exposes a theme color to a webview as `'vscode-' + id.replace('.', '-')` (its webview theming, the same in 1.96.0 and 1.144.0), so only the first dot becomes a dash. GitTree's `gittree.color.added` arrives as `--vscode-gittree-color.added`. `status.ts` read `--vscode-gittree-color-added`, which nothing defines, and fell back to `--vscode-foreground`, for every status in every webview tree since P14-T5 moved the palette from `gitDecoration.*` (one dot) to `gittree.color.*` (two). The Compare pane and the editor take a `ThemeColor` and were never affected.

**What changed:** `themeColor(id, fallback)` reads the name VS Code gives, with the dot left escaped (`var(--vscode-gittree-color\.added, …)`), then the all-dashes name in case VS Code ever replaces every dot, then the fallback. `statusColor` goes through it, and so `dirStatusColor`.

**Checked in a real window** on VS Code 1.144.0 and 1.96.0: the Log's Changed Files over a seven-file commit reads modified `#E2C08D`, added `#81B88B`, renamed `#73C991`, deleted `#C74E39`, where every name had read `#BFBFBF` (1.144.0's foreground).

Docs: CHANGELOG. FEATURES already promised status-colored names.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `test/unit/webview/status.test.ts`: the reference names the variable as VS Code does, escaped, then the all-dashes one, then the fallback; an id with one dot is a plain name.
- **Playwright:** `commit.spec.ts`: a color set the way VS Code's webview sets it (`--vscode-gittree-color.untracked`) reaches the row's name and icon.

POST-P14bb ✅ 2026-11-04 (branch `new-way-4`): **The Branches panel is the Repositories panel, its branches under tabs.** The owner: "Should we rename it? I think we should choose a better name because that panel is not just for branches, it's also for repositories … Please suggest me the names first" and "I don't think there should be multiple overlapping sections (RECENT, LOCAL, and REMOTE are all about branches). I think we should merge them into one thing that can be switched by a dropdown (but if you find better designs, please suggest me)." Offered Repositories, Repositories & Branches, Workspace and Overview, and tabs, a dropdown or one merged list; the owner picked **Repositories** and **tabs**.

**The rename.** `gittree.view.branches.name` is *Repositories*, with the `$(repo)` icon; the id stays `gittree.branches`, so a moved view, a keybinding and `gittree.branches.focus` carry over. The opt-in tree that was *Repositories* is *Repositories (Tree)* (`$(list-tree)`, beside *Branches (Tree)*), and its row command *Show This Repository in Branches (Tree)*, the view it scopes. Every user-facing string that said the Branches panel or view (the walkthrough, the playground README, New Branch (Repositories Panel), three settings' descriptions, `docs/settings.md` regenerated) says Repositories; the log's own Branches pane keeps its name.

**The tabs** (`model.ts`: `BranchTab`, `shownTab`, a `tabs` row; `Rows.TabsRow`; `PanelList.stickyId`):
- Recent, Local, Remote and Tags are one row of tabs under the repositories' rows, in the look of GitTree's other strips (`gt-tab`), and the list below shows one kind. Each counts what it holds; the hover says it in words (`tabTitle`). Tags is not there while Show Tags is off.
- The panel starts on the tab last picked (`Persisted.tab`); at first, Recent while anything is recent, else Local. An empty tab says what would fill it.
- **Pinned.** The list draws a copy of the tabs over its top once it scrolls past them (`gtp-pinned`, `aria-hidden`, clear of the scrollbar), and a row moved to from the keyboard never comes to rest under it. A click on a tab, pinned or not, focuses the tabs row and starts the new list right under it.
- **Keyboard.** The tabs are one row of the list's roving focus: `←` and `→` switch, round the ends, as on a tab strip; `↓` goes into the list. The row tells a screen reader *Local tab, 2 of 4. Left and Right switch.* (`tabsLabel`).
- **Narrow sidebars.** `TabsRow` measures itself and picks the roomiest look that fits: names and counts, names, then less padding, down to VS Code's narrowest sidebar (170px).
- **Searching** looks in every kind whatever tab is picked: the tabs step aside and each kind's matches sit under a heading, as before; clearing the filter brings the tabs back as they were.

**Fixed on the way:** Collapse All rebuilt the toggle set from the folders open in view, so a folder opened by hand, open *because* it was toggled, stayed open. `collapsedAll` drops that toggle and adds one to a folder open by default; what is out of view (another tab) stays as it was.

**Checked in a real window**, with a throwaway driver on real VS Code 1.144.0 and 1.96.0: a repository shaped like the owner's screenshot (main 2 ahead and 3 behind with 6 changed files, 18 local branches in five folders, 14 remote, 6 tags, a gone upstream, five recent) and the five-repository workspace. Each tab, the head card above the tabs, the pinned strip and a click on it, `←` `→` round the ends and `↓`, a search and Escape, and the sidebar from 780px down to 170px with the last tab's right edge read each time. Screenshots checked by eye.

**Found, not changed:** with its hover buttons showing, the head row cuts its own branch name first (`n origin/main`, nothing at 170px), and a narrow card truncates its buttons (`U…`, `P…`). Both were so before.

Docs: FEATURES, D34 (tabs, the name), D29, CHANGELOG, keyboard, FAQ (rewritten: it still described the panel before D34), migration, parity audit, gap analysis, settings.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `branchPanelModel.test.ts`: the tabs after the status rows, counted; Recent first and Local when nothing is recent; empty tabs' hints; Local's folders, Remote's groups, Tags newest first, each its own; Tags out while hidden; the hover and the screen-reader label; the tabs under several repositories; the search stepping past them; Collapse All on folders opened by hand and by default, and leaving other tabs alone. `panelName.test.ts`: the view is Repositories under its old id, no two GitTree views share a title, no user-facing string calls it the Branches panel or view.
- **Playwright:** `branches.spec.ts`: every test that used a Local or Remote row picks its tab first; new: tabs one list at a time with their counts and the choice kept, starting on the tab last picked, the keyboard on the tabs, the pinned copy and a click on it, a narrow sidebar dropping counts before a tab, an empty tab's hint, the tabs under several repositories, Collapse All on a folder opened by hand, the Tags tab going with Show Tags, the search bringing back the tab picked.

POST-P14bc ✅ 2026-11-04 (branch `new-way-4`): **A narrow Repositories panel keeps the names.** Found while checking POST-P14bb and reported; the owner: "Yes, please fix them."

**What was wrong:**
- The current branch's row let only the branch's own name shrink. What it tracks (`origin/main`) could not shrink at all, being a plain span, so with the row's four hover buttons showing the name read `n`, and at VS Code's narrowest sidebar (170px) it was gone.
- A card's buttons were `flex: 0 1 auto` with ellipsized labels, so as soon as they did not quite fit they all shrank together: `U…`, `P…`, `New B…` in a 370px sidebar. And the card's first line kept `You · yesterday` whole while the commit's subject went to `ch…`.

**What changed:**
- `.gtp-head-upstream` (what the branch tracks, or *not published*) shrinks a thousand times faster than the name, with its hover saying it whole.
- The list frame is a size container (`gtp-list`). Below 19em (about 250px) a head or repository row's hover buttons fold into `⋯`, whose menu has Update Project, Push and Fetch too; a Fetch still turning stays. Below 14.5em (about 190px) the changed-file count and what the branch tracks go; the Commit view counts the files, and the card says what it tracks.
- `useFit` (`Rows.tsx`), the measuring the tabs strip already did, now also serves the card: its buttons never shrink, and the card keeps the roomiest of every button's words, the first one's alone, icons, then icons with less padding. Each button carries its label as `aria-label` and its title as hover.
- A card narrower than 20em leaves out the author and keeps the age; the hover has both.

**Checked in a real window**, with a throwaway driver on real VS Code 1.144.0 and 1.96.0, reading each width from 420px down to 170px with the pointer on the row: the name's own scroll and client widths, how much of what it tracks shows, which hover buttons show, the card's chosen fit and whether any button runs past the card. On the owner-shaped repository and on five repositories. Screenshots checked by eye.

Docs: FEATURES, CHANGELOG.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Playwright:** `branches.spec.ts`, *a narrow sidebar*: the current branch's name whole while what it tracks is cut; four hover buttons, then only `⋯`, then the changed-file count and what it tracks gone; a repository row's hover keeping only `⋯`; a card's buttons never cut off nor shrunk to a letter at 300, 220, 180 and 170px, an icon still named; a narrow card without its author, with its age.

POST-P14bd ✅ 2026-11-07 (branch `new-way-4`): **The Commit window's repository rows wear their repository's colour.** The owner, with a screenshot of two repositories under *Unversioned Files* drawn as green folders: "Please carefully update the source code so that they will show the correct color of each repository."

**What was wrong:** a repository row (`@g:<list>@r:<root>`, multi-root only) was a directory to the tree, so `icon` and `nameColor` gave it `dirStatusColor` of the files beneath it: green over untracked files, the modified colour over modified ones, and every repository in one list the same. IntelliJ's `RepositoryChangesBrowserNode` draws `CheckboxIcon.createAndScale(colorManager.getRootColor(root))`, a filled square in the log's root colour, and its name in plain attributes.

**What changed:**
- `CommitRepoDto.colorIdx` (`buildState`, from `rootColorIndices` over `core.repos`, as the log's `Roots` and the Repositories panel take theirs) and `CommitStateDto.palette` (`gittree.log.graphPalette`, which the view now also listens to).
- `CommitGroupRef.colorIdx` on a `repo` row (0 if the DTO lacks it). The App draws that row's icon as `laneColor(colorIdx, dark, palette)`, `dark` from `useVsCodeTheme`, and leaves its name uncoloured. Group rows, folders and files are as before.
- `FileTreeIcon` is a codicon or a `swatch`: a 9px filled square with 2px corners in a glyph's 16px slot, the log's `Roots` chip. The codicons have no filled square (`primitive-square` and `debug-stop` are outlines in 0.0.36; seen in the real window). Under forced colours the square stays, in the text's colour.

**Checked in a real window**, with a throwaway driver on real VS Code 1.144.0 and 1.96.0 and five repositories (two with modified files, two with only new ones): each repository row's square against its dot in the Repositories panel, by computed colour, in Dark Modern, then Light Modern switched live, then with the colour-blind palette. All matched; names plain; enlarged screenshots checked by eye.

Docs: FEATURES, CHANGELOG, parity audit.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `commitTree.test.ts`: a repository row carries its slot under Changes and under Unversioned Files, a merged changelist row none; a repository whose slot did not arrive takes 0; a single repository has no repository row.
- **Integration:** `multiRoot.test.ts`: every repository's `colorIdx` is `rootColorIndices`', the same as the Repositories panel gives it, repoD's differs from repoE's, and both views report the same palette.
- **Playwright:** `commit.spec.ts` (the mock's opt-in second repository): a repository row's swatch in its colour under both lists, its name plain, its files still coloured; the light theme's shade; the colour-blind palette; a lone repository without a swatch, its folders still folders.

POST-P14be ✅ 2026-11-07 (branch `new-way-4`): **The Commit window can put repositories first.** The owner asked for a way to switch between "categorized by change list" (the current tree) and "categorized by repositories", designed first. Design shown and approved with the owner's answers: the branch label on repository rows in both groupings (yes), a repository's checkbox includes its Unversioned Files (yes), remembered per workspace (yes). Two optional extras were put off for later, and the owner asked to be reminded of them: *Commit Only This Repository* on a repository row's menu, and repository rows that stay pinned at the top while a long one scrolls.

**The switch:** View Options (IntelliJ's eye, `ChangesView.ViewOptions`), before Expand all, only with several repositories (`SetRepositoryChangesGroupingAction` hides Repository with one root). Its *Group By* has two radio rows, **Changelist › Repository** and **Repository › Changelist** (`viewOptions.ts`); the eye gets IntelliJ's dot off the default, as the log's Graph Options does. `COMMIT_METHODS.setGroupBy` writes `gittree.commit.groupBy` (`changelist` | `repository`, scope window) for the workspace (`groupBy.ts`), as the Repositories panel's Show items and the paradigm switch do; the config listener pushes. Palette: `gittree.commit.groupByRepository` / `groupByChangelist`, shown with `gittree.multiRepo`, the one that changes something. Deviation **D37**.

**The tree** (`assembleCommitTree`, `groupByOf`): with several repositories and `repository`, one row per repository with changes (`@r:<root>`, `kind: 'repository'`), its non-empty lists beneath in IntelliJ's order (`@r:<root>@g:<key>`, `kind: 'repo'`: one repository's copy, the same scope and menu as a repository row under a list). Nothing changed anywhere: the one empty Changes row, as the other grouping. `CommitGroupRef.shows` says what a row draws (a list's folder, or a repository's square, plain name and branch), separate from what it acts on. Paths never collide between the groupings (`@g:` first or `@r:` first), so each keeps its own open folders in one persisted set.

**Rows:** `CommitRepoDto.head` (`repoHeadOf`: branch, commit, upstream, unborn) becomes a tinted label after the count (`headLabel`; FileTree's new `badge`, `gt-tree-badge`), in both groupings, hidden below 15em of tree (a container query) and outlined under forced colours; it is in the row's accessible name. The host now names and orders repositories as the Repositories panel does (`repoLabels`, `sortRootsByLabel`), the Commit button's targets too. A repository row's menu (`commitRepository`): New Changelist…, Shelve Changes… (`gittree.shelve.dialog` now takes `{ repoRoot }`), Rollback… (`gittree.rollback.dialog` likewise, `repoRootOf` in `ui/rowContext.ts`; shown with `repoHasTracked`).

**Keeping the place:** `apply` notes, before the rows are rebuilt, where the user is (FileTree's new handle, `viewState`: the keyboard's row, a row on screen and its offset, whether at the top). A layout effect maps the selection, the keyboard's row and that row to their twins (`twinRow`/`twinRows`: files, folders through `CommitTreeModel.folders`, a list copy to the same copy, a spanning list to its first repository's copy, a repository to its first list), opens what hides them, and FileTree's `restore` puts the row back at its offset before paint (`scrollToIndex(i, { offset })`), or keeps a list at its top there. Each grouping opens every row the first time it shows rows of its own (`seeded`), a changelist or repository row seen for the first time opens (`known`, migrated from older state without reopening what the user shut), and Collapse all shuts only the grouping on show (`collapsedAll`).

**Found on the way, fixed:** FileTree kept the keyboard's row by index, so folding a folder above it, or a file arriving above it, handed it to another row; the switch then carried that row. It follows its row by path now, and a row folded away hands the keyboard to its folder (`ancestorDirs` takes folders too).

**Checked in a real window**, with throwaway drivers on real VS Code 1.144.0 and 1.96.0: five repositories, one on a branch with no upstream, nested new files. Each grouping's rows level by level (names, counts, squares, labels and their hover); the menu's roles and ticks; the setting written to the workspace file; both palette commands; the right-click menus of a repository with only new files (no Rollback…) and one with tracked changes; a folded repository staying folded across two switches; Collapse all in one grouping leaving the other open; the keyboard's row staying put when a folder above it folds; and in a 60px tree scrolled to the bottom, the selected row keeping its 16px from the top through three switches while the list scrolled under it. A single-repository workspace with `groupBy: repository`: no View Options, no repository rows, neither palette command. Screenshots checked by eye.

Docs: FEATURES, CHANGELOG, D37, parity audit, gap analysis, settings (generated).

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `commitTree.test.ts`: the repository-first shape, order, depths and counts; empty lists and repositories left out; the empty Changes row with nothing changed; one repository always by changelist; odd setting values; what each row stands for and shows; `hasTracked`; the branch on repository rows in both groupings; the diff walk's order; a repository's tick taking its new files; folder identities; no path shared between groupings; `twinRow` for every kind of row and a row gone, `twinRows` de-duplicating; `collapsedAll`; `headLabel`'s four cases; `groupRowContext` for each kind. `viewOptions.test.ts`; `groupBy.test.ts` (`commitGroupByOf`, `repoHeadOf`); `rowContext.test.ts`; `treeModel.test.ts` (`ancestorDirs` of a folder).
- **Integration:** `multiRoot.test.ts`: names and order the same as the Repositories panel's, repoD's head; both palette commands writing the workspace setting and the state following, the setting put back after.
- **Playwright:** `commit.spec.ts`: View Options only with several repositories; its menu, ticks and starting row; picking Repository › Changelist posting `setGroupBy` and the tree's levels; picking the current one sending nothing; a repository row's square, plain name, label and menu payload, and a list row's; the label under a list; ticking a repository; a switch keeping the file selected and the keyboard on it, both ways; a list copy staying selected; a scrolled tree keeping the row's offset; open and shut kept per grouping; every row open on a first visit; Collapse all per grouping; a repository appearing later appearing open; the keyboard row following its row when a folder above folds, and its folder taking it when folded away. The two-repository colour specs now name rows with their branch.

POST-P14bf ✅ 2026-11-07 (branch `new-way-4`): **Add to VCS and Move to Another Changelist… for a repository's or a folder's new files; folder rows get a menu.** The owner, with two screenshots: a repository row under Unversioned Files offered a changelist's menu (Commit Changelist…, Set as Active, Rename, Delete, Shelve), and a folder row only VS Code's Cut, Copy and Paste. Asked for: moving an entire repository's unversioned files to the default list or another, and IntelliJ's options on folders, at least Add to VCS.

**IntelliJ, read first:** the changes tree has one menu for every node (`ChangesViewPopupMenu`): Commit, Rollback, Move to Another Changelist, Show Diff, Jump to Source, Delete, Add to VCS (`ScheduleForAdditionAction`, shown only when the selection holds unversioned files), the changelist actions, Shelve, Create Patch…; each shows itself by what is selected, and a folder or Unversioned Files node selects every file beneath it. `MoveChangesToAnotherListAction` adds unversioned files to VCS in the list it moves them to (`addUnversionedFilesToVcsInBackground(project, targetList, …)`), and its chooser leaves out the one list every change is already in.

**What changed:**
- `groupRowContext` says `listIsUnversioned`; the list actions (`commitList`, `setActive`, `rename`, `delete`, `shelf.shelve`) are gated `!listIsUnversioned` in both sections, and `addToVcs` and `changelist.moveTo` are offered there instead. P14-T2a had given Unversioned Files a list's whole menu; Shelve on it shelved the whole repository (`listOf` found no list).
- `folderRowContext` (webview section `commitFolder`): the folder's files in tree order (`relPath` first, for Open Changes), the folder itself (`folder`), `fileUnversioned`, `folderHasUnversioned`; inside a multi-selection, every selected file of the clicked repository, as a file row. Menu: Add to VCS, Open Changes, Move to Another Changelist…, Roll Back…, Add to .gitignore. The repository row (`commitRepository`) adds Add to VCS (`repoHasUnversioned`) and Move.
- Host (`register.ts`): `rowFiles` reads what any row stands for (files and folders by `relPaths`; list rows by `targets`, read now, Unversioned Files being `unversioned()`; a repository row by `repoRoot`). `addToVcs` adds each repository's new files (`add -N`, sliced by `chunked`), and moves only the loose ones to the default list: a file already moved into a list stays there (it used to be moved to the default). `moveTo` merges the lists across repositories (`moveChoices`: by default-ness or name, default first, the one source list left out), makes a missing list under the same name, moves, and adds the new files to VCS (`GITTREE_TEST_CHANGELIST_PICK` answers its picker in tests). Its title is IntelliJ's, *Move to Another Changelist…*.
- Add to .gitignore on a folder (`folderOf`): when every file is new and `git ls-files -- <folder>` is empty, one rule, `/folder/` (`ignorePattern(path, true)`, in the nearest ignore file above it); else a line per file as before.
- `gittree.shelve.dialog` and `gittree.rollback.dialog` already took a repository row's `{ repoRoot }` (POST-P14be).
- A folder seen for the first time opens, as new changelist and repository rows already did (`known` now holds every row with rows beneath it), so files moved to a new list are in sight; one the user shut stays shut.

**Checked in a real window** with a throwaway driver on real VS Code 1.144.0 and 1.96.0, five repositories: the right-click menus of the merged Unversioned Files row, a repository under it, a changelist's repository row and a folder (each item read off VS Code's menu), a folder row's payload; Add to .gitignore on `tools/scripts` writing `/scripts/` and the row leaving; Add to VCS on `api`'s Unversioned Files row (`git status`: ` A endpoint.ts`, under Changes); Move to Another Changelist… on `web`'s, through the picker to a new list "Web new" (both files ` A`, the list's rows open); the repository rows' menus with repositories first. The scratch repositories put back after each run.

Docs: FEATURES, CHANGELOG, parity audit, gap analysis.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:** `moveChoices.test.ts` (keys, order and merging, the source list left out, the default kept when alone, `listIdFor`, `chunked`); `commitWindowMenus.test.ts` (each kind of row's menu evaluated from package.json: Unversioned Files, a changelist, a folder of new, tracked and mixed files, a repository row with and without new files, the staging paradigm; the title); `gitignoreRules.test.ts` (a folder's trailing `/`, escaped, in the nearest ignore file above it, mixed with files); `rowContext.test.ts` (`folderOf`, refusing `..`); `commitTree.test.ts` (`listIsUnversioned` in all four rows it is drawn as, `repoHasUnversioned`, `folderRowContext`: the payload, a collapsed chain, mixed files, a selection kept to the clicked repository, the same under both groupings, nothing for other rows).

Everything made is taken out of the index and off the disk, and the lists removed.
