POST-P14bg ✅ 2026-11-07 (branch `new-way-4`): **Every commit check starts unticked.** The owner: the Commit window's options to check source code before committing start ticked, which matches IntelliJ, but they want them unticked. IntelliJ's `VcsConfiguration` ticks Analyze code (`CHECK_CODE_SMELLS_BEFORE_PROJECT_COMMIT`, off only in PyCharm, RubyMine and CLion) and Check TODO (`CHECK_NEW_TODO = true`); GitTree had followed it. Now `gittree.commit.checks.analyze` and `gittree.commit.checks.todo` default to `false`, as `reformat`, `optimizeImports` and `cleanup` already did. The code's fallbacks come from one pure constant, `CHECK_DEFAULTS` (`src/commit/checks/checkDefaults.ts`), which `readOptions` uses and a unit test holds package.json to. *Run advanced checks after a commit is done* (`deferAdvanced`) stays `true`, as IntelliJ's `NON_MODAL_COMMIT_POSTPONE_SLOW_CHECKS`: it says when a ticked check runs and is no check itself. A value the user set keeps winning. Deviation **D38**.

Tests (written, not run):
- **Unit:** `checkDefaults.test.ts`: `CHECK_DEFAULTS` all false; package.json's five check defaults false and equal to it; every boolean `gittree.commit.checks.*` but `deferAdvanced` is a check `CHECK_DEFAULTS` knows, so a new check can't arrive ticked unnoticed; `deferAdvanced` still `true`.
- **Integration:** phase03's analyze-gate test ticks `analyze` itself (and clears it after); phase11's deferred-checks test asserts the untouched options read Analyze and TODO unticked, then ticks `todo` itself (and clears it after).
- **Playwright:** the mock host's options match the new defaults; new tests: every check box unticked with severity, pattern and profile greyed and the defer box ticked; ticking Check TODO posts it and frees the pattern. The severity test now ticks Analyze before picking a severity.

Docs: CHANGELOG (Changed), FEATURES, D38, gap analysis, settings (generated).

POST-P14bh ✅ 2026-11-07 (branch `new-way-4`): **Git tool window: Log tabs that hold still, a Branch selection that survives `Paths ▾`, radio presets, compact filter chips, a year-first Date column, and a Graph toggle.** The owner, with three screenshots, reported four bugs and asked for three changes.

**1. Switching Log tabs made the whole window jump.** Measured in real VS Code 1.144.0 (a driver recording every frame's sizes across four switches): a tab behind another was `position: absolute; inset: 0` over the whole root, strip included, so it was laid out 29 px taller than a tab in front. Brought forward, its commit list (sized from its own height while hidden) was 30 px taller than its pane. `.gt-log-rows` lacked `min-height: 0`, so the list held its box at its own height: it grew with a pane but never shrank. The pane body scrolled around it (a second scrollbar, the rows narrower), and the canvas redrew a frame late, stretched. 339 jumpy frames over four switches; the same driver after the fix: 0. Fix: one `.gt-log-stack` under the strip, every panel `absolute; inset: 0` in it (front and behind the same box), `min-height: 0; overflow: hidden` on `.gt-log-rows`, and `GraphCanvas` drawing in `useLayoutEffect` (the bitmap resized and redrawn before paint). The short-panel case (list stuck at 400 px in a 150 px pane, the owner's screenshot's two scrollbars) is gone too.

**2. `Paths ▾ · Roots` reset `Branch ▾` to HEAD.** `rootAgnostic` dropped `refs` and `range` on purpose: carried into a repository without the branch, `git log` fails on `unknown revision`. Now it keeps them, as IntelliJ's filters stay when its roots change. `LogService.getPage` checks the named revisions first, with one `git cat-file --batch-check=%(objecttype)` over `<name>^{commit}` lines on stdin (memoized per names, HEAD and refs generation). It leaves out the missing (`withoutMissingRefs`), and when nothing is left it returns an empty, finished page without running `git log`. Ranges typed into `Branch or tag` (`a..b`, `a^!`) are git's to read, unchecked (`isRevisionRange`). `LogPageDto.missingRefs` carries the names; the view shows IntelliJ's *No commits matching filters* with *Not in this repository: …* and a **Reset filters** link (`vcs.log.no.commits.matching.status`), and `Branch ▾` lists a ticked name the repository lacks first, marked *not in this repository*, so it can be unticked. **Found on the way:** after a switch the tab never re-asked for branches (the effect ran once per tab), so `Branch ▾` and the Branches pane kept the previous repository's until a ref changed; now keyed on `init.repoRoot`.

**3. `Branch ▾`'s presets.** `HEAD (Current)` and `All branches` are radio buttons (`PresetRow`, grouped by `useId()` since every tab's bar is in one page) in a `role="radiogroup"` block above the list, ruled off, outside its scrolling; the list is `role="group"` "Branches and tags".

**4. "Merge commits still shown under No Merges": not so.** Checked against git in the real window, scrolling every row's `data-vscode-context` sha out of the list: HEAD by date 47 rows = `git log --no-merges` 47, 0 of git's 6 merges listed; All branches by date and topologically 77 rows, 0 of 7 merges. In the owner's screenshot the list has no merge either (the sandbox's `Merge branch 'release/1.0'` and the three-parent `Merge branches 'docs/typos-a' and 'docs/typos-b'` sit exactly where rows are missing). The lines that look like merging are the merged branches' own commits (`docs: tighten the guide`, `docs: fix a line…`) running down into the commit they branched from; the host passes the lanes through each hidden merge to its first parent (`GraphLayout.hide`), and the merged branch shows as a tip. No code change.

**5. Compact filter chips** (owner's pick of three designs: short values). `filterChips.ts` (pure): IntelliJ's own value rules — `displayableText` (one value, or `|`-joined, cut at 20 with `…`), a user by name with `me` for your `user.email`, `Since …`/`Until …` in the Date column's day format, a path by its last part; the whole value in the chip's `title`. CSS: 18 px line, 4 px radius, the `×` a codicon inside. `gittree.log.filterChips: full` keeps the old chip (D39). Note: IntelliJ already names users without e-mails; the old full chips were the departure.

**6. Date column** `YYYY/MM/DD HH:mm` (`createLogDateFormat`, `formatToParts` in `en-US` with `hourCycle: 'h23'`); `gittree.log.dateFormat: locale` is IntelliJ's (D40). The Push dialog's tooltip keeps IntelliJ's.

**7. Graph toggle:** `gittree.log.showGraph` (on), *Presentation Settings ▸ Graph* first under Show; off, no canvas and the message at the row's edge.

Plumbing: `LogPresentationDto` gains `showGraph`, `dateFormat`, `filterChips`; `PRESENTATION_CHOICES` + `readPresentation` (a wrong value keeps the default) + `presentationSettingIds()` for the config listener; the webview's `asPresentation` moved to `presentationMenu.ts`. `currentSession` exposes `eof` and `missingRefs`.

Verified in real VS Code 1.144.0 over two repositories (`local`, `local - Copy` with `only-in-copy` and without `feature/priority`): chips 21 px compact / 25 px full, values and titles as above; dates `2026/11/04 22:49` ↔ `11/4/2026 10:49 PM` live on the setting; Branch ▾ radios outside the list with a solid rule; `feature/priority` kept across the switch, empty log naming it, list marking it, `only-in-copy` listed; with `main` added, 53 rows; back in `local`, 56; Graph off drops the canvas and the 44 px padding, writes `gittree.log.showGraph: false`.

Tests (written, not run): unit — `logFilter.test.ts` (ranges, named revisions, batch-check answers incl. CRLF and short answers, `withoutMissingRefs`), `logService.test.ts` (real git: missing branch left out, none left → empty finished page, missing range end, hashes/`HEAD~1`/`side..main` unchecked), `presentation.test.ts` (Graph scope/id, choices declared in the manifest, `readPresentation`, patch never passes choices, defaults), `presentationMenu.test.ts` (Graph row and toggle, `asPresentation`), `dateFormat.test.ts` (format, midnight `00`, locale-proof, zones, `formatFilterDay`), new `filterChips.test.ts`; Playwright — presets by role, eye menu with Graph first, default date regex, compact chips (user, `me`, date, 20-character cut), full chips, locale date without re-paging, Graph toggle, radio presets, Roots keeping Branch (mock now has per-tab roots with their own refs and reports `missingRefs`), tab boxes equal with no overflow, list shrinking with a short viewport; integration — `logRoots.test.ts` (selection kept across the switch; a branch only repoA has: empty log with `missingRefs`, then rows with repoB's branch beside it, and back).

Docs: CHANGELOG, FEATURES, D39, D40, gap analysis, parity audit, settings (generated).

POST-P14bi ✅ 2026-11-07 (branch `new-way-4`): **A new commit shows in the log.** The owner had sometimes seen a new commit not appear in the graph and asked whether that, or anything like it, still happens. Four causes found; each reproduced in real VS Code 1.144.0 before the fix (drivers `commitrefresh.cjs` and `commitrace.cjs`, an 80- and a 2,500-commit repository built on C:) and gone after it.

**1. A log on top hid the new commit.** The preserving reset (452618b, ad47123) anchored on the row under the top edge and put it back at the top (`scrollToIndex(i, 'top')`). A commit made on top of that row is row 0, so the list scrolled by exactly one row and the commit sat above the window: the list looked unchanged, apart from the branch label leaving the old first row. Measured: commit from a terminal, from GitTree's Commit window, with and without a selection, every time `scrollTop` 0 → 24 and row 0 off screen. Frame by frame (a `requestAnimationFrame` sampler in the webview): the new commit was painted for one frame, then scrolled away; scrolled down, one frame showed the rows shifted by the new commit, and the anchor lost its sub-row offset (730.4 → 744). Fix: IntelliJ's `SelectionSnapshot` (`VcsLogGraphTable.updateDataPack`): on top (`ScrollingUtil.getVisibleRows().first == 0`) it scrolls back to row 0; otherwise the first selected row between the leading and trailing rows, or else the leading row, keeps its top gap to the pixel. `scrollAnchor.ts` (pure) takes it when page 0 arrives, not when the reset does, so a scroll or click in between counts; the restore is a layout effect (the rAF frame was the shifted one) and sets the graph's viewport in the same frame (`listWindow.ts`, shared with `VirtualList`). After: `scrollTop` 0 and the commit on screen; scrolled, 730.4 → 754.4, and no frame with another row under the top edge.

**2. A busy workspace held off every refresh.** `StatusRefresher.poke` restarted its 200 ms debounce on each event with no cap. With a file written every 100 ms (an ignored `noise.log`), a commit from a terminal did not reach the model or the log within 8 s, and arrived 0.9 s after the writing stopped. Fix: `maxWaitMs` (1000) from the burst's first event. After: listed in 1.1 s with the writing still going.

**3. An outside commit right after a GitTree operation was dropped.** The queue calls an event self-inflicted from a mutation's start to a second after its end, and the refresher dropped those, trusting the post-op refresh. That refresh had usually read by then. Trace of a GitTree commit and a terminal commit 800 ms later: status read at +410 ms, the terminal commit's four `.git` events at +962 ms all `suppressed self-inflicted`, nothing after; the commit was missing from the log 4 s later. Fix: such an event is dropped only while a refresh that reads after it is coming (one waiting in the debounce, or a mutation still running); otherwise it pokes. Our own late events still fold into the waiting post-op refresh. After: listed at 650, 800 and 900 ms.

**4. A page read twice garbled the graph.** `rehydrate()` makes the webview re-post every unanswered request; a page read waits behind any running mutation. With **Refresh** clicked during an 8 s commit (a pre-commit hook that sleeps and refuses) and the panel hidden and shown, the trace showed two `git log` page reads; both were laid out (`GraphLayout` carried its lanes from the first copy into the second), the host's rows doubled, and the graph grew an extra lane (20 → 32 px). Also latent: a replay of a page already sent set the webview's next page to 1, and a page past the next one (meant for a replaced session) was read. Fix: `PageReads` per session (vscode-free): a page being read is joined, one already sent is replayed with `LogPageDto.next` (the webview pages on from it), one past the next is dropped as stale. After: one read, 20 px.

Not changed: the log shows one repository at a time (D24), so a commit in another root of the workspace does not show until `Paths ▾ · Roots` picks it.

Verified on the final build in VS Code 1.144.0 (all seven scenarios) and 1.96.0 (the four on-top/scrolled/noise ones and the frame sampling).

Tests (written, not run): unit — new `scrollAnchor.test.ts` (on top, partly scrolled, selection on and off screen, bottom edge, past the last row, empty), `listWindow.test.ts`, `pageReads.test.ts` (join, replay, stale, read, a failed read joined and retried); `statusRefresher.test.ts` (a stream of events every 20 ms with a commit made in it; an outside commit inside the window after the post-op read; our own late events still one refresh). Playwright — the mock host can put new commits on top (`__mockNewCommits`): on top the new commit is row 0 at `scrollTop` 0; on top with a selection, the selection moves down with its row; scrolled, the top row keeps its offset to the pixel; a selected row on screen keeps its place; after a replay with `next: 5` the list asks for page 5, not 1. Integration — phase04: a page asked for twice while read is laid out once (row count equals `rev-list --count`); a commit from outside 400 ms after a GitTree operation reaches the model and the log (skips if the machine cannot land it inside the window).

Docs: CHANGELOG (Fixed), FEATURES, gap analysis.

POST-P14bj ✅ 2026-11-09 (branch `new-way-6`): **Repository Dots: a switch, off by default, that no longer moves on hover.** The owner, with a screenshot of the Repositories panel over 20 repositories: the dots (one per repository on each branch row) are useful, but on hover they jumped left, and they need not show all the time. Three designs for the hover shown (buttons before the status columns; buttons floating over the row's end; a fixed lane for the buttons) and three for the switch. The owner chose the first (VS Code's own layout: in Source Control a row's actions sit before its decoration letter), a one-click toolbar toggle mirrored in the `⋯` menu, the dots off by default, and the name *Repository Dots*.

**Why they moved.** A branch row ran `name · counts · dots · age`, all pushed right; on hover or selection the 1–4 buttons (22 px each) replaced the age (26 px), so everything left of them shifted by about 40 px (60 on the current branch, a few pixels right on a tag).

**Hover.** `BranchRow` now renders `.gtp-row-buttons` after the spacer and before the counts, dots and age, and the CSS rule that hid `.gtp-age` while buttons showed is gone. The buttons take the spacer's room, then the name's (it ends in `…`); counts, dots and age never move, hovered or selected, and the age stays visible. Checked by screenshot at 620 and 300 px: the dots' and age's x identical before and during hover.

**Switch.** New setting `gittree.branches.showRepositoryDots` (default `false`) in `BranchPanelStateDto` and `BranchPanelSettingKey`. Rows draw dots only when `model.markers` (several repositories in view) and the setting are both true; the card keeps its per-repository lines on `model.markers` alone, so hiding them loses nothing. Toggled from:
- a toolbar button between Fetch and New Branch, only with several repositories in view: a 2×2 glyph of filled and ringed dots (a row of three read as the `⋯` beside it in a screenshot), `aria-pressed`, *Show/Hide Repository Dots* on hover;
- `⋯` › *Show* › **Repository Dots**, between Incoming / Outgoing Counts and Group by Folder, also only with several repositories;
- command `gittree.branches.toggleRepositoryDots`, *GitTree: Toggle Repository Dots*, in the palette when `gittree.multiRepo`.

Docs: FEATURES (Repositories panel), CHANGELOG (Changed), settings.md regenerated.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Playwright** (`branches.spec.ts`; the mock starts with the dots off, `__mockDots` opts in):
  - off by default, the toolbar button shows and hides them and tells the host;
  - the `⋯` row is the same switch;
  - the hover buttons sit before the counts, dots and age, whose positions do not change on hover or selection;
  - no button or menu row with one repository in view, or narrowed to one;
  - with the dots off, a card still lists the repositories.
- The existing per-repository marker test opts in to the dots.
- **Integration** `branchPanel.test.ts` T25: the setting ships off, and the command flips what `buildState` reports, then puts the setting back.
- The unit model helper gains the new state field.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; webviews and the extension bundle built. No suite run.

POST-P14bk ✅ 2026-11-09 (branch `new-way-6`): **A branch's card opens when asked, and stays short with many repositories.** The owner, with a screenshot of a card over 20 repositories (one line each, all twenty saying *up to date · 2 ahead, 4 behind current*, about 550 px pushing the list down), named two problems: a click should be able to select a branch without the card, and the card takes too much room with many repositories. Five ways to open the details and five ways to shorten them were offered, ranked; the owner chose: click selects, the card opens on demand inline (1); repositories grouped by what they say (a); the ones without the branch named too (b); a double-click opens the details. The cap of option (c) — *+N more states* when nearly every repository differs — was not chosen and is not built.

**Gestures** (`App.tsx`). `activate` tells a pointer's click (`detail`) from a key (no `detail`) and the chevron (`twisty`, from the click's target): a click on a branch or the head row only selects it (the list focuses it); the chevron toggles the card on its first click (the second click of a double-click on it is ignored, so it does not shut what it opened); a double-click (`detail === 2`) on the row toggles it; Enter, Space and → toggle as before; Escape now shuts an open card as ← does. The chevron stays a span, as VS Code's twistie is not a focusable control. The row's hover text says *Double-click or Enter for details*.

**Lines** (`model.ts`, pure). `cardLines(entry, ctx)` builds the card line by line, and `buildPanel` sizes the row from them (`cardHeight(lines)`) and hands them to the row (`cardLines`), so the drawing and the windowed list's height cannot disagree. The model now takes the card's facts (`PanelUiState.detailsOf`) and its opened groups (`openGroups`), because how repositories group depends on each one's relation to its checked-out branch. `cardGroups` keys each repository by *checked out*, *tracks elsewhere*, `trackingShort` and `relationShort`, largest group first, then a `missing` group of the repositories in view without the branch. `groupLabel` names up to three (`NAMED_REPOS`), counts more (`13 repositories`, `All 20` when one group holds every repository in view and none lacks it), and writes `not in shared` / `not in 4 repositories`. A counted group is expandable: opened, it lists its repositories a line each (`member`). With one repository in view the card is as before. With several and the branch in one, the full tracking and relation lines stay, the tracking line naming the repository, then the missing line. Facts not yet arrived group by what is known; their arrival can split a group (one growth, once).

**Drawing** (`Rows.tsx`). `BranchCard` draws the model's lines: a group line has its repositories' dots (`flex-shrink` 1000, so they give way first, down to one dot; the line's hover names every repository), its label (a button with a chevron when expandable, `aria-expanded`), and what the group shares, with *Stop tracking* on a group that tracks elsewhere (one repository: the old `root` form; several: the target narrowed to them). The opened groups belong to the card they were opened in (`groupsOpen.card`), so a card opened any other way, the filter's Enter included, starts with them shut.

Checked by screenshot on the mock with 20 repositories, at 620 and 300 px: a click selects and opens nothing; a double-click opens *17 repositories › · up to date · 3 ahead, 1 behind current*, *extra-4, extra-9 · ↓2 · …*, *not in shared* (4 lines where there were 20); the count lists its 17; at 300 px the labels stay whole and the dots give way; the chevron and Escape shut the card.

Docs: FEATURES (Repositories panel), CHANGELOG (Changed), keyboard.md.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit** (`branchPanelModel.test.ts`):
  - twenty repositories in step are one *All 20* line, not expandable;
  - the ones that differ get their own line after the largest group;
  - named versus counted groups, and an opened group's members listed in place;
  - the missing line, named and counted;
  - one repository of several keeps its full lines, then the missing line;
  - with one repository in view, nothing is said of others;
  - facts arriving split a group;
  - the checked-out repositories are a group of their own;
  - every `groupLabel` form;
  - a detached HEAD's two lines;
  - `buildPanel` sizes an open card by its facts and opened groups, and a row whose card is shut carries no lines;
  - the existing size test moved to the new API.
- **Playwright** (`branches.spec.ts`):
  - a click selects and asks for nothing, a double-click opens the card and closes it again;
  - the chevron opens and closes, and Escape closes;
  - twenty repositories make two lines, the count lists 19 and closes again, and a reopened card starts with its groups closed;
  - a branch in one of three repositories names it and the two without it;
  - the head row opens on a double-click, not a click;
  - the keyboard test gains Enter and Escape;
  - every test that opened a card with a click now double-clicks;
  - the tracking-elsewhere test checks its two lines.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; webviews and the extension bundle built. No suite run.

POST-P14bl ✅ 2026-11-10 (branch `new-way-6`): **A repository's Show in New Log Tab; the bottom panel is titled GitTree.** The owner, with a screenshot of a repository's right-click menu in the Repositories panel: an item that opens a new tab in the Log, filtered to that repository with the branch filter at HEAD. Then: rename the panel from "Git" to "GitTree".

**The item.** New command `gittree.repositories.showInLog` (`REPOSITORY_LOG_TAB_COMMAND` in the protocol), *Show in New Log Tab* as the branch menu's item is called, hidden from the palette. It calls `logView.openTab({ ...DEFAULT_FILTER }, root, { newTab: true })`. `DEFAULT_FILTER` is the Log's own default (`refs: 'HEAD'`, date order). `newTab` makes `findTab` skip the main tab (`skipMain`): the owner asked for a new tab, and the main tab with that repository at HEAD would otherwise be what came forward. An extra tab already showing it still does, as the branch item's does. Its title is `Log`, given the repository's name by `stripTitles` whenever another tab would read the same.

**Both menus.** `REPOSITORY_MENU` gains a first group, `view`, with the entry. The sidebar's Repositories section gets the same command first (`0_view@1`), so the unit test that holds the two menus to each other still passes. The panel's webview puts it in one group with its own *Show Only This Repository*: `showMenu`'s new `joinLead` drops the rule between them. The one repository's head menu leaves it out (`withoutRepositoryLogTab`) when the current branch's menu, with its own *Show in New Log Tab*, stands above. When HEAD is detached there is no branch menu, and it stays.

**The panel.** `gittree.panel.title`: `Git` → `GitTree`, the `gittree-panel` container's title in VS Code's panel bar. The ids are unchanged, so layouts and keybindings carry over. Prose that names the panel follows (FEATURES, README, CHANGELOG); "Git tool window", IntelliJ's name for what it stands for, stays where it means that.

Testing (written, **not run**, by the owner's standing instruction):
- **Unit:**
  - `repositoryMenu.test.ts` passes unchanged (both menus gained the entry), plus an assertion that the menu now opens with *Show in New Log Tab*;
  - `logTabs.test.ts`: `findTab` with `skipMain` passes over the main tab and still finds an extra one.
- **Integration** `branchPanel.test.ts`:
  - T26: the repository menu leads with the item and its run;
  - T27: run, it opens a new tab showing that repository at HEAD even while the main tab shows it, and run again it brings the same tab back.
- **Playwright** `branches.spec.ts`:
  - the repository menu puts *Show Only This Repository* and *Show in New Log Tab* together and runs the command with the repository's root;
  - the single-repository head menu has *Show in New Log Tab* once.

Gates: typecheck (both tsconfigs) + lint clean; webviews and the extension bundle built. No suite run.
